Design System

EngenXT · living documentation · no build

Building the tree from the page…

Nothing matches that.

Introduction

The design system behind EngenXT, a fleet-fuelling app used by drivers standing at a pump. This page is generated from the same CSS the product loads. Nothing on it is a copy.

Reading the stylesheet…

What it is for

Drivers use this app one-handed, outdoors, in daylight, with someone waiting behind them. That decides most of the system: high contrast, large targets, plain words, motion only where it costs nobody time.

Scope

Two pages share this system. index.html is sign-up plus the home screen. engenxt-onboarding.html is where a driver lands afterwards. Only index.html has moved to linked CSS so far, so the components documented here are the ones that page renders.

No build step, a requirement rather than a shortcut

No package manager, no bundler, no build step. Both pages open by double-clicking them. The deployed site is those same files served statically. A reviewer with no toolchain, on a plane, can open the product. That property is load-bearing, so the system lives in plain <link> tags, CSS custom properties and cascade layers. Never in a compiler.

Dependency direction

Everything flows one way. A layer may use what is above it and must not reach down.

LayerFileHoldsMay use
tokenssrc/css/tokens.cssEvery value the product may use. Nothing that uses themnothing
foundationssrc/css/foundations.cssReset, focus ring, motion laws, keyframestokens
atomssrc/css/atoms.cssParts that cannot be divided further and still mean anythingtokens, foundations
moleculessrc/css/molecules.cssSmall groups of atoms doing one jobabove
organismssrc/css/organisms.cssDistinct sections and the screensabove
templatessrc/css/templates.cssThe device frame and the page it sits onabove
utilitiessrc/css/utilities.cssOne job, no opinionabove
developersrc/css/developer.cssTooling. Last in the order, so it can never affect product rendering. The prototype does load it (see the note below). This documentation page does notabove
Two honest exceptions to the table above. The prototype does load developer.css, the eighth <link> in index.html, because the demo tooling has to work in the deployed prototype. It sits last in the layer order, so it cannot change how a product component renders. Drop it the day the prototype becomes a product. The other exception is filing. Six tooling rules used to live in templates.css. They are in developer-layout.css now, which still declares @layer templates, so their cascade position is unchanged. Promoting them into the developer layer would have pushed them past utilities into a source-order fight with developer.css, which already styles the same two blocks. That is a cascade change wearing a tidy-up's clothes. They changed address, not rank.

Why layers rather than specificity

The order is declared once, at the top of tokens.css. A later layer beats an earlier one whatever the specificity. A screen's override no longer has to out-specify an atom. It lives further down the list instead. That keeps selectors flat and removes the reason people reach for !important.

There is one consequence. Two rules of equal specificity that relied on which was typed first will flip when they land in different layers. Splitting the stylesheet did that. The flips were caught by comparing every computed property on every element before and after. The rules involved were pinned into their original file, so today's rendering is preserved.

Pinning had a cost. Three .o-signup-sheet__resend rules stayed in atoms.css, against the filing law, because moving them might have moved pixels. That has since been tested rather than assumed. They are in organisms.css now. The fingerprint of all 104 states is unchanged. The conflict was real but it was not, in the end, a trade.

Building This in React

This system is a stylesheet. It is being rebuilt as React components. Everything below is what carries over, what changes shape and what should be left behind.

What carries over unchanged

The tokens, the accessibility contract and the state model. Those three are the system. The class names are not: they are how a stylesheet expresses a component when it has no other way to.

In CSS todayIn ReactWhy
213 custom properties on :rootone theme object, or the same custom properties kept as-isThey already have semantic names. Renaming them to camelCase buys nothing and breaks every measured contrast pair in this document.
.a-btn--primary<Button variant="primary">A modifier is a value of one prop, not a separate component.
data-state="error"state="error" or invalidRuntime state is already an attribute rather than a class, so it maps straight to a prop.
@layer orderwhatever your styling tool orders byThe layers exist so a screen can override an atom without out-specifying it. Keep the guarantee, not the mechanism.
aria-invalid, role="status", aria-modalthe same attributesThese are the product's accessibility contract. They are not styling and they do not get an opinion.

The component API, derived from the CSS

Every prop below is a modifier or a state attribute that exists in the stylesheet today. Nothing here is invented: if a component has no variants, it has no variant prop.

ComponentPropsNotes
Button variant: 'primary' | 'outline' | 'onDark' | 'outlineOnDark'
size: 'md' | 'sm'
lead?: boolean
disabled?: boolean		 lead is only meaningful on outline. Loading is composition rather than a prop: put a Spinner in the children.
Link variant: 'default' | 'quiet' | 'onDark'
flush?: boolean
disabled?: boolean		 Renders button or a depending on whether it navigates. Both keep the 48px height.
Field state: 'idle' | 'error' | 'pending' Must render a label wrapping the input. That is what makes the whole 56px box focus the control.
Helper tone?: 'error' | 'warning' | 'success' The tone injects the glyph. Tie it to the field with aria-describedby.
IconWrap variant: 'default' | 'bare' | 'lg' | 'mark' | 'onDark'
tone?: 'error' | 'warning' | 'success' | 'info'
motion?: 'pop'		 info exists only on mark. The glyph inherits ink from the row, so do not set a colour on the icon.
OtpCell filled?: boolean
tone?: 'error' | 'success' | 'locked'		 Two independent axes. Filled and wrong at once is a real combination, so do not collapse them into one enum.
Scrim state: 'opening' | 'open' | 'closing' Three values because the exit has to be animated before unmount.
Toast visible?: boolean One instance near the root, with role="status". Not one per screen.
OtpGroup tone?: 'error' | 'success' The group holds the outcome and mirrors it onto every cell.
ResendRow single?: boolean single is the layout when only one control remains.
SheetHeader heading?: boolean Defaults true. False is the outcome stages, where the panel supplies its own title.
SignupSheet stage: 'number' | 'code' | 'blocked'
state: 'opening' | 'active' | 'closing'
lead?: boolean, more?: boolean		 The one component whose state machine is worth porting as a machine rather than as booleans.
DeviceFrame width: 390 | 375 | 360 | 320
keyboard: 'open' | 'closed'		 Prototype furniture. See what not to port, below.

Four laws that must survive the rewrite

  1. State is never a class. It is already an attribute here. In React it becomes a prop. What must not happen is a class like is-open reappearing, because then two things describe the same state and one of them goes stale.
  2. A parent may place a child but never restyle it. A screen may say where a button sits and how much room it takes. It may not reach in and change its colour. In React that means layout props and wrappers, never a descendant selector into another component's internals.
  3. One primary action per context. Exactly one variant="primary" visible at a time. When priority moves, move the coat with lead rather than adding a second primary.
  4. No component sets a font size in pixels. Every size comes from the type scale, because a second scale exists for large text and a hard-coded size will not answer it.

What not to port

Leave behindBecause
t-device-frame, p-signup-demoThey draw a phone on a desktop page so the prototype can be reviewed in a browser. A real app is already in a phone.
m-status-bar, m-notch, m-keyboardDrawn operating-system chrome. The real ones come from the device.
The --kb- token familyIt paints the drawn keyboard and nothing else.
The developer layer and every d- classDemo tooling: the state rail, the inspector, the layer overlay.
--keyboard-insetIt exists because the prototype simulates a keyboard. On a device this is the visual viewport API.
The @supports glass fallbacksWorth re-checking against your real browser support rather than inheriting.

Suggested order

  1. The tokens, as a theme. Nothing else can be built honestly first.
  2. The primitives: reset, focus ring, reduced-motion law. They are global and they are small.
  3. The five atoms the sign-up flow needs: Button, Link, Field, Label, Helper. That is a whole screen.
  4. The molecules those atoms compose into, then the organisms.
  5. The screens last, because a screen is only a template with real content in it.

Check yourself against the gates rather than against the pixels. This repository verifies contrast on rendered pixels across 104 states, tap targets against --size-target, plus that nothing is stranded past a scroller. Those checks describe outcomes rather than implementations, so they are worth rebuilding in whatever the React project uses for tests. See Contribution and Governance.

UI Principles

Twelve rules the system already follows. Each one is stated so it can be checked, says where it is enforced and carries the exception the code actually takes. A principle with no stated exception is a principle somebody is quietly breaking.

Visual hierarchy

Rank is carried by three tokens at once: size, weight and text colour. A colour may never outrank its role. --color-text-tertiary is for inert controls and placeholders, --color-text-muted for decoration. A line a driver reads takes neither.

Enforced at: src/css/tokens.css:104-107. --color-text-tertiary: #6C6C70; /* disabled and placeholder, 4.69:1 on sunken. 3.72:1 on the sheet, so it is for inert controls only, never for a line being read. */ and --color-text-muted: #b0bcd0; /* decorative only, never load-bearing text */. Applied at src/css/organisms.css:371-381, where .o-outcome-panel__clock takes --text-title / --weight-heavy / --color-text-primary while .o-outcome-panel__meta takes --text-caption2 / --weight-medium / --color-text-secondary, under the comment "A supporting offer, not the outcome, so it sits below the clock and quieter than both the title and the number."

The exception: The break is written up at engenxt-onboarding.html:2072-2074. .o-step__label in tertiary measures 4.19:1 on the page grey it sits on. At 11px it gets no large-text allowance. It is "a line being read", while tertiary is "for controls that are switched off". In src/css/organisms.css the stated exception runs the other way: .o-signup-sheet__resend:disabled { color: var(--color-text-secondary); } with "Except this one... the countdown is content, so it takes the colour content takes". A disabled control refuses the disabled ink on purpose.

Priority of content and actions

When the keyboard takes the screen, height is given up in one fixed order. Nothing jumps the queue: 1 tooling, 2 the step counter, 3 an instruction the driver has already acted on, 4 the loose spacing steps. The base gap is never given up, at any width.

Enforced at: src/css/templates.css:47-59. "THE COMPRESSION ORDER... It must not be the base gap: the base gap is what says which things belong together". That matters most when there is least room to read it. Enforced in that order at templates.css:60 ([data-keyboard="open"] .a-progress { display: none }), :66, :69-70. Restated as law at index.html:3580, RULES['Keyboard'].

The exception: The exception sits inside step 3. src/css/templates.css:63-66 scopes the selector to .o-signup-sheet[data-stage="number"] .m-sheet-header__sub rather than all stages: "On the code stage it names the channel and the masked number, which is exactly what a driver checks against their handset mid-code, so it stays." Widen that selector to every stage and the rule breaks. A second worked failure sits at templates.css:72-77. Giving the sheet the full height without giving the stage a way to spend it parked the CTA "at the furthest point from the thumb that is still on the sheet", over 97px of empty sheet.

Proximity and grouping

Every rendered gap inside a stage is 8, 16 or 24, with 32 only to close a centred group. Each number means exactly one thing. Looser steps are the base gap plus a margin on the element that starts them, never a base plus a nudge.

Enforced at: src/css/organisms.css:243-255: "The organism owns the vertical rhythm. Nothing in the markup sets spacing." Under ONE SCALE, THREE MEANINGS, "every rendered gap is 8, 16 or 24 and each number means one thing". 8 is parts of one thing (label to its field, field to its helper, the cells to the line that reports on them), 16 is one group to the next (the control block to the card about it), 24 is a decision or a different route. The reason follows: "Gaps used to be composed as 16 plus a 4px nudge, which rendered 18 and 20: values close enough to the group step to read as noise rather than meaning." The arithmetic is written on each rule: organisms.css:263 "8 + 8 = 16", :267 "8 + 16 = 24", :330 "8 + 24 = 32". Machine-checked at index.html:3854, ['Spacing', 'every rendered gap is on the scale', ... var ok = [4, 8, 16, 24, 32]].

The exception: Admitted and left unfixed at src/css/organisms.css:273-281. Gaps are authored between boxes: "a 48px target holds about 18px of line, so it carries ~15px of nothing above and below it. The eye is therefore shown roughly 23, 39 and 31 where the scale says 8, 24 and 16." The negative-margin correction was tried and "overshot: it tightened the button gap to 17 and closed the row to 1", so it "wants a designer looking at it rather than a blind correction". The measured gate passes. The eye does not.

Alignment

Alignment is decided by whether the thing has a neighbour to line up with, not by house style. A key-over-value stack is left-aligned only when it sits in a column that shares one reading edge. Standing alone on a centred panel, it centres. Any such override is scoped to the context, never written onto the atom.

Enforced at: src/css/organisms.css:128-140, .o-status-page__meta .m-figure { align-items: center; text-align: center; }. A key-over-value stack is left-aligned so that a COLUMN of them shares one edge to read down. There is no column here. The left edge buys nothing and it costs the obvious thing: DOWNLOAD SIZE is twice the width of 42 MB, so the number sits off under one end of its own label. The comment says it is scoped to this row on purpose. The same atom sits in the home notice on the dark hero, in a block whose every line is left-aligned, where staying left-aligned is right. The atom's own default stays left at src/css/molecules.css:228, .m-figure { ... text-align: left; }.

The exception: The nearby failure is quoted at organisms.css:124-126. The row itself "sat on the left edge of cards whose every other line is centred - the one left-handed thing on the screen", fixed with justify-content: center at :126. The stated exception to centring is .m-resend-row[data-single="true"] { justify-content: center; } (molecules.css:226). The row centres only when a state has withdrawn one of the two links, "so the survivor centres rather than sitting hard left where its partner used to balance it."

Consistency

A component reads tokens and never raw values. If a value has no token, the token is added first. Two gates hold it. Nothing painted may resolve to a value with no token. No token may be declared and never used. The only permitted unused tokens are on a named list saying which sibling flow spends each one.

Enforced at: index.html:3572, RULES['Tokens']: "A component reads tokens, never raw values. If you need a value that has no token, add the token first." Gates at index.html:3871 (['Tokens', 'no component paints a value that has no token']) and index.html:3945 (['Tokens', 'no token is declared and never used']). The naming rationale is at src/css/tokens.css:238-245: the file already used 0.97 and 0.985, "plus one 0.99 on a station row that was doing the subtle job at a value of its own. Naming them is what stops a third appearing."

The exception: --press-subtle (src/css/tokens.css:247) is declared in the sign-up bundle and nothing in src/css uses it. Grep finds its only users in the other file: engenxt-onboarding.html:1502, 1696, 2429, 2524, 3442. It survives the dead-token gate only because index.html:3993 lists it by hand: '--press-subtle': 'the app's rows and cards, which are tapped rather than aimed at'. The comment at index.html:3985 is blunt about the rest. Anything not on the list "is still dead the moment it is unused".

Responsive and mobile-first behaviour

The component decides, not the window: four real device widths in points (390x844, 375x812, 360x800, 320x720), plus page reflow at 900px and full bleed at 440px. Text size belongs to the user. [data-text="large"] repoints the whole --text-* ramp on the root, so every component grows without knowing it did. 320 and large text must be tested together.

Enforced at: src/css/templates.css:113-125: "360 x 800 is not an iPhone at all: it is the single most common Android viewport, which for a South African fleet is the one that matters most. 320 is not a current phone, it is the floor, kept as a stress test." Ramp repointing at src/css/tokens.css:430-446. Breakpoints at templates.css:158-164 and :172-176. Stated at index.html:3579, RULES['Responsive']: "Test at 320 and at large text together, because that is the pair that breaks things."

The exception: The drawn Android keyboard is exempt on purpose. src/css/molecules.css:254-260: "These sizes deliberately sit outside the type ramp." The ramp exists so app text answers the driver's text-size setting. A drawing of Android's own keyboard must not follow it, because "a real keyboard does not grow when app text does" (font-size: 21px, font-size: 9px). The opposite exception is tokens.css:445, --size-otp-cell: 60px; /* the square has to keep containing its glyph */: one non-type size that has to follow the ramp anyway.

One clear primary action per context

Exactly one visible .a-btn--primary per active context. Every other action is .a-btn--outline. When priority moves, the primary coat moves with it via data-lead rather than a second primary appearing. A blocked screen replaces its action with the recovery instead of showing both.

Enforced at: src/css/atoms.css:34-37 .a-btn--primary { background: var(--gradient-primary); color: var(--color-text-on-dark); box-shadow: var(--shadow-btn); } against atoms.css:44-50 .a-btn--outline { min-height: var(--size-target); background: transparent; color: var(--color-text-secondary); border: 1px solid var(--color-border-field); font-weight: var(--weight-semibold); }. index.html:2130: "The home screen has exactly one action. When something blocks entry the action is replaced by the recovery, never left on screen looking dead." src/css/organisms.css:147-148: "o-status-page. A blocking status with one recovery action... because an account choice is not the answer." src/css/templates.css:31-37 fades .o-auth-hero__actions to opacity 0 once the sheet is up, because "a driver who taps it is tapping the thing they have already done".

The exception: The sanctioned override is src/css/atoms.css:55-60. .a-btn--outline[data-lead="true"] takes var(--gradient-primary), --weight-bold and --shadow-btn: "when a person becomes the last resort the scan sheet says so with data-lead", so the fact wears the primary coat. The coat moves. It is not duplicated. Same move for the link at atoms.css:132. The rule is one hidden attribute away from being broken at index.html:296-298, where the biometrics panel holds two .a-btn--primary in the DOM (bioEnable, bioContinue). It stays legal only because bioContinue carries hidden, which foundations.css:38 forces to display: none !important.

Interaction feedback

Every pressable thing reports the press with a scale token chosen by what it is: --press-firm (0.97) for a control you aim at, --press-subtle (0.985) for a surface you tap. A third value is drift unless the rule states its reason in place. Disabled controls suppress the press rather than animating a dead action.

Enforced at: src/css/tokens.css:238-247: two depths, "because there are two kinds of thing to press", a control you aim at and a surface you tap. "Naming them is what stops a third appearing." Consumed at src/css/atoms.css:32 .a-btn:active { transform: scale(var(--press-firm)); transition-duration: .1s; }, suppressed at atoms.css:87 .a-btn[aria-disabled="true"]:active, .a-btn:disabled:active { transform: none; }. Documented in the panel at docs/design-system/index.html:745: "--press-firm on controls, --press-subtle on rows".

The exception: The one legal third value names itself at engenxt-onboarding.html:2061-2063: /* Not --press-firm. This is imitating a physical shutter rather than a button, and the travel is the point of it. */ .a-shutter:active .a-shutter__ring { transform: scale(0.92); }, pre-declared as legal by tokens.css:244-245. The illegal third value is the one the token was written to kill: "one 0.99 on a station row that was doing the subtle job at a value of its own" (tokens.css:242-243).

Typography and readability

Every size comes from the --text-* ramp, every weight from the seven named --weight-* values, every line-height from one of four leading jobs, repeating tracking from the five --tracking-* steps. An off-scale value is legal only as a single-instance optic. It must say so on the rule that uses it.

Enforced at: src/css/tokens.css:200-219 (the 13-step ramp), :222-236 ("four values carry ninety-six per cent of the uses - it simply had no names, so nothing could tell a considered 800 from a typo... a stray 750 is now visibly not one of these"), :250-259 ("1.45, 1.5, 1.55 and 1.6 were four ways of saying 'body'; 1.05, 1.08, 1.1, 1.18 and 1.2 were five ways of saying 'headline'"), :261-269 ("a handful of headline optics keep their own value and say so where they are written").

The exception: Two rules take that exemption and both declare it. src/css/organisms.css:79-82: .o-auth-hero__title { ... letter-spacing: -1.6px; }, "Off the scale: one headline optic." At 42px the display step is too loose and the hero step too tight. It is the only 42px line. And src/css/molecules.css:86-89: .m-brand-lockup__tag { ... letter-spacing: 1.5px; }, "Off the tracking scale on purpose: this is a 10px uppercase strapline under a wordmark." Breaking it is a second component adopting -1.6px, which turns a stated one-off into an unnamed sixth tracking step that no gate catches. index.html:3871 measures only shadows and backgrounds.

Padding, margin and gap responsibilities

The organism owns the vertical rhythm through its gap. Nothing in the markup sets spacing. A parent may PLACE a child (where it sits, how much room it takes, whether it shows) but may never RESTYLE its insides. A child that must look different in a context carries a modifier for it.

Enforced at: src/css/organisms.css:243 "The organism owns the vertical rhythm. Nothing in the markup sets spacing." index.html:3575, RULES['Composition']: "Colour, type, padding, radius and border stay with the component that owns them... That is why the links on a content edge take a-link--flush rather than the row cancelling their padding." The modifier that keeps the law: src/css/atoms.css:108 .a-link--flush { padding-inline: 0; }, where "The vertical padding stays, because the 48px target depends on it". It is declared as a modifier rather than a row rule at molecules.css:221-223.

The exception: The law is written from two real breaches. At src/css/atoms.css:62-70, .o-scan__actions .a-btn--outline "set the border and the ink from the organism - which is the Composition law's own worked example of what a parent may not do", fixed by writing .a-btn--outlineOnDark. At src/css/molecules.css:66-75, .o-home .m-brand-lockup__name recoloured a child and "was invisible to the parity gate, which drops any rule whose classes do not all root into the manifest. So the gate went on proving the lockup identical in both files while the two files rendered it in different colours." That crossing has since been closed. Three rules styling .o-signup-sheet__resend from inside @layer atoms now sit with the organism that owns them, verified computed-identical across all 104 states.

Accessibility

Every interactive control in the product clears 48px in its smallest dimension (--size-target), counting hit area from an absolutely-positioned pseudo-element. One two-tone focus ring serves the whole file. Colour never carries meaning alone. Any state signalled by colour re-states itself structurally under forced-colors. Every animation collapses to a still frame under reduced motion.

Enforced at: src/css/tokens.css:318 --size-target: 48px; /* minimum tap target */, consumed at atoms.css:45, :72, :78, :91 and organisms.css:216. Where the visible box is smaller, a pseudo-element reaches the floor (molecules.css:187-189 .m-sheet-header__close::after { inset: 0 -6px -12px -6px; }, "36 + 12 = the 48 the tap floor asks for"; organisms.css:309-311 .o-signup-sheet__noteMore::before { inset: -17px -10px; } /* 15px of text to the 48px floor */). One ring at foundations.css:30-34. "Tone is never carried by colour alone" at atoms.css:239. forced-colors block at atoms.css:390-397. Reduced motion at foundations.css:71-83. Measured, not asserted: tools/sweep.js:110-111 reads the token off the root: "One law, one writer: the floor is the token the spec panel reads, not a number this file made up."

The exception: index.html:3577 names two documented exceptions to the 48px floor. First, the six OTP cells, because "six squares and five gaps cannot each clear 48 inside 320 minus the gutters, so the row takes the largest size that fits rather than pretending the rule holds" (templates.css:166-170 tightens .m-otp-group to gap: var(--sp-1); the gate skips them at index.html:3895, if (/a-otp-cell/.test(cls)) { return; }). Second, the whole d- layer, "a desktop review surface driven with a mouse". A third exception runs against the focus ring: organisms.css:356-365 removes it from .o-outcome-panel__title and .o-status-page__title, because script focuses them to announce a screen: "a ring drawn round a heading nobody moved to reads as a control."

Documented exceptions

An exception is legal only where it is written beside the code that breaks the rule and says what the break bought. The shape is always the same three parts: name the rule, name the exception, name the reason. A rule that cannot be exempted says so in the same breath.

Enforced at: The pattern repeats verbatim across the files: index.html:3577 "Two documented exceptions"; src/css/atoms.css:126-131 "Except this one."; src/css/atoms.css:321 "5px is a deliberate hairline bar thickness, not a grid value"; src/css/tokens.css:244-245 pre-authorises the shutter's 0.92; src/css/molecules.css:254-256 exempts the drawn keyboard from the ramp; src/css/organisms.css:80-82 exempts one 42px line from the tracking scale. The rule that refuses exemption is src/css/tokens.css:97-99: "Error TEXT is darker than the brand red. --color-secondary is the Engen red in the logo, so it must not be darkened to satisfy a text contrast rule". Hence a second red, --color-error-text: #B81510, rather than a bent brand colour.

The exception: Two exception comments in the repo have gone stale, which is the real failure mode of this pattern. src/css/molecules.css:203 still reads "6px between cells is optical" while the rule it heads, molecules.css:211, declares gap: var(--sp-2), which is 8px. A later comment at :206-210 corrects it. The first was never updated to match. src/css/molecules.css:214-215 claims "the negative inline margin pulls the links back onto the content edge" while .m-resend-row at :216-219 contains no negative margin at all. The job moved to .a-link--flush and the comment stayed. The system's own defence against this is tokens.css:44-47, where a manifest in a comment "goes stale silently". That one had gone stale, naming a template that no longer exists and an attribute that never did.

Foundations

213 tokens: every value the product may use, plus nothing that uses them. These are the decisions a React implementation has to carry over first: a component can be rewritten, but if the scale underneath it changes, the product changes.

Reading the stylesheet…

Token Architecture

A mature scale usually has two or three: a palette of raw values (--blue-60), a semantic layer that gives each one a job (--color-text-primary: var(--blue-60)) and sometimes a third per component. This one has a single tier named as though it had two.

Understand that before you change anything. The names already say what a value is for rather than what it is. That is the hard half of the work and it's done. What's missing is the indirection underneath. --color-text-primary holds #0d1e2a directly, so two roles that share a colour today have no way of saying they are the same colour. The counts below are measured from the live stylesheet, not asserted.

Do not "fix" this by adding a palette tier on a quiet afternoon. It is safe only if every value is preserved exactly. Prove that with the fingerprint instrument described under Traceability, not by eye. A half-done palette, where some roles point at it and some still hold literals, is worse than the single tier you started with.

Colour

One tier, named for the job rather than the value. There is no separate palette underneath, so --color-text-primary holds #0d1e2a directly. A React theme should keep these names: they are already the semantic layer.

Reference Palette

There is no reference palette. The brand values live inside the semantic names, and the tinted steps are derived from them with color-mix() rather than written out. Those derived tokens are marked alias below.

Semantic Colours

Text and Surface Colours

Text is --color-text-primary, -secondary, -tertiary and -muted, in falling order of contrast. Surfaces are --color-surface, -sunken, -page and -hover. The --color-on-dark-* family is white at six alphas, for the hero and the scanner.

Action and Feedback Colours

--color-primary for the action, --color-secondary for the brand red, then --color-success, --color-warning and --color-error-text, each with a matching -bg.

Contrast and Usage

Two tokens are restricted and the restriction is not visible in the value. --color-text-tertiary is 3.72:1 on the sheet: it is for inert controls and placeholders, never for a line being read. --color-text-muted is decorative only. Measured pairs:

InkOnRatioVerdict
--color-text-primary--color-surface16.99AAA
--color-text-secondary--color-surface7.04AAA
--color-text-tertiary--color-surface5.23AA, inert controls only
--color-error-text--color-secondary-bg5.91AA
--color-success--color-success-bg6.12AA
--color-warning--color-warning-bg5.69AA
--color-text-on-dark--color-hero-118.13AAA

Gradients and Glass

Typography

The ramp is in the token table above. This is every place the product actually sets a size: 37 text styles across the five production stylesheets. Each one lists its role, its tokens, the element it is used on and when not to reach for it.

21 of the 37 set no line-height and inherit the user agent's normal. That is roughly 1.2 and is not a step on the four-value --leading-* scale. Only 16 take a leading token. That is the largest single gap in the type system. Two-line labels collide there when large text is on. It is recorded, not fixed, because fixing it moves pixels.

There is a measured proposal for it: line-height-proposal.md. It groups all 21 by semantic role, with before-and-after boxes at both text sizes. Three of them already change shape under large text, going from one line to two: .m-figure__key, .o-auth-hero__how and .o-status-page__title. Nothing in it has been applied.

2 styles use a literal pixel size, marked px below. Both sit inside the drawn Android keyboard. That is operating-system chrome and must not grow when app text does. It is the one sanctioned exemption. Anywhere else a literal size is a bug, because it will not answer [data-text="large"].

Font Families, Scale, Weights, Leading and Tracking

Large-Text Behaviour

Fifteen tokens are declared twice. The second set lives under [data-text="large"] and takes over when the driver turns large text on. One attribute on the root resizes the whole flow, because no component holds a font size of its own.

This is the payoff for having a type scale at all. It's easy to break without noticing. A hard-coded font-size: 15px looks identical to var(--text-body) until somebody switches this on. Then it's the one line of text on the screen that didn't grow.

The steps are not uniform. That is deliberate. Body sizes gain 2px, the three display sizes gain 4px and the OTP cell gains 8px so the digits keep their room. In proportion the smallest text grows most: around a fifth at --text-nano against a sixteenth at --text-figure. That is the right way round, since the small text is what people turn this on for.

Spacing

A 4px base: --sp-1 through --sp-8, 4px to 32px. Two half-steps join it, --sp-1h (6px) and --sp-2h (10px), because both are structural here rather than exceptional. There is no 18. Gaps that composed to 18 and 20 sat close enough to the group step to read as noise rather than meaning, so they were removed. The tokens and their resolved values are in the Tokens table under Spacing.

The Scale

Sizing and Layout

Object sizes, control heights and the one runtime property. --size-target is the 48px floor every interactive thing clears. --size-control is 56px, the height of a primary button and a field.

--keyboard-inset is declared at 0px and owned at runtime. When the keyboard opens, the page writes the real height onto the device frame. Three layers size themselves against it: the sheet's height and max-height, the frame's own height, the drawn keypad.

The declaration earns its place. Every one of its six consumers was written as var(--keyboard-inset, 0px), so the property resolved only because each of them carried its own fallback. That made those fallbacks load-bearing rather than defensive. Drop one and the sheet collapses silently, with nothing to fail. Declared, the fallbacks become belt and braces and the property has a stated default.

Zero is the whole of the default. No other keyboard height belongs in the stylesheet. The real number comes from the operating system at runtime, so a second value here would be a guess competing with a measurement. The unmigrated page carries the declaration for token parity and does not consume it.

Borders and Strokes

Two width tokens: --size-hairline at 0.5px for device chrome, and --size-border at 1.5px for fields. Outline controls use a literal 1px, which is a convention rather than a token. The OTP cell runs its own 2 / 2.5 / 3px state ramp. Colours are --color-border, -light, -strong and -field.

Radius

Shadows and Elevation

One light source, from above. Focus rings and glows are optics rather than elevation: they are drawn in place from colour tokens and are not on this scale.

Opacity

No opacity scale exists. Production holds six raw values (.85 .55 .5 .45 .35 .20). Three of them are different answers to "this control is inert". A React implementation should decide the scale rather than port the six. Recorded under Known Limitations.

Motion

--ease-out for anything arriving, --ease-emphasis for the sheet, --ease-spring where an overshoot is wanted, and --ease-in-out for a state change on a control that is neither arriving nor leaving. The keyframes themselves are under Code Primitives.

Layers and Z-Index

Responsive Breakpoints

There are no breakpoint tokens. The product is a phone app, so what varies is the device width rather than the window: 390, 375, 360 and 320, carried on data-width on the frame. The two real media queries belong to the demo page around the phone, at 900px and 440px. Both are raw pixel values in the stylesheet.

Icons and Assets

Icons are inline SVG sized by attribute rather than by token, at 13, 16, 17, 18 and 25 pixels. --size-glyph exists but has one consumer. --size-plate and --size-plate-lg size the wrap behind an icon. There are no image assets, no icon font and no sprite: every glyph is markup.

Operating-System Chrome

The --kb- family paints the drawn Android keyboard. It is not product surface and a React implementation should not port it: a real keyboard comes from the operating system.

Code Primitives

The rules that apply to bare elements, before any class exists. A primitive has no name you can call. You get it by using the element. It's the one layer a developer inherits without knowing it is there, so read it before you write anything.

All of it lives in src/css/foundations.css, inside @layer foundations. That's second of eight, so any atom, molecule or screen with an opinion beats these rules whatever the specificity. A primitive sets the default. It never wins an argument.

The reset

Three declarations, applied to everything including generated boxes. Border-box makes a width the width you see, so a padded field still fits its column. Zeroing margin and padding means somebody asked for every gap on the page. No browser default is quietly contributing. 

*, *::before, *::after { box-sizing: border-box; margin: 0; padding: 0; }

The ground

body paints the page colour and sets the family and ink that everything else inherits. It's the only place in the system that names a font family. Every other rule inherits it or is a bug. 

body {
  min-height: 100vh;
  background: var(--color-surface-page);
  font-family: var(--font-sans);
  -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased;
  color: var(--color-text-primary);
}

Form controls don't inherit type from their parent. Browsers hand them a system face instead, so the inheritance is repaired here by hand. button is stripped back to a plain box as well. Every button on the page then takes its appearance from a class rather than from the user agent. 

input, button, a { font-family: inherit; }
button { background: none; border: none; color: inherit; }

The focus ring

One ring for the entire system, two-tone on purpose. The dark core reads against the white sheet. The light halo around it reads against the near-black hero. A single-colour ring would disappear on one of the two. The sign-up flow uses both. 

:focus-visible {
  outline: 3px solid var(--color-focus);
  outline-offset: 2px;
  box-shadow: 0 0 0 6px var(--color-focus-halo);
}

:focus-visible rather than :focus, so the ring appears for a keyboard and not for a tap. Never remove it from a component. If it looks wrong on something you're building, that thing sits too tight against its neighbour. Give it room instead of taking the ring away.

:focus-visible Press Tab through these. The ring is the real one, from the production CSS
A link
A link on the hero

Hiding

[hidden] is forced to display: none. It's the one !important in the foundations layer and it earns the place. A hidden child of a flex row still takes its share of the gap if something else set it to display: flex. That leaves a hole with no visible cause. 

[hidden] { display: none !important; }

Use the attribute, not a class. Hiding is state. Here, state is never a class.

The nudge

A wrong entry shakes the control the driver can actually correct: the field, not the screen. Moving the whole column moves the parts that are fine too. That reads as "everything is wrong" when one thing is. 

[data-shake="true"] { animation: shakeX var(--dur-slow) ease; }
Why it is on the field and not the card. Two animations on one element share one animation shorthand. When the shake finished, the shorthand fell back to the card's stageIn and replayed the entrance. Field, helper, card and button faded out then floated back up, half a second after the error appeared. Different elements, no shared property, no replay. Adding a second animation to an element that already has one is the trap.

Motion

Every keyframe in the system is declared here, so a component can reach for an existing movement instead of inventing a near-copy. The list below is read out of the live stylesheet at a fixed demo duration. In the product each one plays at its own duration token.

Durations and easings are tokens, not numbers: --dur-fast through --dur-slow, plus four easings. --ease-out is the default for anything arriving, --ease-emphasis is for the sheet and --ease-spring is only for where an overshoot is wanted. Full values are in Tokens under Duration and Easing.

Reduced motion is honoured twice

Once for the operating system setting. Once for the demo toggle in the developer panel. A stakeholder watching on a borrowed laptop can't change the OS preference, but the flow still has to be reviewable with the movement off. 

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { /* … */ }
[data-motion="reduced"] *, [data-motion="reduced"] *::before, … { /* … */ }

Both collapse animation and transition to 0.001ms rather than 0. Anything waiting on a transitionend or animationend event still receives it, so the flow doesn't stall. Both reset animation-delay and animation-iteration-count too. A staggered entrance won't leave the last element invisible for half a second. A loop won't run forever at a millisecond a turn.

They are !important and apply to every element, so a new component gets reduced-motion support for free. You write nothing. You do need the end state to be the readable one: if an element animates in from opacity: 0, it must not be left at zero when the animation is skipped.

u-visually-hidden

Text that only a screen reader gets.

LevelUtility OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/utilities.css StatusStable

One job, no opinion. It clips an element to a single pixel and takes it out of flow, so it stays in the accessibility tree while occupying no space. The live region that announces every screen change uses it.

Do not reach for display: none or visibility: hidden when you want this. Both remove the element from the accessibility tree, which is the opposite of the intent. Use [hidden] when you want it gone for everyone. 

<span class="u-visually-hidden" id="announcer" role="status" aria-live="polite"></span>

React note. This becomes a VisuallyHidden component or a shared style, not a class a caller remembers to add. The live region it wraps is a single app-level instance that everything writes to, so it belongs near the root of the tree rather than inside a screen.

Atoms

Atoms are the smallest functional UI elements. They cannot be divided further without losing their meaning.

13 exist. Each one below carries the same twelve subsections, so a component can be read in the same order every time: what it is for, what it is made of, what it can look like, what it can be doing, then the exact markup to copy.

a-btn

The label and the box of every pressable action in the product.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

Primary actionthe one button a driver is meant to press

Purpose and Usage

Use it for any action a driver performs with a thumb. It is full width by default, because on a forecourt the target is the whole row rather than a word inside it.

Do not use it for navigation or for a change of mind. Those are a-link, which reads as text rather than as a surface. One primary button per context: if a second action matters, it takes a-btn--outline.

Anatomy

A single element. It has no child parts of its own, which is what keeps it an atom. It centres its content on both axes with a --sp-2 gap, so a spinner or an icon placed beside the label sits correctly without any extra rule.

Label alone, then label with a child

Variants

Five, plus one that is reached by an attribute rather than a class.

On a light surface
.a-btn--primary .a-btn--outline .a-btn--outline.a-btn--sm .a-btn--outline[data-lead="true"]
On the hero
.a-btn--onDark .a-btn--outlineOnDark

--outline[data-lead="true"] is state rather than a variant. When the safest recovery becomes the leading action, the outline button takes the primary coat where it stands, so the two do not swap places under the thumb.

States

Default, disabled and busypress any of them to see the scale
default aria-disabled="true" disabled
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Defaultno attributethe variant's own coat
Hover:hoveroutline only, to --color-hover-wash. The filled variants do not respond, because a thumb does not hover
Focus visible:focus-visiblethe system ring from foundations, never removed here
Active:activescale(var(--press-firm)), 0.97, over 0.1s
Disabledaria-disabled="true" or disabledsunken fill, tertiary ink, no shadow, opacity 0.5. The press scale is cancelled
Loadingan a-spinner placed beside the labelnothing on the button itself. The gap already spaces it

Both disabled forms are styled on purpose. aria-disabled keeps the control in the focus order so a screen reader can find it and hear why it is inactive. The real disabled attribute takes it out. Primary actions use the ARIA form.

Behaviour

Press scales the whole button to 0.97 in 0.1s, then releases over --dur-fast. Colour, background and border move over --dur-base, so a change of variant reads as the same object changing rather than a new one arriving.

Keyboard behaviour is the browser's. It is a real <button>, so Enter and Space both fire it and it sits in the tab order without a tabindex.

Content Guidance

A verb and its object: "Send my code", not "Submit". Keep it to about three words. The label wraps rather than truncates. The button grows to hold it, so a long label costs vertical space rather than meaning. Nothing in the system truncates text.

Accessibility

  • It must be a <button type="button">. A styled div loses Enter, Space and the role.
  • Its accessible name is its text. An icon beside the label is aria-hidden, since it repeats a word already there.
  • Minimum height is --size-control (56px), above the --size-target floor of 48px.
  • The focus ring comes from the foundations layer and is never removed here.
  • Disabled state is announced, not merely greyed, because it carries a real attribute.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--size-controlminimum height, 56px
--size-targetminimum height of the outline and small variants, 48px
--sp-2gap between the label and anything beside it
--radius-pillthe corner
--text-subhead · --weight-bold · --tracking-tightthe label
--gradient-primary · --color-text-on-dark · --shadow-btnthe primary coat
--color-border-fieldthe outline edge, chosen for contrast on glass
--color-surface-sunken · --color-text-tertiarydisabled
--press-firmthe press scale, 0.97
--dur-fast · --dur-base · --ease-in-outthe transitions

Markup Contract

<button type="button" class="a-btn a-btn--primary">Send my code</button>

<button type="button" class="a-btn a-btn--outline">Not now</button>

<button type="button" class="a-btn a-btn--primary" aria-disabled="true">Send my code</button>

A screen may add its own class alongside for position, as o-signup-sheet__cta does. It must not restyle the button from there.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It is full width, so it follows whatever column it is in. The device widths the product targets do not change it. The demo page's breakpoints do not reach inside the frame.

Related Components

Parent: o-signup-sheet and o-auth-hero place it. Alternative: a-link for a text control. Child it commonly holds: a-spinner.

a-link

A text control for a way out or a way round.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

The base linka control that reads as text

Purpose and Usage

Use it for a second route: another code, another channel, another number, help. It reads as text so it cannot compete with the one action a driver is meant to take. It is still 48px tall so a thumb can land on it.

Do not use it for the main action. That is a-btn, which is a surface rather than a word. Do not reach for a link because a button felt too loud either. If the action matters enough to be the second thing on a screen it takes a-btn--outline.

Anatomy

A single element, no child parts. It is an inline flex box that centres its label on both axes, with min-height at --size-target and --space-control-block above and below. The height and the padding together are what make 48px, which is why --flush only drops the inline padding.

It sets font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums. The resend countdown ticks through 0:45 to 0:00 inside this atom. Proportional digits make the label twitch on every second.

Variants

Three. They stack: the two ways out under the code cells ship as --quiet --flush.

On the sheet
.a-link .a-link--quiet .a-link--quiet.a-link--flush
On the hero
.a-link--onDarkGet help on WhatsApp
VariantWhat it changesWhen to reach for it
--quiet--color-text-secondary, underlined at 3px offseta route that is not the point of the screen. It differentiates upward from disabled rather than sharing its grey
--flushpadding-inline: 0a link sitting on a container's content edge. The block padding stays, because the 48px target depends on it
--onDarkwhite ink, underlined in --color-on-dark-faintthe hero. It is the only face built for a dark ground

The base link is not underlined. It relies on --color-primary-dark against secondary ink to read as a control. Both other faces are underlined, because a grey link on grey text and a white link on a near-black hero cannot carry the job on hue alone.

States

Live and disabledtab to any of them to see the ring
default disabled --quiet, disabled
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Defaultno attributethe variant's own ink
Focus visible:focus-visiblethe system ring from foundations. The atom writes no focus rule, so nothing here can cancel it
Disabled:disabled or [disabled]--color-text-tertiary, weight drops to --weight-medium, cursor to default

No hover and no press. A hover state on a control this size would be styling for a mouse that is not there. The label has no surface to scale.

One instance overrides the disabled colour on purpose. Disabled, o-signup-sheet__resend is not a control being ignored: it is the sentence telling a driver when they can try again. So the organism puts the ink back to --color-text-secondary. Tertiary is 3.72:1 on the sheet, which is a colour for inert controls rather than for a line being read. That is the only exemption to this atom's disabled rule, written where the countdown lives. The weight stays at 500, which is how the waiting countdown is told apart from "Resend" at 600.

Behaviour

No transition of its own. Colour changes land immediately when the resend timer expires, which is the moment a driver is waiting for.

Keyboard behaviour is the element's. Use <button type="button"> unless the control truly navigates; use <a href> when it leaves the app, as the WhatsApp help link on the hero does. Disabled is the real attribute rather than an ARIA one, so a countdown that cannot be pressed leaves the tab order instead of catching focus once a second.

Content Guidance

A verb and its object, sentence case, no arrow, no "click here". "Request another code", "Use a different registered number". Say where it goes rather than what it is: "Get help on WhatsApp" names the destination, so nobody spends data finding out.

Labels wrap. m-resend-row is written to wrap its two links rather than crush them, which is what lets these labels stay long enough to be plain.

Accessibility

  • Minimum height is --size-target, 48px. Quiet text links are held to the same floor as buttons, with the block padding making the number up.
  • --flush removes only the inline padding, so the target survives sitting on a content edge.
  • The focus ring comes from foundations. This atom declares no focus rule of any kind.
  • Disabled uses the real attribute, so the state is announced rather than merely grey.
  • --color-text-secondary is 5.01:1 on the composited sheet. The two underlined faces carry a second cue besides hue.
  • An <a> that opens a new tab takes rel="noopener" alongside target="_blank".

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--size-targetminimum height, 48px
--space-control-block · --sp-1padding, 8px block and 4px inline
--text-footnote · --weight-semibold · --tracking-tightthe label
--color-primary-darkthe base ink
--color-text-secondary--quiet
--color-text-on-dark · --color-on-dark-faint--onDark ink and its underline
--color-text-tertiary · --weight-mediumdisabled

Markup Contract

<button class="a-link a-link--flush o-signup-sheet__resend" id="resendBtn" type="button" disabled>Request another code in 0:45</button>

<button class="a-link a-link--quiet a-link--flush" id="channelSwitchBtn" type="button">Send code by SMS</button>

<button class="a-link a-link--quiet a-link--flush o-signup-sheet__noteMore" id="privacyMore" type="button" aria-expanded="false">More about your information</button>

<a class="a-link a-link--onDark" id="homeHelp" href="https://wa.me/…" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Get help on WhatsApp</a>

A screen may add its own class alongside for position, as o-signup-sheet__resend and o-signup-sheet__noteMore do. The one thing those classes are allowed to change is documented above, in States. The disclosure link carries aria-expanded because it opens a card rather than going somewhere.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It is inline flex, so it takes the width of its label and no more. Under [data-text="large"] --text-footnote goes from 14px to 16px, at which point the two links in m-resend-row stop fitting side by side and the row wraps them onto separate lines. The wrapping belongs to the row. The link only grows.

Related Components

Alternative: a-btn for the action a screen is for. Placed by: m-resend-row, o-signup-sheet and o-auth-hero.

a-field

The bordered box a driver types a single value into.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

The atom on its ownbox plus input, nothing else

Purpose and Usage

Use it for one typed value on one line. It draws the edge, the fill and the focus ring; the <input> inside it draws nothing. That split is the point of the component: an input with its own border cannot be made to share a box with a prefix or an icon without the two edges fighting.

Do not use it for the six code cells. Those are a-otp-cell in m-otp-group, which are one character each with their own two axes of state. Do not use it for a pressable row either. It has no press state because nothing about it is a button.

Anatomy

Two parts. .a-field is the box. .a-field__input is the control inside it. The box is a flex row with a --sp-3 gap, so a prefix placed before the input is spaced without any rule from the parent. The input takes flex: 1 with min-width: 0, so a long value scrolls inside the box rather than pushing it wide.

The box element is a <label>. That is load-bearing. Tapping anywhere in the 56px row forwards focus to the input, which is what a label does natively. An earlier fix stretched the input to fill the box instead; its own background then rode over the field's rounded corners as a square block.

Composed with its moleculethe only field that ships

Variants

None. The CSS declares no .a-field-- modifier. The one composition it takes is m-phone-field, a molecule that shares the same element and hangs its own __prefix parts inside. That molecule has no box rule of its own, so both classes must go on one element or the field is unstyled.

States

Two orthogonal axes. data-state carries idle, error and pending. :focus-within carries focus. They are separate because squeezing focus and validity into one slot made focusing a complete field hide its own result.

Idle, error and pendingfocus is live: click into any of them
idle [data-state="error"] [data-state="pending"]
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Idleno attributethe base coat: glass fill, --color-border-field edge
Focus:focus-withinborder to --color-primary plus a 3px --color-primary-12 ring
Error[data-state="error"]border to --color-error-text, fill to --color-secondary-bg
Pending[data-state="pending"]opacity .85, nothing else
Error in forced colours[data-state="error"]a 2px dashed outline held 2px clear, because the system discards the border colour

Pending stays at .85 rather than fading further. The spinner in the helper and the disabled button already carry the wait. A faded number reads as a frozen form. Error is the only state that is invalid: setFieldState puts aria-invalid="true" on the input for error alone, never for pending, because the number is fine while it is being sent.

Behaviour

Border colour, background, box shadow and opacity all move over --dur-base, so a field turning red reads as the same box changing rather than a new one arriving. There is no transform. Nothing about a field is pressed.

.a-field__input:focus sets outline: none. The system ring is dropped there because the field takes a ring of its own through :focus-within. That ring follows the border and its 16px radius rather than boxing the bare input inside it. a-otp-cell makes the same trade for the same reason.

The shake on a wrong number is not the field's. It comes from [data-shake="true"] in foundations, applied to this element because it is the thing a driver can correct.

Content Guidance

The placeholder is an example, never the name. "Example: 82 123 4567" shows the shape the field wants. Its colour is --color-text-tertiary at weight 500, which is 3.72:1 on the sheet, so nothing a driver needs may live there. The name lives in a-label above the box, where it stays visible once the field is filled.

Accessibility

  • The box is a real <label> wrapping its input, so the whole 56px row is the target.
  • Minimum height is --size-control, 56px, above the --size-target floor of 48px.
  • The border is --color-border-field at 3.2:1 against the field fill, which is the non-text contrast bar.
  • The helper below it is bound with aria-describedby, so the message is read with the field rather than found later.
  • aria-invalid="true" goes on the input for error only. Five paths turn the field red; all five now set it.
  • Forced colours discard every colour cue, so error re-states itself as a dashed outline.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--size-controlminimum height, 56px
--space-control-inlinepadding left and right, 16px
--sp-3gap between a prefix and the input
--glass-card-bgthe fill
--size-border · --color-border-fieldthe edge, 1.5px
--radius-cardthe corner, 16px
--shadow-fieldthe lift off the sheet
--color-primary · --color-primary-12focus border and its 3px ring
--color-error-text · --color-secondary-bgerror
--text-subhead · --weight-semibold · --color-text-primarythe typed value
--color-primarythe caret
--color-text-tertiary · --weight-mediumthe placeholder
--dur-baseevery transition on the box

Markup Contract

<label class="a-field m-phone-field" id="phoneField">
  <span class="m-phone-field__prefix" aria-hidden="true">
    <span class="m-phone-field__flag">🇿🇦</span>
    <span class="m-phone-field__code">+27</span>
  </span>
  <input class="a-field__input" id="phoneInput" type="tel" inputmode="numeric" enterkeyhint="send"
         autocomplete="tel-national" placeholder="Example: 82 123 4567"
         aria-describedby="phoneHelper">
</label>

That is the whole of it as it ships. The element carries both classes or the box is unstyled. The prefix is aria-hidden because a flag emoji plus "+27" read aloud in front of the field's name is noise: the input asks for the national number, which is what autocomplete="tel-national" declares. The surrounding stage sets no spacing on it. The organism owns the vertical rhythm.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It is a block-level flex row, so it fills whatever column holds it, at 320 as at 430. Under [data-text="large"] the value's --text-subhead goes from 17px to 19px. The box grows to hold it, because 56px is a minimum rather than a height.

Related Components

Composed by: m-phone-field, on the same element. Placed by: o-signup-sheet. Named by: a-label. Reported on by: a-helper. Alternative for a code: a-otp-cell.

a-label

The name of a field, sitting above it.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

The only label in the product

Purpose and Usage

Use it to name a field. It is three declarations: block, caption weight 600, secondary ink. That is the whole component. The smallness is deliberate. A label that could be styled per screen is a label that will be styled per screen.

Do not use it as a heading. A section title on the sheet is m-sheet-header__title. Do not use it for the line under a control either: that reports on the field and is a-helper. And do not delete it in favour of a placeholder. A placeholder disappears the moment a driver starts typing, taking the field's name with it.

Anatomy

A single element with no child parts, which is what keeps it an atom. It sets no spacing. The stage that holds it supplies the 8px gap down to its field, which is the "parts of one thing" step in the sheet's rhythm.

Label above its fieldthe gap belongs to the stage, not to either of these

Variants

None. The CSS declares no .a-label-- modifier. There is no on-dark form, because no field in the product sits on the hero.

States

None. The block has no :hover, no :focus-visible, no :disabled and no attribute selector. It is not a control, so it has nothing to be in a state about. A field going red does not change the colour of its name; the error is reported by the field's own border and by the helper below it.

Behaviour

No transition and no script. Its one behaviour is the browser's: for points at the input's id, so tapping the words moves focus into the field. The field is itself a <label>, so between them the name and the box are both targets.

Content Guidance

A noun phrase in sentence case, no colon, no asterisk. "Mobile number". Name the thing being asked for rather than the format it wants: the format goes in the placeholder, the reason goes in the helper. Keep it to two or three words. It wraps rather than truncates, like everything else in the system.

Accessibility

  • It must be a real <label for>. A styled span looks identical to an eye and gives the input no accessible name.
  • Never let a placeholder stand in for it. The name has to survive being filled in.
  • --color-text-secondary is 5.01:1 on the composited sheet, above 4.5:1 for text at 13px.
  • It has no minimum height. The 48px target rule covers interactive controls. This is a name rather than a control.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--text-captionthe size, 13px
--weight-semiboldthe weight, 600, so a small line still reads as a name
--color-text-secondarythe ink, one step under the value it names

Three tokens, no spacing, no colour of its own for any state. That is the entire surface area of this component.

Markup Contract

<label class="a-label" for="phoneInput">Mobile number</label>

One line, one instance in the whole page. It sits directly above a-field inside o-signup-sheet__stage, with no wrapper between them, because the stage's flex gap is what spaces them.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. display: block means it takes the stage's width at every device size the product targets. Under [data-text="large"] --text-caption goes from 13px to 15px, which it follows without a rule.

Related Components

Names: a-field. Placed by: o-signup-sheet. Paired below the field by: a-helper. Not used by m-otp-group, which names itself with role="group" plus aria-label instead.

a-helper

The line under a control that reports on it.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

Neutralwhat the control will do next
We'll send a 6-digit code on WhatsApp. A small amount of mobile data is needed.

Purpose and Usage

One line under one control, saying what that control will do or what just happened to it. It is bound to the control with aria-describedby, so it is the same object to a screen reader as it is to an eye.

Do not use it to carry a whole outcome. When the answer is a dead end or a wait with a way out, that is m-alert, which has a title, a body and room for actions. Do not use it for a rule the driver keeps regardless of what they typed: that is o-signup-sheet__note, which sits quieter and stays put.

Anatomy

A block with two optional children. .a-helper__icon is a glyph injected before the text, nudged 2px down onto the baseline with a --sp-1 gap after it. .a-helper__note is a second block underneath, for a consequence. It declares weight 500 so it cannot inherit a tone's 600, which is what sets it apart from the sentence above. The note exists because "one attempt left" was read as one sentence with "signing in pauses". A consequence is not the error, so it gets its own line.

An a-spinner placed inside is spaced by the helper's own rule, --sp-2 after it, sitting 3px down.

Icon, text, note and spinner
.a-helper__icon + textEnter your mobile number. .a-helper__noteOne attempt left.Use the newest code we sent. Another incorrect code will pause sign-in for 15 minutes. .a-spinner insideChecking your code…This normally takes a few seconds.

Variants

None. The CSS declares no .a-helper-- modifier. Everything that changes about it changes through data-tone, which is a state rather than a variant: the same line under the same control, saying a different thing.

States

Four toneseach glyph is injected by setHelper
no attributeWe'll send a code to this number by SMS. [data-tone="error"]That looks like a landline. Enter a mobile number, like 82 123 4567. [data-tone="warning"]One attempt left. [data-tone="success"]Code confirmed. You're signed in.
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Neutralno attribute--color-text-secondary at the inherited weight
Error[data-tone="error"]--color-error-text, weight 600, plus the alert triangle
Warning[data-tone="warning"]--color-warning, weight 600, plus the clock
Success[data-tone="success"]--color-success, weight 600, plus the tick

There is no busy tone. Waiting is expressed by putting a spinner in the neutral helper, which is why the spinner has a spacing rule here rather than a tone of its own. Tone is never carried by colour alone: the glyph goes in with the text, in one call.

Behaviour

It has no transition. A helper changes because setHelper rewrote it. That function does two things in one place so they cannot drift: it writes the glyph plus the message into the element, then sets or removes data-tone. Passing no tone removes the attribute, so a helper can never keep yesterday's colour under today's words.

The element is role="status", so a rewrite is announced politely without moving focus. Every state that changes what a driver should do next rewrites it.

Content Guidance

One short sentence, ending in a full stop. Say what to do, not what went wrong: "Enter the full mobile number. Example: 82 123 4567." beats "Invalid input". The consequence of a next failure goes in __note, never appended to the first sentence.

The text wraps. Nothing in the system truncates. A helper under the code cells holds &nbsp; when it has nothing to say, so the line keeps its height rather than letting the button jump every time a message clears.

Accessibility

  • It carries role="status", a polite live region. A new message is read without stealing focus from the field.
  • The control above points at it with aria-describedby, so the message is heard with the control rather than found afterwards.
  • Tone is never colour alone. Error, warning and success each inject their own glyph.
  • The glyph is aria-hidden inside the icon span and adds no words.
  • --color-error-text is 4.74:1 on the composited sheet, --color-warning 4.69:1, --color-success 5.09:1, all above 4.5:1 at this size.
  • The reserved &nbsp; carries no words, so a helper holding the line announces nothing.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--text-caption2the text size, 12px
--leading-bodyline height, 1.5, so a wrapped message stays readable
--color-text-secondaryneutral ink, also the note's ink
--color-error-text · --weight-semiboldthe error tone
--color-success · --weight-semiboldthe success tone
--color-warning · --weight-semiboldthe warning tone
--sp-1gap after the icon, gap above the note
--weight-mediumthe note, so it does not inherit a tone's 600
--sp-2gap after a spinner placed inside

No spacing token above it. The helper sets no margin of its own; the stage that holds it places it 8px under its control, which is the "parts of one thing" step.

Markup Contract

<span class="a-helper" id="phoneHelper" role="status"></span>

<span class="a-helper" id="otpHelper" role="status">&nbsp;</span>

Filled by setHelper, which produces this shape:

<span class="a-helper" data-tone="error">
  <span class="a-helper__icon"><svg …></svg></span>
  One attempt left.
  <span class="a-helper__note">Use the newest code we sent.</span>
</span>

The phone helper ships empty. The code helper ships holding one space, because its stage would otherwise jump the first time a message arrived. Both are spans rather than paragraphs, because the live region is replaced whole on every change. Nothing writes tone by hand: go through setHelper or the glyph will not match the colour.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It is a block, so it takes the width of the stage and wraps. Under [data-text="large"] --text-caption2 goes from 12px to 14px, at which point a message that fitted one line at 390 may take two. That costs height, which the stage absorbs, rather than meaning.

Related Components

Reports on: a-field and m-otp-group. Placed by: o-signup-sheet. Child it commonly holds: a-spinner. Escalate to: m-alert when the message needs a title or an action.

a-otp-cell

One square of the six a driver types a code into.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

Six cells in their groupthree typed, three waiting

Purpose and Usage

Use it only for a one-time code. Six squares say how long the code is before a word of instruction is read. Each digit lands in its own box, so a driver can see at a glance how far they have got.

Do not use it for a PIN, a card number or anything a person knows by heart. Those belong in a-field, which is one box for one value and does not chop a number into pieces. Never ship one on its own: the accessible name of a lone square is "Digit 1 of 6", which is a lie if there is no 6.

Anatomy

A single <input>. It is square by aspect-ratio: 1 / 1 rather than by a matched width and height, so the row can flex and stay square while it does. flex: 1 with min-width: 0 lets it shrink on a narrow frame. max-width: var(--size-otp-cell) stops it growing past 52px on a wide one.

It has no children. The digit is the input's own value, centred at --text-title. The caret is the brand blue. There is no maxlength: the operating system fills a whole code into the first box, and a cap of one would silently throw five digits away.

Variants

None. Everything the cell can look like is a state it was put into, not a version of it somebody chose. Size follows the container and the text ramp.

States

Two axes, because a cell can be filled and wrong at the same time. data-state is what the driver typed. data-tone is what came back from the check. One attribute holds one value, so neither can erase the other.

What the driver typeddata-state
default [data-state="filled"]
What came backdata-tone, mirrored onto every cell by the group
[data-tone="success"] [data-tone="error"] [data-tone="locked"] [aria-disabled="true"]
The whole row, answeredevery cell wears the group's tone
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Emptyno attribute--color-surface-sunken fill, --color-border-field edge at --size-border
Filleddata-state="filled"edge, fill and ink all move to the brand: --color-primary, --color-primary-bg, --color-primary
Focus visible:focus-visiblethe global ring is switched off. The cell's own border goes --color-primary with a 3px --color-primary-12 halo
Correctdata-tone="success"--color-success edge and ink on --color-success-bg
Wrongdata-tone="error"--color-error-text edge and ink on --color-secondary-bg, plus the border thickens to 2.5px
Lockeddata-tone="locked"opacity: .45
Inactivearia-disabled="true"opacity: .55
Focused while wrong[data-tone="error"]:focus-visiblethe halo becomes --color-secondary-12 so focus cannot paint over the error
Focused while correct[data-tone="success"]:focus-visiblethe halo becomes --color-success-20
Forced colours@media (forced-colors: active)border 2px, 3px when filled. The group re-states its tone as an outline: solid for success, dashed for error

Focus lands on the cell's own edge rather than as a ring floating around it. The global :focus-visible is written for controls that draw no edge of their own: a 3px outline held 2px clear plus a 6px halo. Put that around a square that already has a border and the driver is looking at four concentric shapes with a gap between them, which is the sort of thing you notice before you notice the digit you typed. One edge, one halo, concentric by construction because a box-shadow follows the radius.

Error is the one state that changes weight as well as hue. Success and error are the two outcomes a driver most needs to tell apart, so they differ by 1px of border before they differ by colour at all.

Behaviour

Border colour, background and ink cross over --dur-fast. Nothing else moves. The cell does not animate on fill, because six of them filling in sequence would be six animations in under two seconds.

Typing a digit advances focus to the next cell. Backspace in an empty cell steps back. Focus selects the cell's contents, so overtyping replaces rather than appends. A paste or an operating-system autofill arrives as one string in one box. The flow spreads it across the six from wherever it landed. When the sixth digit lands the code submits itself after 240ms. There is no Continue button under it: six digits is a complete instruction.

While the code is being checked the cells go readOnly with aria-disabled="true", never disabled. Disabling the focused cell drops focus to the body in the middle of the task.

A wrong code shakes the group, not the cell. The shake belongs to the thing the driver can correct as a whole.

Content Guidance

One digit, never a letter. Input is stripped to \d on the way in, so a keyboard that offers letters cannot poison the value. No placeholder: an empty box in a row of six already says what it wants.

Accessibility

  • Each cell is a real <input> with aria-label="Digit N of 6", so a screen reader can say where in the code the caret is.
  • inputmode="numeric" on every cell brings the number pad up on a phone.
  • autocomplete="one-time-code" on the first cell only. The other five are off, which is what stops the platform offering the same code six times.
  • The 52px square clears the --size-target floor of 48px on its own, without padding.
  • Locked is readonly plus aria-disabled. The cell stays in the focus order and stays announced, so a driver waiting on a check is not thrown out of the field.
  • Forced colours discard every background and border colour, which is every cue this component has. Success and error re-state themselves structurally as a solid or dashed outline on the group.
  • The group is labelled "6-digit code" and points at the helper line with aria-describedby, so the outcome is heard as well as seen.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--size-otp-cellthe maximum width, 52px, which the square then matches in height
--text-title · --weight-boldthe digit, 22px
--color-text-primarythe digit before an outcome
--color-surface-sunkenthe empty fill
--size-border · --color-border-fieldthe resting edge, 1.5px at 3.2:1 against the fill
--radius-card-smthe corner, 12px
--color-primary · --color-primary-bgfilled
--color-primary-12the focus halo
--color-success · --color-success-bg · --color-success-20the correct tone and its focus halo
--color-error-text · --color-secondary-bg · --color-secondary-12the wrong tone and its focus halo
--dur-fastthe colour transitions, 0.12s

--color-error-text rather than --color-secondary for the ink. --color-secondary is the Engen red in the logo, so it may not be darkened to satisfy a text contrast rule. Error text gets its own darker value at 4.74:1 instead. The background stays --color-secondary-bg, which is the brand red's own tint. The 2.5px error border is a literal, not a value the scale owns.

Markup Contract

The cells do not exist in index.html. buildOtp creates six of them into an empty m-otp-group, which is what the page ships: 

<div class="m-otp-group" id="otpGroup" role="group" aria-label="6-digit code"
     aria-describedby="otpHelper"></div>

Each cell is built like this, with i running 0 to 5:

<input class="a-otp-cell" type="text" inputmode="numeric"
       autocomplete="one-time-code" aria-label="Digit 1 of 6">

<input class="a-otp-cell" type="text" inputmode="numeric"
       autocomplete="off" aria-label="Digit 2 of 6">

The group owns no descendant selector outside forced colours. It keeps its own data-tone so a whole-row selector can still ask about it, then mirrors the same value onto each cell. The molecule propagates the tone. The cell decides what that tone looks like.

Responsive Behaviour

The one atom in the system whose size token moves with the text ramp. [data-text="large"] re-points --size-otp-cell from 52px to 60px, because --text-title goes 22 to 24 and the square has to keep containing its glyph.

Below that ceiling the cell is elastic. flex: 1 and min-width: 0 let six of them plus five --sp-2 gaps fit a narrow frame, shrinking together and staying square.

Related Components

Parent: m-otp-group, which is the only place it may appear. Partner below it: a-helper, which carries the outcome in words. Alternative for a single value: a-field.

a-icon-wrap

The plate an icon stands on, sized for a list row or for the top of a screen.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

The row plate40px, brand tint, one icon centred

Purpose and Usage

Use it when an icon needs a ground of its own. A bare glyph on a card competes with the text beside it. The plate gives the glyph a shape at a fixed size, so every row in a list lines up on the same left edge whatever icon it holds.

Do not make it pressable. It draws no focus ring, no hover, no press. If the icon is the control, the control is a-btn with the plate inside it. Do not use it to say something the words do not already say: it is aria-hidden in every place the product uses it, so a tone it carries alone is a tone a screen reader never hears.

Anatomy

One square element. It centres its content on both axes. It refuses to shrink in a flex row. It draws a fill rather than an outline. The tone tints already lift the plate off the surface. A ring on top of them read as a border around the icon instead of a plate behind it.

--mark adds one pseudo-element. It is a radial gradient held inset: -34px, so the light reaches 34px past the plate on every side and fades to nothing at its own edge. The gradient is painted in currentColor, which is what lets one rule per tone do the whole job. That clearance is real space: o-status-page adds --sp-3 above and below the mark so the halo fades in air rather than grazing the card corner.

Plate beside mark40px beside 88px
