.a-icon-wrap .a-icon-wrap--lg.a-icon-wrap--mark

Variants

Four. They pick a size or a ground, never both.

On a light surface
.a-icon-wrap .a-icon-wrap--bare .a-icon-wrap--lg
On the hero
.a-icon-wrap--onDark
The markthe plate that leads a screen
.a-icon-wrap--lg.a-icon-wrap--mark
VariantWhat it changes
--barebackground to --color-surface. No tint. The plate a row uses when what it holds is not available, so the ground shows through instead of the brand's own light
--lg--size-stage, 64px. The corner becomes --radius-circle
--onDarkbackground to --color-accent-on-dark-fill, a 15% wash of the accent
--mark--size-stage-lg, 88px, plus position: relative and the halo. It also forces its svg to --size-glyph, 38px, whatever size the icon builder asked for

--mark is declared after --lg in the file, so the pair that the product actually ships renders at 88px with the circular corner it took from --lg. On its own --mark would keep the small card corner, which is not a shape the product uses. Ship the two together.

States

No hover, no focus, no press, no disabled. It is not a control. What it has is tone, which the plate reads one way and the mark reads another.

Tone on the platethe fill changes
no tone [data-tone="error"] [data-tone="warning"] [data-tone="success"]
Tone on the markthe halo changes
--mark[data-tone="warning"] --mark[data-tone="success"] --mark[data-tone="info"]
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Defaultno attribute--color-primary-bg, the brand tint
Errordata-tone="error"fill to --color-secondary-bg
Warningdata-tone="warning"fill to --color-warning-bg
Successdata-tone="success"fill to --color-success-bg
Error halo--mark[data-tone="error"]color to --color-secondary, which the halo picks up through currentColor
Warning halo--mark[data-tone="warning"]color to --color-warning
Success halo--mark[data-tone="success"]color to --color-success
Info halo--mark[data-tone="info"]color to --color-primary
Arrivaldata-motion="pop"pop .5s var(--ease-spring) both, a scale from 0.5 with a fade

Two attributes with one name doing two jobs, on purpose. On the plate a tone is a fill. On the mark it is light. The mark keeps the plate's fill underneath, so --mark[data-tone="warning"] is an amber disc inside an amber glow.

There is no [data-tone="info"] fill rule. Info keeps the default --color-primary-bg, which is already the blue the info halo wants. An offer is not an outcome, so the biometrics ask rings in the brand's own blue rather than in red or green.

Behaviour

Nothing responds to a pointer. The one motion it owns is pop, requested by an attribute rather than applied by default, so a plate that is already on screen does not jump when a tone changes. Reduced motion collapses it to a still frame, whether that comes from the operating system or from the demo toggle.

The halo is pointer-events: none, so the 34px of light around a mark never eats a tap meant for something under it.

Content Guidance

It holds one icon. No text, no badge, no second glyph. The icons come from iconMarkup, which draws 24-viewBox strokes and picks its own stroke width from the size: 2.25 at 16px and under, 2 up to 21px, 1.75 above that. Ask for 20px inside a plate and 28px inside a mark, which the mark then scales to 38.

Accessibility

  • Every use in the product carries aria-hidden="true". It is decoration with a job, never information.
  • The tone it shows is always also in the words beside it. A red plate over a sentence that does not say what is wrong is a failure, not a shortcut.
  • It is a span. Do not give it a role or a tabindex: it has no keyboard behaviour to justify either.
  • Under forced colours the fills are discarded. Nothing is lost, because nothing was carried by the fill alone.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--size-platethe base square, 40px
--size-stage--lg, 64px
--size-stage-lg--mark, 88px
--size-glyphthe icon inside a mark, 38px
--radius-card-smthe corner, 12px
--radius-circlethe corner under --lg and on the halo
--color-primary-bgthe default fill
--color-surface--bare
--color-accent-on-dark-fill--onDark
--color-secondary-bg · --color-warning-bg · --color-success-bgthe three tone fills
--color-secondary · --color-warning · --color-success · --color-primarythe four halo colours
--ease-springthe pop curve

The halo's inset: -34px and its opacity: 0.20 are literals. Neither is a spacing step or an opacity step, because the reach of a light is not a gap between two objects.

Markup Contract

<span class="a-icon-wrap a-icon-wrap--lg a-icon-wrap--mark o-status-page__icon"
      id="statusIcon" data-tone="warning" aria-hidden="true"></span>

<span class="a-icon-wrap a-icon-wrap--onDark">
  <svg width="20" height="20" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"
       stroke="var(--color-accent-on-dark)" stroke-width="2"
       stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true">
    <circle cx="12" cy="12" r="10"/><path d="m9 12 2 2 4-4"/>
  </svg>
</span>

The first is empty in the source. The status screen fills it at runtime with iconMarkup(name, colour, 28), so one element serves every outcome. The second is written out, because a static row's icon never changes.

A screen may add its own class alongside for position, as o-status-page__icon does with margin-inline: auto. It must not set the plate's size or its fill from there.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. Every size it uses is a fixed token which large text mode does not re-point, so a plate holds 40, 64 or 88 while the words beside it grow. That is deliberate: the icon is not being read, the sentence is.

It refuses to shrink in a flex row, so a long line of text wraps rather than squeezing the plate out of square.

Related Components

Parents that place it: o-status-page and o-outcome-panel. Partner in tone: a-helper, which carries in words what the plate carries in colour. Not an alternative to a-btn: if it can be pressed, it is a button holding a plate.

a-spinner

The turning ring that says the product is waiting on something.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

Inside the button that is waitingthe word does the telling, the ring does the reassuring

Purpose and Usage

Use it beside a word that says what is being waited for. It goes in a primary button while a request is out, or on a helper line while a code is being checked. It is the product's only indeterminate wait indicator.

Never ship it alone. A ring with no words is a hang to whoever is holding the phone, and under reduced motion it is a still ring with no words, which is worse. If there is nothing to say, there is nothing to spin about. Do not use it for a wait with a known length: a countdown is a number, not a ring.

Anatomy

A class on an <svg>. The SVG holds two shapes: a full circle for the track and a quarter arc on top of it for the moving part. The gap between the two is what reads as motion once the whole element turns.

The class contributes two declarations. It spins the element and it refuses to shrink in a flex row, so a long label cannot squeeze the ring into an ellipse. Everything else about the spinner is in the SVG: its size, its stroke, its two colours.

The markup is built by spinnerMarkup(size, track, arc). Size drives the radius and the centre, so one function draws the same ring at any diameter. Track and arc default to --color-on-dark-line and --color-text-on-dark, which are the colours for a filled button.

Variants

None. There is one class and no modifier. Size and colour are arguments to the builder, not versions of the component, so a new pairing costs a call rather than a rule.

States

None. The class has no state selector: no hover, no focus, no disabled, no data attribute. A spinner is present or it is not. Its presence is the state. What changes it is the environment.

The two pairings the product ships22px on a filled button, 14px on a helper line
Checking your code…
ConditionHow it is carriedWhat changes
Turningthe class alonespin .7s linear infinite, a full rotation
Reduced motion, from the system@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)the blanket foundations rule cuts every animation to 0.001ms and one iteration. The ring freezes on its first frame
Reduced motion, from the demo toggle[data-motion="reduced"] on the rootthe same freeze, so the demo shows what a driver with the setting on would see
On a helper line.a-helper .a-spinnervertical-align: -3px and margin-right: var(--sp-2)

That last row is the one rule anywhere that reaches the spinner from outside itself. It sits in the a-helper block. It sets placement rather than appearance: a spinner dropped into a run of text needs a baseline nudge that only the line it sits on knows about. Nothing sets its colour, its size or its animation from outside.

Freezing is the reason the rule about words is a rule. Under reduced motion the ring stops carrying anything at all, so whatever the spinner was saying has to already be in the sentence beside it.

Behaviour

One full turn every 0.7 seconds, linear, forever. Linear because an eased rotation reads as a stutter when it loops.

0.7s is a literal, not a token. No --dur value comes close: the scale runs 0.12 to 0.42 and every one of those is a transition between two states. A spin has no second state.

Where it appears, it appears in place. The primary button swaps its label for a spinner plus a new label rather than emptying out, so the button keeps its height and the layout does not move under a thumb that is still on it. The same holds on the code screen: the controls stay where they are and go quiet, because verifying is a moment rather than a destination.

Content Guidance

The spinner has no text of its own. The words beside it do the work, so write them as a present participle naming the task: "Sending code…", "Checking your code…", "Waiting for Face ID…". Say what is happening, not that something is happening. "Loading…" is not one of these.

Accessibility

  • Always aria-hidden="true". It is a picture of a wait, not the announcement of one.
  • The wait is announced by the text it sits beside: a button label a screen reader re-reads, or an a-helper carrying role="status".
  • Reduced motion freezes it. Because the words carry the meaning, nothing is lost when it stops.
  • It is fill="none" with stroke colours passed in, so under forced colours it renders as an outline rather than disappearing into a filled shape.
  • A busy button carries both aria-disabled="true" and the real disabled attribute, set together by setBtn. The spinner is decoration on top of that, never the thing that makes the control inactive.

Tokens

The block itself uses no tokens. Its two declarations are a keyframe name with a literal duration and a flex property. Everything a token could set here is set on the SVG instead, or by the one placement rule in a-helper.

TokenWhere it is appliedUsed for
--color-on-dark-linethe SVG's strokethe track's default, an 18% white which is a step on the on-dark ramp rather than a hand-written alpha
--color-text-on-darkthe SVG's strokethe arc's default, for a filled button
--color-borderpassed as trackthe track on a helper line
--color-text-secondarypassed as arcthe arc on a helper line, matching the ink it sits in
--sp-2.a-helper .a-spinnerthe gap to the words on a helper line

Inside a button no gap token is named, because a-btn already carries a --sp-2 gap between its children. The spinner needs no rule to sit correctly there.

Markup Contract

The SVG that spinnerMarkup() produces at its defaults:

<svg class="a-spinner" width="22" height="22" viewBox="0 0 22 22"
     fill="none" aria-hidden="true">
  <circle cx="11" cy="11" r="8"
          stroke="var(--color-on-dark-line)" stroke-width="2.5"/>
  <path d="M11 3a8 8 0 0 1 8 8"
        stroke="var(--color-text-on-dark)" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>

The two calls the product makes:

el.phoneCta.innerHTML = spinnerMarkup() + '<span>Sending code…</span>';

setHelper(el.otpHelper,
  spinnerMarkup(14, 'var(--color-border)', 'var(--color-text-secondary)') + '…');

Hand-written instances exist where the surrounding markup is static rather than built, as on the fuelling screen in the app file. They carry the same class, the same two shapes, the same fill="none". Only the radius and the stroke width change with the diameter.

Responsive Behaviour

None. Its size is an attribute on the SVG, so it does not follow the text ramp and does not change under [data-text="large"]. That is right for a ring: it is not being read.

It will not shrink in a flex row, so on a narrow frame the label beside it wraps instead. The ring stays round.

Related Components

Parents that hold it: a-btn while a request is out, a-helper while a code is being checked. Partner it must always have: the words beside it. Not an alternative to a-progress, which counts steps in a journey rather than reporting a wait.

a-progress

The line that says which step of the journey a driver is on.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

The step counter11px uppercase, above the stage

Step 1 of 2

Purpose and Usage

Use it at the top of a stage in a multi-step sheet, to answer "how much more of this is there" before a driver has to ask. It is orientation, not a control: it never moves, never fills and cannot be pressed.

Do not use it as a loading bar. There is no track, no fill and no percentage. It is one short line of text. Do not put it on a screen that is an outcome rather than a step. An outcome is not a step. Neither is a question.

Anatomy

A paragraph with one <span> inside it. The span carries no class and no rule of its own. It exists so a script can write to a textContent without touching the paragraph's other children, of which there are none. A group of one is not a group, which is why this is filed as an atom and not a molecule: it bonds nothing.

The paragraph is display: flex. That looks odd on a line of text. It is deliberate. The display has to live in the stylesheet, because an inline display written by a script beats every rule in the file. When it did, the keyboard rule below asked for display: none and never once won. State is an attribute. The attribute is the one the platform already has.

Variants

None.

States

One, carried by the platform's own attribute. There is no hover, no focus, no press: it is not a control.

Both steps, then gonethe third is hidden and takes its margin with it

Step 1 of 2

Step 2 of 2

Step 2 of 2

StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Shownno attributedisplay: flex, with --sp-4 under it
Hiddenhiddendisplay: none. The element leaves the flow and the accessibility tree together
Keyboard open.t-device-frame[data-keyboard="open"]display: none. That rule lives in templates.css, not here

The block writes its own [hidden] rule even though foundations already declares [hidden] { display: none !important }. Belt and braces on a component whose whole visible state is its display.

Behaviour

It does not animate. It changes by being rewritten or by being hidden, both from script.

Three rules govern when it goes. The stage hides it on blocked, biometrics and success, because counting to 2 of 2 on a finished journey adds nothing and costs the group the room it wants. The code view hides it again once a code is confirmed, since a confirmed code is the end of the journey even though the stage it ends on is still the code stage. The device frame hides it whenever the keyboard is open: a driver mid-type knows which step they are on, so this is the least useful height on the screen.

That third rule is the first thing the sheet gives up when the keyboard takes the room. The order is set in templates.css: tooling, then orientation, then instruction that has already been acted on, then the loose steps.

Content Guidance

"Step 1 of 2". A count and a total, nothing else. The label is written as 'Step ' + Math.min(step + 1, 2) + ' of 2', so it cannot say 3 of 2 even if a stage is added past the second one.

Two steps is the whole product journey: a number, then a code. Do not name the steps here. The heading under it already says what the step is.

Accessibility

  • A plain <p>. No role, no aria-live: the count is read in document order when the stage is entered, not announced on every change.
  • It is text, not a graphic, so it needs no progressbar role and no aria-valuenow. Do not add them: they would promise a fill that does not exist.
  • hidden removes it from the accessibility tree as well as from the layout, which is the point of using the platform attribute rather than a class.
  • Uppercase is applied with text-transform, so the text stays "Step 1 of 2" for a screen reader rather than being spelled out.
  • --color-text-secondary is 5.01:1 on the composited sheet, which clears AA at this size.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--text-microthe size, 11px
--weight-boldthe weight, 700, which is what keeps 11px legible
--tracking-capsletter spacing, 0.08em, the uppercase step
--color-text-secondarythe ink
--sp-4the space to the stage below, 16px

Markup Contract

<p class="a-progress" id="progress"><span id="progressLabel">Step 1 of 2</span></p>

Two ids because two things are addressed separately: the paragraph is what gets hidden, the span is what gets rewritten. Setting textContent on the paragraph would work today and would delete the span, so the next write would have nowhere to land.

It sits between m-sheet-header and the first stage. It takes no class from the sheet: its own bottom margin is the whole of its spacing contract.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. --text-micro re-points from 11px to 13px under [data-text="large"], so the line grows with the rest of the ramp without a rule here.

The one thing that moves it is the keyboard. That is the template's rule rather than the atom's. The atom only makes sure a rule can win.

Related Components

Parent: o-signup-sheet, which places it above the stage. Above it: m-sheet-header. The template that hides it: t-device-frame.

a-handle

The small grey bar that says a panel can be pulled down.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

The grab bar36 by 5, at true size

The stage is plain rather than checkered on purpose. --color-handle is rgba(0,0,0,0.25), an alpha wash, so a pattern behind it would be read as part of the bar.

Purpose and Usage

Use it at the top of a panel a driver can dismiss with a thumb. It is the platform's own affordance, which is why it needs no label: a bar in that position already means "this comes down".

Do not use it as a divider or an ornament. A bar that promises a drag on something that cannot be dragged is a lie the driver only finds out by pulling. Do not treat the bar as the control either. It is 5px tall. The pressable thing is the region around it, which is o-signup-sheet__grab in o-signup-sheet.

Anatomy

One element with no children. 36px wide, 5px tall, fully rounded, one background colour. The comment above the rule records that 5px is a deliberate hairline thickness rather than a value off the spacing scale.

It sets no margin, no position and no alignment, so the parent decides where it sits. That is what lets the same atom serve two different sheets: in engenxt-onboarding.html the station sheet places it with one rule, .o-stations__sheet .a-handle { align-self: center; flex-shrink: 0; }, while the thickness and the colour stay with the atom.

Variants

None. No modifier class exists. The station-sheet rule above is placement by a parent, not a second look. The comment on that rule says why it is written that way: there used to be a second bar under another name, one pixel thinner in a lighter grey, which was one scale written twice.

States

None. The block has no :hover, no :focus-visible, no :active, no [data-state], no [hidden]. It is not interactive, so it has nothing to respond to. Any press feedback belongs to the region that surrounds it.

Behaviour

The atom does nothing. It never moves, never scales, never fades. It carries no transition at all.

Around it, o-signup-sheet__grab takes a click in index.html and calls closeSheet(). That is a soft dismissal: mid-code, or on a partly typed number, it arms a second tap instead of closing.

Content Guidance

No content. It holds no text and must not be given a label, a title or a tooltip. Naming it would put a control in the accessibility tree that is not there.

Accessibility

  • Decorative. In index.html the wrapper carries aria-hidden="true"; in engenxt-onboarding.html the atom carries it itself.
  • Never focusable. The comment on o-signup-sheet__grab records that it was a button once: it duplicated the close control while sitting ahead of it in the tab order, so it was demoted to pointer affordance only.
  • Its tap target is the region around it. o-signup-sheet__grab sets min-height: var(--size-target), 48px, so a 5px bar is still pressable.
  • Because it is an alpha wash it darkens whatever surface it lands on rather than fighting it, which keeps it visible on a glass sheet without a border.
  • It is never the only way out. The sheet has a close button, the scrim takes a tap, Escape works.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--color-handlethe bar, rgba(0,0,0,0.25)
--radius-pillthe ends, 999px

Two tokens is the whole of it. The 36px width and the 5px height are literals in the rule, deliberately, because they are a shape rather than a step on a scale.

Markup Contract

<!-- index.html: the wrapper is the target, the atom is the mark -->
<div class="o-signup-sheet__grab" id="sheetGrab" aria-hidden="true">
  <span class="a-handle"></span>
</div>

<!-- engenxt-onboarding.html: hidden on the atom, placed by the sheet -->
<span class="a-handle" aria-hidden="true"></span>

An empty span or div, either is fine. A parent may set where it sits. A parent must not set its size or its colour.

Responsive Behaviour

None. It is 36 by 5 in every frame the demo offers, 390 down to 320. It does not grow at the large text setting either, which is right: it is a shape, not type, so scaling it with the type ramp would only make it heavier.

Related Components

Parent: o-signup-sheet holds it in its grab region. Partner in that dismissal: a-scrim, which offers the same soft close from outside the sheet. Compare a-notch, the other fixed-size bar in the system, which is hardware rather than product.

a-toast

One short line on a dark strip, near the thumb, gone in a few seconds.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

Inside a real framet-device-frame at 320, so the 24px insets and the 96px lift are true
Tap again to close. You will need a new code.

Like the scrim, it cannot show itself on a bare canvas. It is position: absolute with left and right at --sp-6 and bottom at 96px, so its width and its height off the floor are both borrowed from the screen it is pinned to. Outside a positioned ancestor it would measure itself against the document. The frame above is the real t-device-frame. The id is dropped here because the specimen has no script to hook.

Purpose and Usage

Use it to confirm something that has already happened, or to warn about the tap just made. It is the seeing half of a message. index.html raises it from one place only, with two possible lines, both saying that a dismissal will cost the driver work.

Never use it for anything the driver has to act on. It is pointer-events: none at all times, it holds no control, it leaves on a timer nobody set. Never let it carry the only copy of a fact. Never stack them either: there is one element with one timer, so a second message overwrites the first rather than queueing behind it.

Anatomy

One element, no children. Its text is written straight onto it with textContent. Centred type, 12px of padding above and below, 16px left and right, a 12px corner, then --shadow-toast under it so it reads as a thing floating over the screen rather than a band welded across it.

It is pinned 24px in from each side, so its width is the frame's width less 48. It sits 96px up from the bottom. That number is a literal in the rule, not a token.

Variants

None. One class, one attribute, one look.

States

Visiblethe other state is an element at opacity 0, which a still page cannot show
Tap again to close. Your number will not be saved.
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Restingno data-stateopacity: 0. Still in the DOM, still pointer-events: none, so it costs nothing
Visible[data-state="visible"]opacity: 1, reached over --dur-base, 0.30s

A transition rather than an animation, which matters: a message replaced mid-fade reverses from wherever it had got to instead of restarting from zero.

Behaviour

toast(msg) writes the text, sets data-state="visible", clears any running timer then sets a new one. One element, one timer, so the newest message wins the strip while the clock starts again from the top.

showStage clears the timer then removes the attribute on every stage change. The comment records why: a toast raised on the way in was outliving the screen it spoke about, sitting over the next stage's primary button.

The dwell differs between the two pages. index.html holds it for 2600ms, which suits the single line it raises. engenxt-onboarding.html scales it, Math.max(2600, msg.length * 45), because two of the messages there are the message: the manager text runs to about 120 characters, which nobody reads in 2.6 seconds.

Under reduced motion the blanket rule in foundations.css cuts the transition to 0.001ms, so it appears instantly. The dwell survives, because it is a timer rather than a transition. The message still stays long enough to read.

Content Guidance

  • One sentence. Two only when the second is the consequence of the first: "Tap again to close. You will need a new code."
  • Say what will happen in the driver's own terms. Both of the messages in index.html are about work being lost.
  • No links, no buttons, no icons. There is nowhere to put them, nothing would be pressable, nothing would be reachable by keyboard.
  • It wraps rather than truncates. A long line costs height. Nothing in this system truncates text.
  • Keep it short anyway. On the onboarding page the dwell grows with the length, which is a fix for a message that is already too long to be a toast.

Accessibility

  • In index.html it is role="status", so the element announces its own text. Polite is right: nothing here demands an interruption.
  • In engenxt-onboarding.html it is aria-hidden="true", with a separate u-visually-hidden announcer beside it that reply(msg) writes to at the same time. Two live regions carrying the same sentence made the phone read every message twice. Two channels, one message: the toast is the seeing half, the announcer is the hearing half.
  • pointer-events: none is on the base rule, never lifted, so it can never eat a tap meant for the screen under it.
  • Contrast is --color-text-on-dark #ffffff on --color-text-primary #0d1e2a, the strongest pair in the palette.
  • It sits at --z-toast, 40, under --z-hardware at 50. m-keyboard shares that 40, so document order decides between them: the toast is written after the keyboard in index.html, which puts the message over the keypad rather than behind it.
  • It never carries the only copy of anything a driver must act on. The comment in engenxt-onboarding.html records what the missing rule cost there: five controls answered a tap by writing to a visually hidden span, so every one of them was silent to anybody looking at the screen.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-6the left and right inset, 24px
--sp-3 · --sp-4the padding, 12px over 16px
--z-toastthe stacking order, 40
--color-text-primarythe ground, #0d1e2a
--color-text-on-darkthe ink, #ffffff
--text-caption · --weight-semibold · --leading-bodythe type, 13px at 600 over 1.5
--radius-card-smthe corner, 12px
--shadow-toastthe lift, 0 4px 20px rgba(0,0,0,0.30)
--dur-basethe opacity transition, 0.30s

bottom: 96px is the one measurement with no token behind it.

Markup Contract

<!-- index.html: the strip is its own live region -->
<div class="a-toast" id="toast" role="status"></div>

<!-- engenxt-onboarding.html: the strip is silent, the span speaks -->
<div class="a-toast" id="toast" aria-hidden="true"></div>
<span class="u-visually-hidden" id="announcer" role="status" aria-live="polite"></span>

It ships empty with no data-state. The script owns both the text and the attribute. It must be a direct child of a positioned ancestor that represents the screen, which in both pages is t-device-frame.

Responsive Behaviour

The strip is fluid across the width. Left and right stay at 24px, so it is the frame's width less 48 at 390, 375, 360 or 320. The 96px lift is fixed, so on the shorter 720px frame it sits proportionally higher up the screen.

At the large text setting --text-caption goes from 13px to 15px, so the strip grows taller. Nothing clips, because the line wraps.

Related Components

Raised by the soft dismissal that a-scrim and a-handle offer on o-signup-sheet. Its hearing half: u-visually-hidden. Shares its layer with m-keyboard. Ground: t-device-frame.

a-scrim

The dark wash that puts the screen behind an open sheet out of reach.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

Inside a real framet-device-frame at 320, the narrowest device the demo offers

The scrim cannot show itself on a bare canvas. It is position: absolute; inset: 0, so with no positioned ancestor it would take the size of the document rather than the size of a screen. The specimen above sits in a real t-device-frame, which is the containing block it has in the product, at the 320 width so it fits this column. It is pinned at data-state="opening", the one state that ends at full opacity.

The notch stays black over it. That is the stacking scale working: m-notch sits at --z-hardware, 50, while the scrim sits at --z-scrim, 9. An app cannot dim a bezel.

Purpose and Usage

Use it under a modal sheet. It does two jobs at once. It darkens what is behind so the sheet reads as the only live thing. It also gives the driver a large target that means "not this".

Do not use it as a loading veil or a general dimmer. o-status-page already covers the blocking case with --color-scrim-deep and a blur, because a blocking status is not something a driver may tap away. If the overlay cannot be dismissed by touching it, this is the wrong atom.

Anatomy

One element, no children, five declarations: position, inset, stacking order, colour then display. There is nothing inside it to lay out, which is exactly why it is filed as an atom rather than as an overlay organism: the comment beside a-progress in the same file makes the same argument, that a group of one is not a group.

Variants

None. One class, one wash. Everything else it does is state.

States

StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Closedno data-statedisplay: none. Gone rather than transparent, so it cannot take a tap it is not entitled to
Present[data-state], any valuedisplay: block. The attribute alone is what makes it exist
Opening[data-state="opening"]scrimIn over --dur-base, 0.30s, forwards. Opacity 0 to 1
Closing[data-state="closing"]scrimOut over --dur-quick, 0.24s, forwards. Opacity 1 to 0

Only opening and closing are ever written. Out is quicker than in, by design: arriving is worth watching, leaving is not.

There is no second specimen for the states. Both are animations, so a still page can only show where they land: opening lands on the wash above, closing lands on nothing.

Behaviour

setSheetShown(state) in index.html is the only writer. It puts the same data-state on the scrim and on the sheet in one call, so the two cannot disagree about whether a sheet is open. Its comment states the rule: state rides data-state, CSS derives display from it, absence is gone.

A click on the scrim calls closeSheet() with no force argument. On a partly typed number, or while a code is live, that does not close. It arms a second tap for 4 seconds and raises an a-toast saying so. Escape calls closeSheet(true), which skips the confirmation.

The attribute comes off when the sheet's own animationend fires, with a 400ms timer behind it as the fallback for an animationend that never arrives. Both paths are idempotent.

Under prefers-reduced-motion, or with data-motion="reduced" on an ancestor, foundations.css cuts every animation to 0.001ms. The scrim then appears at full strength with no travel. That is correct. The fact being communicated is the barrier, not the fade.

Content Guidance

No content. It holds nothing. Never put a close button, a caption or a message on it. A control on a surface whose whole purpose is dismissal would be pressed by accident on the way to dismissing.

Accessibility

  • It is a plain div with a click handler. No role, no tabindex, no name. That is deliberate: a focusable scrim puts a nameless dismissal in the tab order ahead of the sheet's own close button.
  • Because it is pointer-only, the same dismissal must exist by keyboard. It does. Escape closes the sheet from anywhere. The sheet also carries a real close button.
  • It is not what hides the background from assistive technology. setBackgroundInert does that, setting inert plus aria-hidden="true" on the hero. The scrim is the visual half of the same fact.
  • --color-scrim is rgba(0,0,0,0.45), a wash rather than a curtain, so the screen behind stays recognisable as the place the driver came from.
  • It sits one step below the sheet: --z-scrim is 9, --z-sheet is 10. The sheet is always above its own scrim.
  • Closed it is display: none, so it is out of the accessibility tree entirely rather than an invisible tap-eater over the hero.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--color-scrimthe wash, rgba(0,0,0,0.45)
--z-scrimthe stacking order, 9
--dur-basethe fade in, 0.30s
--dur-quickthe fade out, 0.24s

--color-scrim-deep is a sibling token this atom never touches. It belongs to o-status-page, the overlay that blocks rather than dismisses.

Markup Contract

<div class="a-scrim" id="sheetScrim"></div>

<section class="o-signup-sheet" id="sheet" role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-labelledby="sheetTitle">
  …
</section>

It ships with no data-state, so a page that never runs its script shows no scrim. It is a sibling of the sheet, directly inside t-device-frame, which is the positioned ancestor it needs. The id is a script hook. The atom does not appear in engenxt-onboarding.html at all.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. inset: 0 fills whatever positioned ancestor it is given, so it follows the frame at 390, 375, 360 and 320 without a single media query. Move it into a different container and it will fill that instead, which is the one way to misuse it.

Related Components

Partner: o-signup-sheet, which shares its data-state writer. Alternative for a blocking overlay: o-status-page. Raised by the same dismissal: a-toast. Ground: t-device-frame.

a-notch

The black cut-out at the top of the phone, drawn so the design is judged against a real screen.

LevelAtom OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/atoms.css StatusStable

Preview

The iPhone notch126 by 34, rounded on the bottom two corners only

The atom is statically positioned, so it draws itself correctly anywhere. Its place on the screen is not its own: m-notch pins it to the top of the frame.

Purpose and Usage

It is hardware, not product. It exists so a hero, a sheet or a status bar can be reviewed against the space a real handset actually leaves. Without it a reviewer is approving a layout on a screen nobody owns.

Nothing in the product may reach into it. Nothing may take a colour from it either. --color-hardware is #000, with a comment on the token saying it is the physical bezel rather than a brand colour. Do not use it as a decorative shape somewhere else. A black lozenge in the middle of a screen is not a notch, it is a mistake.

Anatomy

One element, no children, one declaration line. 126 by 34, one fill, one radius.

Only the bottom two corners are rounded: 0 0 var(--radius-notch) var(--radius-notch). The top edge is the top of the phone, so the frame's own overflow: hidden and its corner radius finish it. Rounding all four would put a gap of screen above the notch that no handset has.

Variants

None. No modifier class exists. The hole-punch form is a template override keyed off the frame width, not a variant of the atom, so it is documented under Responsive Behaviour.

States

None. The block has no :hover, no :focus-visible, no :active, no [data-state], no [data-tone], no [hidden]. It is a painted rectangle that never changes.

Behaviour

None of its own. It has no transition and no animation. It takes no input: its parent m-notch sets pointer-events: none, so the notch cannot swallow a tap meant for the screen beneath it.

It changes shape once, when the demo switches the frame to the Android width. That is a change of device rather than a state of the component.

Content Guidance

No content. Never put text, an icon or a status inside it. The time and the signal bars belong to m-status-bar, which sits below the notch rather than in it.

Accessibility

  • Decorative hardware. Its wrapper carries aria-hidden="true" in both pages, so nothing in the notch is announced.
  • m-notch sets pointer-events: none, so it is invisible to the pointer as well as to the screen reader.
  • It holds no text, so it has no contrast requirement of its own.
  • It sits at --z-hardware, 50, the top of the stacking scale, above --z-toast at 40 and --z-scrim at 9. A phone's bezel is in front of everything the app can draw, so the model matches the hardware.
  • The forced-colours block in atoms.css does not mention it. Nothing in the product depends on the notch being visible, so it is left to the system.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--color-hardwarethe fill, #000
--radius-notchthe bottom two corners, 22px
--radius-circlethe corner of the hole-punch form, 50%

126 by 34 is written as a literal, as is the 14 by 14 of the hole-punch. They are device measurements. Putting them on the product's size scale would invite a product element to reuse them.

Markup Contract

<div class="m-notch" aria-hidden="true"><div class="a-notch"></div></div>

Identical in index.html at line 87 and in engenxt-onboarding.html at line 4096. It is never used alone. On its own it has no position, so it would sit in the document flow at the top of whatever contains it.

Responsive Behaviour

One real change, driven by the frame rather than by the viewport. In templates.css: 

.t-device-frame[data-width="360"] .a-notch {
  width: 14px; height: 14px; margin-top: 10px;
  border-radius: var(--radius-circle);
}

At 360 the frame is standing in for the most common Android viewport, so the chrome follows the platform. The notch becomes a hole-punch, while the frame's own corner drops to 20px in the rule above it. The comment gives the reason plainly. A 360-wide frame wearing an iPhone notch is a review of a phone that does not exist.

The 390, 375 and 320 frames keep the notch unchanged. The hole-punch cannot be shown on a canvas here, because the rule needs .t-device-frame[data-width="360"] as an ancestor and that frame is 360 by 800.

Related Components

Parent: m-notch, which bonds the notch to a position and adds no appearance. Sibling chrome: m-status-bar and m-keyboard. Ground: t-device-frame, which owns the width the notch answers to.

Molecules

Molecules are focused combinations of atoms working together as one reusable unit.

11 exist. Three of them draw the handset rather than the product: the notch, the status bar and the keyboard are operating-system chrome. They are marked as such.

m-phone-field

The South African dialling code, fixed beside the box where the rest of the number is typed.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The number stage's one inputflat ground, because the fill is translucent

Purpose and Usage

Use it wherever a South African mobile number is taken. The country code is fixed at +27, so the driver types the national number without its leading zero. Every driver in this product is registered by a South African fleet, which is why the code is markup rather than a picker.

Do not use it for a number that could be foreign. There is nothing to change the code to. Do not use it for a landline either: the number stage rejects a landline prefix in script, not in the field.

Do not put m-phone-field on an element of its own. It shares one element with a-field. It has no rules that draw a box. Both classes go on the same tag or the field arrives unstyled.

Anatomy

A <label> carrying a-field m-phone-field. Inside it, __prefix holds __flag then __code, followed by the a-field__input. The prefix is flex-shrink: 0, so it keeps its width whatever the number does.

The divider after the code is the prefix's own border-right: 1px of --color-primary-12, held --sp-3 clear of the code by padding. There is no separate rule for it.

The block selector .m-phone-field declares nothing. The 56px minimum height, the glass fill, the border, the radius and the shadow all come from a-field on the same element. m-phone-field is the namespace its two __ parts hang off.

The prefix on its ownflag, code, divider
🇿🇦 +27

Variants

None.

States

None of its own. The block contains no :hover, no :focus-visible, no :active, no [data-state]. Every state a driver sees is drawn by a-field, which is on the same element, so the previews below are real.

Idle, error, pendingclick the first to see focus
idle .a-field[data-state="error"] .a-field[data-state="pending"]
StateWhere the rule livesWhat changes
Idleno attributethe field's own coat
Focus.a-field:focus-withinborder to --color-primary plus a 3px --color-primary-12 halo. The ring lives on the field so it follows the border
Error.a-field[data-state="error"]border --color-error-text, background --color-secondary-bg. The input takes aria-invalid="true" at the same time
Pending.a-field[data-state="pending"]opacity .85. It is not invalid. The number is fine, it is being sent
Shake[data-shake="true"], foundationsone shakeX over --dur-slow, on the field rather than on the stage

There is no valid state. Nine digits is a complete number, not a recognised one, so only the server can confirm it. A tick here would promise something the client cannot know.

Behaviour

The field is a <label> wrapping its input, so a tap anywhere in the 56px row moves the caret into the number. The prefix is inside that row. An earlier cure stretched the input instead. Its background then rode over the field's rounded corners as a square block.

The input reformats as it is typed, into 82 123 4567, capped at nine digits. A pasted +27, 0027 or leading 0 all land on the same nine. Reformatting rewrites the value, so the caret is restored to the position holding the same digit count.

Border colour, background, box-shadow and opacity move over --dur-base. Those transitions belong to a-field. The prefix has none.

Content Guidance

The placeholder is an example, not an instruction: "Example: 82 123 4567". The visible label is a separate a-label above the field, reading "Mobile number". The prefix is not a label.

__code reads +27 with nothing after it. No brackets, no pipe, no slash. The divider is a border on the prefix.

Accessibility

  • The prefix is aria-hidden="true". Without it the flag emoji joins the input's name, so the field would announce as "South Africa flag +27 Mobile number".
  • The name comes from the a-label above, which carries for="phoneInput". The wrapping label forwards taps but contributes no words of its own.
  • aria-describedby points at the a-helper, which is role="status", so a correction is spoken when it arrives.
  • aria-invalid="true" is set on error only. Pending never sets it: five paths once turned the field red while two of them told a screen reader nothing was wrong.
  • type="tel", inputmode="numeric", autocomplete="tel-national", enterkeyhint="send". The numeric pad is what the keyboard drawing in m-keyboard shows.
  • Focus is drawn on the field's border. .a-field__input:focus removes the input's own outline so there is one edge, not two.
  • Under forced-colors: active the error state grows a dashed outline, because the red border is discarded by the mode.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-2gap between the flag and the code, 8px
--sp-3padding after the code, before the divider, 12px
--color-primary-12the 1px divider
--text-lead · --leading-flushthe flag, 20px on a line of its own height
--text-body · --weight-heavythe country code

Five tokens is the whole block. Everything that draws the box belongs to a-field: --size-control, --space-control-inline, --glass-card-bg, --size-border, --color-border-field, --radius-card, --shadow-field.

Markup Contract

<label class="a-label" for="phoneInput">Mobile number</label>
<label class="a-field m-phone-field" id="phoneField">
  <span class="m-phone-field__prefix" aria-hidden="true">
    <span class="m-phone-field__flag">🇿🇦</span>
    <span class="m-phone-field__code">+27</span>
  </span>
  <input class="a-field__input" id="phoneInput" type="tel" inputmode="numeric" enterkeyhint="send"
         autocomplete="tel-national" placeholder="Example: 82 123 4567"
         aria-describedby="phoneHelper">
</label>
<span class="a-helper" id="phoneHelper" role="status"></span>

The label, the field and the helper are one unit. Splitting them breaks the name or the description. data-state goes on the field, never on the input.

Responsive Behaviour

No media query of its own. Under [data-text="large"] the ramp re-points: --text-lead goes 20 to 22 and --text-body goes 15 to 17, so the prefix grows with the rest of the page.

When it grows, the prefix keeps its width and the input gives up the space. The prefix is flex-shrink: 0; the input is flex: 1 with min-width: 0. That holds at 320 with large text on, which is the tightest case the product targets.

Related Components

Shares its element with a-field. Named by a-label, described by a-helper. Parent: o-signup-sheet. The stage after it collects the code in m-otp-group.

m-otp-group

The row of six code cells, held together so that six inputs read as one field.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

Four digits intype in a cell to see focus land on its own edge

Purpose and Usage

One use: the six-digit sign-in code on the code stage. It exists so a driver sees one thing to fill rather than six things to fill, which is also what the group role tells a screen reader.

Do not reach for it for any other short code. Its label says "6-digit code", its tone values are verification outcomes, its children are wired to auto-submit at six digits. A different code length is a different component, not a modifier on this one.

Do not style the cells from here. The group propagates a tone, each a-otp-cell decides what that tone looks like.

Anatomy

Three declarations, no element parts of its own. It is a flex row with a --sp-2 gap, centred. Its children are six a-otp-cell inputs, which the page builds in script rather than writing in markup.

Centred rather than space-between, which is the one decision in the block. The cells are capped at --size-otp-cell, so space-between handed every spare pixel to the gaps: the declared 6px rendered 7.6px at 390 and about 15.6px at 430, so the row's rhythm changed with the handset, the six boxes stopped reading as one group. Centring makes the gap the token on every width. The cells divide what is left.

The gap is --sp-2, 8px. It is an optical value rather than a rhythm value: what it is tuned for is six squares plus five gaps filling the content width at 320dp, which is the narrowest device the product targets. The source comment beside it still describes the 6px this was before the value moved onto the token. Read the declaration, not the number in the comment.

Variants

None.

States

The three tones the group carriesevery cell paints itself from the tone the group hands it
no attribute
[data-tone="error"]
[data-tone="success"]
[data-tone="locked"]
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes on the group
Defaultno attributenothing. The row is a row
Error[data-tone="error"]nothing here in normal colours. Under forced colours every cell in the group takes a 2px dashed outline
Success[data-tone="success"]nothing here in normal colours. Under forced colours every cell takes a 2px solid outline
Locked[data-tone="locked"]nothing on the group. Each cell drops to 45% opacity
Shake[data-shake="true"]the whole row runs shakeX over --dur-slow. That rule lives in src/css/foundations.css, not in this block

The group holds the attribute so a whole-row selector can still ask what happened, then hands the same value to every cell as data-tone. There is no descendant selector here outside the forced-colours block. That is the point of the split: a molecule that set an atom's border colour would be reaching inside it.

Forced colours discards every background colour along with every border colour, which is every cue the row has. The two rules in @media (forced-colors: active) re-express success plus error as outline style, solid against dashed, so the outcome survives with no colour at all.

Locked carries no tone of its own beyond opacity, because it is not an outcome. It is the row waiting for a server, which is what the busy button beside it is already saying.

Behaviour

The group is passive. Everything below is done by the code stage's script to the cells inside it, listed here because nothing else documents the contract a replacement would have to keep.

EventWhat happens
Buildsix <input> elements, type="text", inputmode="numeric". The first takes autocomplete="one-time-code", the rest take off
Type a digitthe cell takes data-state="filled", focus moves to the next cell, any tone on the row is cleared
Six digitssubmission fires 240ms later, with no button pressed. Any further keystroke inside that window cancels the pending submit
Pastenon-digits are stripped. Six digits replace the row wherever they were dropped, fewer than six fill forward from the cell they landed in
Autofillthe OS drops the whole code into the first box. There is no maxlength anywhere in the row, on purpose: a cap of one silently throws five digits away
Backspaceon an empty cell it steps back one, clears that digit, takes focus. On a filled cell the browser's own behaviour stands
Arrow left, arrow rightmove one cell, no wrap at either end
Entersubmits, from any cell
Focusthe cell selects its own content, so typing over a digit replaces it rather than appending
Lockevery cell goes readonly plus aria-disabled="true", never disabled. Disabling the focused cell drops focus to the body in the middle of the task
Wrong codetone goes to error, the row shakes for 500ms, the attribute is then removed

The shake is skipped when a state is reached by jumping from the demo navigator. A shake answers something the driver just did, so a reviewer stepping down a list of states should not get a flinch per click for errors nobody made.

Content Guidance

The group holds no copy. Its only text is its accessible name, "6-digit code", which names the thing rather than instructing: the six boxes already say what to do.

Everything the driver reads sits outside it. The line under the row is a-helper, which the group points at with aria-describedby. Anything longer than a line is m-alert below that. Do not put a message inside the group. It would land between the cells.

Count the digits in the label. "6-digit code" is checkable against the row in front of the driver, which "Verification code" is not.

Accessibility

  • role="group" with aria-label="6-digit code". Without the role a screen reader announces six unrelated text fields.
  • aria-describedby="otpHelper" ties the helper line to the group, so the status under the row is read as part of it.
  • Every cell names its own position: aria-label="Digit 3 of 6". A driver who tabs into the middle of the row knows where they are.
  • Locked cells keep their tab stop. readonly plus aria-disabled="true" means the row can still be read while it cannot be changed, which is the honest description of waiting for a server.
  • Focus is drawn on the cell's own edge rather than as the global ring, because a 3px outline held 2px clear of a small square that already carries a border reads as a box inside a box. That rule belongs to a-otp-cell.
  • Under forced colours the tones become outline styles, so success against error is still two different things with the palette gone.
  • The shake is motion, never information. Under prefers-reduced-motion it collapses to a still frame, so the error is carried by the tone, the helper, the alert.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-2the gap between cells, 8px. The only token in the block

One token, three declarations. Everything else a reader sees belongs to a-otp-cell, which owns --size-otp-cell, the border, the fill, the tone colours. The shake's --dur-slow plus --sp-1 belong to the foundations rule that draws it.

Markup Contract

What is in the page:

<div class="m-otp-group" id="otpGroup" role="group" aria-label="6-digit code"
     aria-describedby="otpHelper"></div>
<span class="a-helper" id="otpHelper" role="status">&nbsp;</span>

It ships empty. The cells are built fresh on every entry to the code stage, which is how a stale digit cannot survive a trip back to the number stage. One cell, as built: 

<input class="a-otp-cell" type="text" inputmode="numeric"
       autocomplete="one-time-code" aria-label="Digit 1 of 6">

The helper is a sibling, not a child. It carries role="status" so its changes are announced, holding a non-breaking space when it has nothing to say so the row above it does not move when it fills.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. No media query touches it. The row is centred with a fixed gap, so the cells take the width that is left up to their own cap. At 320dp they are under the cap and divide the space, at 430 they sit at 52px each with the row centred in whatever remains.

Under [data-text="large"] the cell cap goes from 52px to 60px, because the square has to keep containing its glyph. The gap does not change, so six cells plus five gaps stay inside the content width.

Related Components

Child: a-otp-cell, six of them, which carry every appearance the row has. Parent: o-signup-sheet places it at the top of the code stage. Siblings on that stage: a-helper under the row, m-alert when a line is not enough, m-resend-row further down.

m-sheet-header

Whatever the sheet is asking right now, with the way out of it held on the right.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The code stage's headerhover the circle to see its wash

Enter your code

We sent a 6-digit code on WhatsApp to +27 82 ••• 567.

Purpose and Usage

Use it at the top of a sheet that asks the driver for something. It names the step in a heading, explains it in one sentence, holds the dismiss control at the far edge. In this product it is the header of every stage of the sign-up sheet.

Do not use it above a panel that carries its own heading. Give it data-heading="none" instead, which strips it back to the close control alone. Two headings on one screen compete. The vaguer one wins the position while the specific one gets pushed down.

Do not put actions in it. The close control is the only button it holds. Anything the driver is meant to press belongs at the bottom of the stage, under the thumb.

Anatomy

Two flex children. The first is an unclassed wrapper holding __title then __sub. The second is __close. space-between puts the words left, the circle right, with --sp-3 as the floor between them.

align-items: flex-start, so the circle sits level with the top of the title rather than centred against a block that may run to two lines. Title to sub is --sp-1, 4px. That bind scales with its type: 4 here, 8 under an 18px outcome heading, 12 under the 42px hero. Below the whole header sits --space-group, 16px, which is one group to the next.

__close is a 36px circle, --size-object square at --radius-circle, with the glyph centred in it. It used to be a 48px box with the mark shoved to its trailing edge so the cross would line up with the title's gutter. It did line up, at the cost of a highlight visibly off-centre from its own mark. The circle is drawn at the size the mark deserves, the mark sits in the middle of it, the circle's own edge holds the gutter.

The 48px a thumb is owed is drawn out from __close::after at inset: 0 -6px -12px -6px: 36 plus 12, taken downward and sideways only. Nothing reaches above the button, because the sheet's scroller clips there and a clipped region is not pressable either.

The close control on its own36px circle, 16px mark, 48px reach below and sideways

Variants

None. One attribute changes its shape, listed under States.

States

Full header, then the heading withdrawn
default

Enter your mobile number

Use the mobile number your company registered for you.

 [data-heading="none"]
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Defaultno attributespace-between, --space-group below
Heading withdrawn[data-heading="none"]justify-content: flex-end, margin-bottom: 0. The close control holds the right edge on its own
Close hovered__close:hoverbackground to --color-hover-wash, filling the circle
Close focused:focus-visible from foundationsthe system ring, never removed here
Close withdrawnhidden on the buttonfoundations removes it. Used where dismissing would be a second opinion about a choice already on screen
Sub withdrawna rule in templates.cssdisplay: none on __sub when the keyboard is open on the number stage

data-heading="none" is the outcome state. The heading wrapper is hidden at the same moment, so the attribute is not doing the hiding: it is repairing the layout the hiding leaves behind, where a lone close control would otherwise still be pushed to a space-between edge with 16px of margin under nothing.

Behaviour

The title is rewritten per stage rather than swapped for a new element. "Enter your mobile number" on the number stage, "Enter your code" on the code stage. One header, new words.

On an outcome three things happen at once: the wrapper is hidden, the attribute goes on, the sheet's aria-labelledby is repointed at the panel's own title. They move together because an explicit aria-labelledby reference is followed into hidden elements: hiding the heading does not retire its text. Without the repoint a paused account announced itself as "Enter your mobile number".

When the keyboard is open on the number stage, __sub is removed by a rule in templates.css. It is third in a stated order of what the sheet gives up for keyboard room: tooling, then orientation, then instruction that has been acted on. The subtitle says which number to use. The driver is now typing it. On the code stage it stays, because there it names the channel plus the masked number, which is what a driver checks against their handset mid-code.

Content Guidance

Title: a verb and its object, sentence case, no full stop. "Enter your code". It names the step rather than the screen.

Sub: one plain sentence. Wrap any value the driver is checking in <b>. The masked number is one token, so __sub b sets white-space: nowrap and it never breaks across two lines. Breaking it turns the thing being checked into two fragments.

The mask uses bullets, not asterisks. An asterisk reads as a footnote marker. A driver comparing this against their handset needs to see a mask.

The close control carries no visible label. Its aria-label is "Close".

Accessibility

  • __title is an <h2> with an id. The dialog's aria-labelledby points at it, so the sheet is named by the question it is asking.
  • __close is a <button type="button"> with aria-label="Close". Its glyph carries no aria-hidden, which changes nothing: the label on the button replaces its contents for naming.
  • The tap target is 48px, reached through ::after rather than by inflating what is drawn. It extends down and sideways, never up, because the scroller clips above it. A target drawn upward would have had an untappable top.
  • __sub is --color-text-secondary, measured at 5.01:1 on the composited sheet.
  • The focus ring comes from foundations. Nothing here removes it, including on the circle that already draws its own hover fill.
  • Hiding the heading does not hide its text from aria-labelledby. Repoint the reference at whatever heading is now on screen.
  • The header is a <header> element inside the dialog, so it is scoped to the sheet rather than announced as the page banner.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-3the gap between the words and the circle, 12px
--space-groupmargin below the header, 16px, one group to the next
--text-title · --weight-heavy · --tracking-snugthe title
--sp-1the bind from title to sub, 4px
--text-caption · --leading-bodythe sub, 13px at 1.5
--color-text-primarythe title, plus the <b> inside the sub
--color-text-secondarythe sub, plus the close glyph's stroke
--weight-boldthe <b> inside the sub
--size-objectthe close circle, 36px square
--radius-circlethe circle
--color-hover-washthe circle's hover fill

The ::after insets are plain pixels rather than tokens. They are arithmetic against --size-object to reach --size-target, not a spacing decision, so putting them on the scale would misfile them.

Markup Contract

<header class="m-sheet-header" id="sheetHeader">
  <div id="sheetHeading">
    <h2 class="m-sheet-header__title" id="sheetTitle">Your number</h2>
    <p class="m-sheet-header__sub" id="sheetSub"></p>
    <p class="d-demoHint" id="demoHint" hidden></p>
  </div>
  <button class="m-sheet-header__close" id="sheetClose" type="button" aria-label="Close">
    <svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="var(--color-text-secondary)"
         stroke-width="2.25" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round">
      <path d="M18 6 6 18"/><path d="m6 6 12 12"/>
    </svg>
  </button>
</header>

The wrapper around the title and sub carries no class on purpose. It exists so space-between has two children rather than three, which is what keeps the circle at the far edge whether the sub is present or not. Do not give it appearance.

d-demoHint is prototype scaffolding. It carries the developer prefix because it never ships. It is hidden by its own rule in the developer layer.

Responsive Behaviour

No media query of its own. Two things move it.

Under [data-text="large"] the ramp re-points: --text-title goes 22 to 24, --text-caption goes 13 to 15. The circle stays 36px, because --size-object is not on the type ramp. A control drawn at the size of its mark does not grow when app text does.

With the keyboard open on the number stage the sub is removed outright, which is a state rather than a breakpoint. It returns when the keyboard closes.

A long title wraps rather than squeezing the circle. __close is flex-shrink: 0 with --sp-3 held clear of it, so the words give up the width. That is the behaviour at 320 with large text on.

Related Components

Parent: o-signup-sheet. Directly under it: a-progress, which the keyboard also withdraws. The reason data-heading="none" exists: o-outcome-panel, which brings its own title. Compare m-brand-lockup, the hero's own heading, which the sheet never shows.

m-alert

A tinted card that names one thing that happened, then offers the actions that answer it.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The code stage, expiredone fact, then the two ways out

This code has expired

It was valid for five minutes. Send a new code to continue.

Purpose and Usage

Use it when the flow has to stop the driver, say what happened in one line then hand them the way out. Every one of these in the product is written from a definition in CODE_ALERTS, HOME_NOTICES or the number stage's own set, so the shape is the same wherever it lands: a tone, an icon, a title, a body, zero or more actions.

Do not use it for a field's own message. A wrong number is a-helper under the field, because the message belongs to the input the driver is about to correct. Do not use it for a failure that ends the session either. That is o-status-page, which takes the whole screen because there is nothing left on it to do.

One alert per host. The hosts are real elements in the markup that sit empty until something needs saying: #numberAlert, #codeAlert, #homeNotice.

Anatomy

A column with a --space-stack-default gap, so the actions sit 12px under the sentence they answer. Inside it, __top is a row: the icon, then a plain span holding the title and the body as one block. That bare span is load bearing. It keeps the row at two children so the icon stays beside the whole message rather than beside its first line.

__icon is pulled up one pixel. That is optical, against the cap height of the title next to it. __body b takes the primary ink, bold, in tabular figures, which is how a masked number or a clock inside the sentence stays readable as a number.

Every part, with a bold fact in the body

We couldn't check your code right now

Your code still works. Wait a moment, then try again. Reference EX-1042.

Variants

One: .m-alert--onDark. It swaps the tinted card for translucent white over a 20px backdrop blur, re-inks the title to --color-text-on-dark and the body to --color-on-dark-mid. It is the only form allowed on the hero, because the light tints have no contrast there.

On the hero ground
.m-alert--onDark

You're offline

Connect to Wi-Fi or mobile data to continue.

Note what --onDark does not do. It never touches the tone. The data-tone attribute stays on the element so the definition that wrote it does not have to know which surface it landed on, but every tone rule is scoped away from the modifier.

States

The four tonesno attribute renders as info, because the base already carries that coat
[data-tone="info"]

The WhatsApp code is taking longer than usual

Keep waiting, or send a new code by SMS. Sending a new code will replace the WhatsApp code.
[data-tone="warning"]

You cannot request another code yet

The newest code we sent still works. Enter it now.
[data-tone="error"]

We couldn't check your code right now

Your code still works. Wait a moment, then try again.
[data-tone="success"]

Code sent by SMS

Check your messages and enter the newest 6-digit code.
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Infono attribute, or [data-tone="info"]--color-primary-bg on --color-primary-12. The base rule already sets both, so the attribute restates rather than adds
Error[data-tone="error"]--color-secondary-bg on --color-secondary-25
Warning[data-tone="warning"]--color-warning-bg on --color-warning-25
Success[data-tone="success"]--color-success-bg on --color-success-20
On the hero.m-alert--onDark--color-on-dark-fill, --color-on-dark-line, a 20px backdrop blur, white title, --color-on-dark-mid body

There is no hover, no focus, no pressed, no disabled. It is a card, not a control. The controls inside it carry those.

Every tone rule is written .m-alert:not(.m-alert--onDark)[data-tone="…"]. That guard is not decoration. An unguarded tone selector is a class plus an attribute, which outweighs the modifier's single class, so it wins on the hero as well: white title on a pale tint. That is how both home notices once rendered as blank boxes. Copy the guard when you add a tone.

Behaviour

No animation of its own, no transition. It has none because it does not move: it is written into an empty host, the host is unhidden, the alert is there. Removal empties the host's innerHTML then hides it, so nothing lingers in the accessibility tree between states.

Appearing is announced rather than shown. renderAlert pushes the title plus the body text into the page's live region, so a driver who is not looking at the screen hears the same sentence a driver who is looking reads.

A definition may grow an extra action after the same failure repeats. Two failed server checks add a support link; one refused resend adds a link to the manager. The alert renders whatever it is handed, so the escalation costs the component nothing.

One alert can act without being pressed. When the connection returns, an offline card carrying data-act="retrySend" or data-act="retryVerify" runs that action itself, because a driver who walks back into signal should not have to work out that the screen in front of them is now stale.

Content Guidance

The title states the fact from the driver's side: "This code has expired", not "Error 4004". No punctuation at the end of it.

The body carries what still works before it carries what to do. "Your code and attempts are safe. We'll check it when you reconnect." A driver whose sign-in just failed is counting what they have lost, so the sentence answers that first.

Bold inside the body is for a fact the driver will repeat out loud or check against their handset: a masked number, a reference, a time. Bold takes tabular figures, so a number that ticks does not shuffle its own width.

Actions name the act, never the mechanism. "Send a new code by SMS" beats "Retry". When an alert can offer nothing useful it offers nothing: the resend limit ships with an empty action list, because support cannot lift a security limit. A button that reaches the wrong desk costs a driver the trip.

Accessibility

  • The alert itself has no role. Its host does the announcing: #homeNotice carries role="status", so the notice is read when it arrives.
  • The sheet's two hosts carry tabindex="-1", which makes them focusable by script. Nothing moves focus there today. The live region is what carries the message.
  • When a number alert opens, the phone input's aria-describedby is repointed to #numberAlert, so the message is read as part of the field rather than as a separate thing on the page.
  • The icon is always aria-hidden="true". It repeats the tone the words already carry.
  • Colour is never the only cue. The title says what happened, in words, in every tone.
  • Actions inside are real <button> or real <a> elements, so keyboard order is the browser's.

--sp-2gap between stacked actions

Markup Contract
