      <p class="m-alert__title">This code has expired</p>
      <p class="m-alert__body">It was valid for five minutes. Send a new code to continue.</p>
    </span>
  </div>
  <div class="m-alert__actions">
    <button type="button" class="a-btn a-btn--sm a-btn--primary" data-act="resendNow">Send a new code</button>
  </div>
</div>

The action block is omitted entirely when there are no actions. An empty __actions would still spend a 12px gap on nothing.

One alert in the page is written by hand rather than by a definition: the privacy primer on the number stage. It carries data-tone="info" plus its own tabindex="-1", has no __top row, holds a m-privacy list where the body would sit. 

<div class="m-alert" data-tone="info" tabindex="-1">
  <p class="m-alert__title">About your information</p>
  <div class="m-privacy">…</div>
</div>

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It fills the host it is written into, which is the sheet's content column or the hero's notice slot.

It does answer the driver's text size. Title and body are both on the ramp, so under [data-text="large"] the title goes from 14px to 16px, the body from 12px to 14px, the card grows to hold them. Nothing truncates.

Related Components

Parents: o-signup-sheet hosts it on the number stage as well as the code stage. o-auth-hero hosts the --onDark form. Children: a-btn in --sm, sometimes m-privacy. Alternatives: a-helper for a field's own message, o-status-page when the whole screen has to stop.

m-privacy

A short stack of facts, one icon and one line each, for a reader who scans rather than reads.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The POPIA primershown inside the m-alert that carries its box

About your information

We collect your number. Nothing else.
It signs you in. And runs your points.
Your company registered it. Ask your manager to change it.
We do not sell it.

Purpose and Usage

Use it to state a handful of related facts that a reader must take in at a glance. In the sign-up sheet it carries what POPIA s18 requires: what is collected, why, who set it, what does not happen. Section 18 asks that a person is told before the number is taken, in words they can follow.

It replaced a paragraph. A paragraph made a reader hold five sentences at once. A row asks for one glance each. The audience reads English as a second language on a phone at a pump.

Do not use it for a list of actions. Nothing in it is pressable. Do not use it for more than about five rows: past that the glance becomes a read again, which is the thing it was built to avoid. It has no box of its own, so give it one by putting it inside an m-alert.

Anatomy

A flex column with --sp-2h between rows and --sp-2 above the first. Each __row is __icon followed by a single <span> holding the sentence.

The row aligns to flex-start, so the icon holds the first line when the text wraps under itself. The icon carries 1px of margin-top as an optical correction against the cap height. m-alert__icon makes the same kind of correction in the other direction.

Inside the sentence, <b> is the headline of a few words in --color-text-primary. What follows completes it in the row's own secondary ink. Two weights, one line, no second type size.

One rowicon, headline, completion
We collect your number. Nothing else.

Variants

None.

States

None. The block has no :hover, no :focus-visible, no :active, no [data-state], no [data-tone]. It is four static rows.

The showing and hiding happens one level up. #privacyCard carries the hidden attribute. Foundations turns that into display: none !important. A second foundations rule, #privacyCard:not([hidden]), gives the opened card margin-top: var(--sp-2), so it sits one base step below the sentence that offered it.

Behaviour

Static. It does nothing on its own. The control that reveals it is an a-link in the note above, o-signup-sheet__noteMore, which flips aria-expanded between false and true as it unhides the card.

On open, the card's whole text is flattened to one string and announced through the live region. A screen reader hears the four facts rather than only the word "expanded".

Content Guidance

One bold headline of a few words, then one plain sentence. Both short. The four in the product are the model:

HeadlineCompletionThe fact it settles
We collect your number.Nothing else.what is collected
It signs you in.And runs your points.why
Your company registered it.Ask your manager to change it.who set it, plus the way to correct it
We do not sell it.nonewhat does not happen

Present tense, no idiom, no legal register. Write "We do not sell it", not "Your data is not shared with third parties". A row does not need a completion: the last one has none because the headline is the whole fact.

Accessibility

  • Every __icon is aria-hidden="true". Each one repeats a word already in the row beside it, so announcing it would double the sentence.
  • The rows are plain <div>s with no list role. A screen reader reads four sentences in order, which is what they are.
  • The body ink is --color-text-secondary, measured at 5.01:1 on the composited sheet. The <b> lifts to --color-text-primary, which is darker still.
  • Nothing here is focusable. The disclosure that opens it holds the focus, the aria-expanded and the announcement.
  • The rows run at --text-caption2, 12px. They grow to 14px under [data-text="large"], because they read a token rather than a fixed size.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-2hgap between rows, plus gap from icon to text, 10px
--sp-2margin above the first row, 8px
--text-caption2 · --leading-bodythe row's text, 12px at 1.5
--color-text-secondarythe completion half of each line
--color-text-primary · --weight-boldthe headline inside <b>
--color-primarythe icon stroke, through currentColor

The icon's size is an attribute on the <svg>, not a token. 18px is the mark size the row was drawn around.

Markup Contract

<div class="m-privacy">
  <div class="m-privacy__row">
    <span class="m-privacy__icon"><svg width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none"
      stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"
      aria-hidden="true"><rect width="14" height="20" x="5" y="2" rx="2" ry="2"/><path d="M12 18h.01"/></svg></span>
    <span><b>We collect your number.</b> Nothing else.</span>
  </div>
  <!-- three more rows -->
</div>

The text half must be a single element. Two siblings there would take two gaps of --sp-2h from the flex row, which puts the completion a full step away from the headline it completes.

In the sheet it ships wrapped, which is where its box comes from:

<div id="privacyCard" hidden>
  <div class="m-alert" data-tone="info" tabindex="-1">
    <p class="m-alert__title">About your information</p>
    <div class="m-privacy">…</div>
  </div>
</div>

Responsive Behaviour

No media query of its own. Under [data-text="large"] --text-caption2 goes 12 to 14, so every row grows with the page while the 18px icons stay put. The gap between icon and text does not change.

At 320 with large text on, the longest row wraps to three lines. The icon stays on the first because the row aligns to flex-start, so the column of marks holds down the left edge however unevenly the sentences run.

Related Components

Usual parent: m-alert, which supplies the tinted box and the title above the rows. Opened by an a-link in the note. Placed by o-signup-sheet. Compare m-figure, which is the same key-and-value idea for numbers.

m-resend-row

Two quiet text controls held on the same line, one at each content edge.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The two ways out of the code stageneither of them is the task

Purpose and Usage

Use it for a pair of secondary text controls that sit at the same level. On the code stage those are the two ways out: switch the channel, or go back for a different number. The task is the six digits above them, so both stay quiet.

Do not put a a-btn in it. A filled button in a quiet row makes one of the two exits look like the task. The primary action stands on its own above the row.

Do not use it for a control that is always alone. It exists to hold two and to balance them. When one is withdrawn at runtime it has an attribute for that, but a permanently single control belongs on its own line.

Anatomy

One element with two children. display: flex, justify-content: space-between, --sp-2 as both the column gap and the row-gap, flex-wrap: wrap. That is the whole rule.

The children are a-link a-link--quiet a-link--flush. Flush is what lands both labels on the same content edge the OTP cells and the CTA already hold. It is a modifier on the link rather than a rule in the row, because the inline padding belongs to the link, so the link is the thing that cancels it. A molecule reaching in to unset a child's padding is the Composition law broken.

Its top margin is not its own either. The organism sets it: .o-signup-sheet__stage > .m-resend-row { margin-top: var(--sp-2) }. Position belongs to the parent.

Variants

None.

States

Two children, one child, both busy
default
[data-single="true"]
children disabled
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Defaultno attributespace-between, one label at each edge
Single[data-single="true"]justify-content: center. Set in script whenever one of the two is withdrawn
Withdrawnhidden on the rowfoundations removes it with display: none !important, so the stage does not keep its gap
Busydisabled on each childnothing on the row. The links take --color-text-tertiary at --weight-medium from a-link:disabled

data-single is not decoration. With space-between a surviving lone child sits hard against the left edge, in the position it only held because a partner balanced it. Centring it says the pair is now one.

Behaviour

The row wraps rather than crushing its labels. At 125% text the pair needs more width than 320dp gives, so the second label drops to its own line with --sp-2 of row-gap under the first. Nothing in this system truncates text.

While the code is being checked, both children are disabled in place rather than removed. Verifying is a moment, not a destination, so the controls go quiet instead of disappearing and shifting everything under them. The script remembers which of the two was already off, so re-enabling does not wake a control that was meant to stay asleep.

Hiding one child leaves no hole. [hidden] is display: none !important in foundations, so a hidden child is out of the flex row rather than an invisible item still claiming its gap.

Content Guidance

Two short labels, each naming what happens next. "Send code by SMS". "Use a different registered number". The first flips to "Send on WhatsApp" when SMS is the channel already in use, so the label always offers the other one.

Do not write "Cancel" or "Back" here. Both are directions rather than outcomes. A driver at a pump is choosing an outcome. Keep each label to about five words: longer ones wrap the row onto two lines, which is correct behaviour that still costs height on the stage where height is tightest.

Accessibility

  • Both children are real <button type="button"> elements. They act on this screen, so they are buttons rather than links.
  • Each keeps --size-target, 48px, as its minimum height. a-link--flush drops only the inline padding; the vertical padding stays, because the target depends on it.
  • The row has no role of its own. It is layout. A screen reader reads the two buttons in source order, which is the order they appear in.
  • a-link--quiet differentiates upward from disabled rather than sharing its grey, so a live control here never reads as a dead one.
  • The focus ring comes from foundations and is not removed. A wrapped row focuses in the same order it reads.
  • The row is the element script hides, not the individual pair. Hiding the container keeps the two buttons out of the tab order together.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-2the gap between the two labels, 8px
--sp-2the row-gap once they wrap, so the two axes match

One token, twice. There is no colour, no type, no border here. Everything visible belongs to a-link. The margin-top: var(--sp-2) above the row is spent by the organism.

Markup Contract

<div class="m-resend-row">
  <button class="a-link a-link--quiet a-link--flush" id="channelSwitchBtn" type="button">Send code by SMS</button>
  <button class="a-link a-link--quiet a-link--flush" id="backToNumberBtn" type="button">Use a different registered number</button>
</div>

Exactly two children, both carrying all three link classes. Drop --flush from one of them and that label sits --sp-1 inside the edge its partner holds, which is visible at a glance on a 320 screen.

Responsive Behaviour

No media query of its own. It answers width through flex-wrap alone.

Under [data-text="large"] the links read --text-footnote, which goes 14 to 16, so the pair needs more room than the narrow screen has. Wrapping is the designed answer. The row-gap exists for exactly that moment and is otherwise never seen.

Related Components

Children: a-link, always --quiet --flush. Parent: o-signup-sheet, which sets its top margin. Above it on the same stage: m-otp-group and the primary a-btn.

m-brand-lockup

The mark, the wordmark and the strapline set as one object, so the brand is placed once rather than assembled on each screen.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The hero lockupthe one place the brand speaks at full size

EngenXT

Driver rewards

Purpose and Usage

Use it once per screen, at the top, where a driver needs to know which app they are holding. The sign-in hero uses the full form. The app bar on the home screen uses the short form, without the strapline, because by then the driver is inside the app.

Do not use it as a decoration in the middle of a flow. Do not put it on a sheet. The sheet already sits on top of a screen that carries it, so a second lockup says the driver has arrived somewhere new when they have not.

Do not restyle it from a parent. This block was inverted once for exactly that reason, which is documented under Variants.

Anatomy

A flex row with a --sp-2h gap: the mark, then one plain span holding the name over the strapline. The extra span is what makes the row two children rather than three, so the mark centres against the pair rather than against the first line.

__mark is a --size-object square, 36px, in the brand red on a --radius-chip corner, with --shadow-brand under it. It centres whatever glyph is put inside it, so the icon does not need positioning of its own.

__name is the wordmark at --text-title in --weight-black, on --leading-flush so the strapline sits tight underneath, with a 2px optical margin under it. Its em is not italic. The rule cancels the italic then paints it --color-secondary, which is what makes the XT read as part of the wordmark rather than as emphasis inside a sentence.

__tag is the strapline: --text-micro, uppercased by CSS, at 1.5px of letter spacing. That value is off the tracking scale on purpose. The scale is in em, so at 10px it lands too tight to read as a lockup.

Mark, name, tagthe name alone is the app bar form
.m-brand-lockup__mark .m-brand-lockup__name

EngenXT

__tag is not shown on a light stage here, because it has no light colour. It is written in --color-on-dark-faint, which is white at 45%, with no counterpart rule for a light surface. The strapline belongs to the hero. If a light screen ever needs it, the block needs a colour before that screen ships.

Variants

One: .m-brand-lockup--onDark. It changes exactly one thing, the name's ink, from --color-text-primary to --color-text-on-dark.

Default, on a light surface
.m-brand-lockup EngenXT
On the hero
.m-brand-lockup--onDark

EngenXT

Driver rewards

Light is the default here, dark is the exception. It used to run the other way: the name was written in the hero's white, then the app's home bar, which is a light surface, reached in with .o-home .m-brand-lockup__name to put the ink back. That is a parent restyling a child's insides, which the Composition law forbids outright. It was also invisible to the parity gate, which drops any rule whose classes do not all root into the manifest. So the gate went on proving the lockup identical in both files while the two files rendered it in two different colours. Inverting it put the exception on a modifier, the way a-btn, a-link, a-icon-wrap already do it.

States

Nothing to showthe block implements no state
EngenXT

None. There is no hover, no focus, no pressed, no disabled, no data attribute in the block. It is not a control. Nothing in the product presses it.

Behaviour

None. No transition, no animation, no script. It is written into the page once at load then never touched again. The hero's entrance belongs to o-auth-hero, which carries the lockup along with everything else it holds.

Content Guidance

The wordmark is fixed: Engen<em>XT</em>, one word, no space, no hyphen. The em wraps XT only. It is where the red starts, so the two letters are never typed as a separate word or a separate element.

The strapline is two words, typed in sentence case. CSS uppercases it. Typing it in capitals gives a screen reader a string of letters to spell out.

"Driver rewards" is what the app is to the person holding it. It is not a tagline slot for campaign copy. If it changes, it changes because the product changed.

Accessibility

  • The mark is decoration. The hero hides it with aria-hidden="true" on __mark; the app bar hides the svg instead. Either way the glyph is never announced, because the wordmark beside it already says the name.
  • The name is never a heading: a <p> in the hero, a <span> in the app bar. The screen's real heading is the title under it, so the lockup does not take the first heading a screen reader reaches.
  • The em is a colour hook. The rule cancels its italic then paints it, so it changes no letter shape. The name reads aloud as one word either way.
  • --weight-black at --text-title on --color-text-primary or --color-text-on-dark: large text, high contrast, in both directions.
  • It is not focusable. Nothing in it is a control, so nothing in it takes a tab stop.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-2hgap between the mark and the words, 10px
--size-objectthe mark's width and height, 36px
--color-secondarythe mark's fill, also the XT inside the name
--radius-chipthe mark's corner, 10px
--shadow-brandthe lift under the mark, built from the brand red rather than from black
--text-title · --weight-black · --tracking-snug · --leading-flushthe wordmark
--color-text-primarythe wordmark's default ink
--color-text-on-darkthe wordmark's ink under --onDark
--text-micro · --weight-mediumthe strapline
--color-on-dark-faintthe strapline's ink, white at 45%, which is why it is a hero part

Two values in the block are literal rather than tokens: the 2px under the wordmark, which is optical, plus the 1.5px of strapline tracking, which is off the scale for the reason given in Anatomy. Both carry a comment in the source saying so.

Markup Contract

The full form, from the sign-in hero:

<div class="m-brand-lockup m-brand-lockup--onDark">
  <span class="m-brand-lockup__mark" aria-hidden="true">
    <svg width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" …>…</svg>
  </span>
  <span>
    <p class="m-brand-lockup__name">Engen<em>XT</em></p>
    <p class="m-brand-lockup__tag">Driver rewards</p>
  </span>
</div>

The short form, from the app bar on the home screen:

<span class="m-brand-lockup">
  <span class="m-brand-lockup__mark"><svg width="20" height="20" … aria-hidden="true">…</svg></span>
  <span class="m-brand-lockup__name">Engen<em>XT</em></span>
</span>

The glyph is 18px in the hero, 20px in the app bar. The mark is 36px in both, so the difference is padding inside the square, not a second size of lockup. The wrapping element may be a div or a span; both are in the product today. Nothing in the block depends on which.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. No media query touches it. It is a flex row that takes the width of its content, so it sits at the leading edge of whatever holds it.

Under [data-text="large"] the wordmark moves from 22px to 24px, the strapline from 11px to 13px. The mark does not grow, because --size-object is not on the text ramp. That is deliberate: the words are what a driver reads, the square is furniture.

Related Components

Parent: o-auth-hero places the full form in its __brand slot. The app bar on the home screen places the short form; that bar is product-specific to engenxt-onboarding.html with its CSS still inline in that page. Nearby: a-icon-wrap, which is the general tinted square for icons that are not the brand mark.

m-figure

A small uppercase key over a heavy tabular number, for a fact the driver has to read off the screen rather than out of a sentence.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

Three figures from the update screennumbers a driver can repeat down a phone line
Installed2.4.1 Required2.6.0 Download size42 MB

Purpose and Usage

Use it when the number is the point. The update screen is the case it was built for: "update to the latest version" is an instruction with nothing in it, because a driver cannot tell whether they already did that last week, cannot say which version they are on when they ring their manager, cannot tell a live prompt from one that has been stuck on the screen for a month. Two version numbers side by side make the sentence checkable.

Do not use it for a label on a control, for a heading, or for prose that happens to contain a number. A number inside a sentence belongs in b inside that sentence, which m-alert already styles with tabular figures. Use m-figure only when the reader is meant to compare, copy or quote the value.

It is small enough to look free. It is not. Every figure on a screen is one more thing to read before acting, so two or three is a set, six is a table nobody asked for.

Anatomy

Two parts in a column, 2px apart, left aligned. __key is the smallest type in the system at --text-nano, bold, uppercased by CSS, with --tracking-caps-wide to keep 10px capitals legible, in --color-text-tertiary. __val is --text-lead at --weight-heavy in the primary ink.

The value takes font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums. That is why two figures standing next to each other line up on their digits rather than drifting apart by the width of a 1.

The 2px gap is a literal, not a token. The pair is meant to read as one object, which is closer than any step on the spacing scale allows.

Key over value
.m-figure__keyDownload size .m-figure__val42 MB

Variants

None.

States

One appearancethe block implements no state
Required2.6.0

None. No hover, no focus, no pressed, no disabled, no data attribute. It is text. Nothing about it changes once it is written.

One parent does change it. .o-status-page__meta .m-figure in src/css/organisms.css sets align-items: center plus text-align: center. That is a parent reaching into a child, so it is scoped to that one row deliberately, with the reason in the source: on the status panel three figures stand side by side with nothing above or below to line up with, so a left edge buys nothing, meanwhile DOWNLOAD SIZE is twice the width of 42 MB, which leaves the number sitting off under one end of its own label. Everywhere else the default stays left aligned, because a column of figures does share one edge to read down.

The centred exceptionthis is the parent's rule, not a modifier on the figure
Installed2.4.1 Required2.6.0 Download size42 MB

Behaviour

None. No transition, no animation, no script of its own. On the status page a row of them is written in one pass from the definition's meta array, then never updated. If the value can change while the screen is open, this is the wrong component: a running clock belongs in an element built to be rewritten.

Content Guidance

The key is one or two words, typed in sentence case. CSS uppercases it. Typing capitals hands a screen reader letters to spell out. "Installed", "Required", "Download size".

The value carries its unit: "42 MB", not "42". A number with no unit forces the reader back into the key to work out what it counts, which is the work this component exists to remove.

Keep the value short enough not to wrap. It is 20px at weight 800, so it goes wide fast. If the value needs a sentence, it is not a figure.

Never put a fact in a figure that is not also in the body copy beside it. The status page announces its title plus its body to the live region; the figures are not part of that announcement. A driver listening rather than looking must still get everything they need to act.

Accessibility

  • Two plain spans, no role, no ARIA. A screen reader reads them in order, key then value, which is the order they are written in.
  • The key is not programmatically associated with the value. Nothing pairs them but reading order, so a figure must make sense read aloud as two words in a row.
  • Not focusable. It carries no control, so it takes no tab stop.
  • --color-text-tertiary on the key is the system's dimmest ink. It is used here because the key is the label, the value is the content. Do not reuse the key style for anything a driver has to read on its own.
  • Both sizes are on the text ramp, so the pair grows under the driver's large text setting.
  • It is not announced by the status page's live region. See Content Guidance.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--text-nano · --weight-boldthe key, 10px
--tracking-caps-widethe key's letter spacing, 0.16em, which is what makes 10px capitals readable
--color-text-tertiarythe key's ink
--text-lead · --weight-heavythe value, 20px at 800
--color-text-primarythe value's ink

Five tokens, one literal. The 2px gap is the literal, for the reason under Anatomy. There is no colour token for a dark surface, so a figure on the hero would be primary ink on near black. Nothing in the product puts one there today.

Markup Contract

<span class="m-figure">
  <span class="m-figure__key">Download size</span>
  <span class="m-figure__val">42 MB</span>
</span>

Spans, not dl. The block sets display: flex on the root, so the two children stack whatever their tag. The product writes them from showStatusPage, one figure per entry in the definition's meta array, so a screen that wants figures declares them as data rather than as markup: 

meta: [
  { key: 'Installed', val: APP_VERSION.installed },
  { key: 'Required',  val: APP_VERSION.required },
  { key: 'Download size', val: '42 MB' }
]

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. No media query touches it. It takes the width of its own content, so the row that holds it decides how the set is laid out.

Under [data-text="large"] the key goes from 10px to 12px, the value from 20px to 22px. Both grow, so a row of them grows. The status page's row does not wrap: it is a plain flex row with a --space-section gap. Three figures is what that row is built to hold at the narrowest width the product targets. Adding a fourth is a layout change, not a content change.

Related Components

Parent: o-status-page lays a row of them out in its __meta slot, centred, which is the one place the alignment is overridden. Alternative: a bold run inside m-alert's body when the number belongs to a sentence rather than standing on its own.

m-status-bar

The handset's clock, signal and battery, drawn across the top of the frame.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The bar as the signup flow uses iton the hero, tone onDark
9:41 No service

It is the one piece of device chrome that draws itself in normal flow, so this is a true specimen rather than a stand-in. It measures 72px here: 54px of top padding, then an 18px line box for the clock. That is what it measures in the product too.

Purpose and Usage

Use it to give a screen a real top. The system reserves the first 78px of the display for the handset, so a layout judged without that band is judged against space it will not get. --size-status-bar holds the number once. The sheet subtracts it when the keyboard is up so the bar stays visible.

Do not use it for product messaging. Everything in it is a drawing of the handset. The one exception is the "No service" word, which reports the demo's simulated connection state, because without it data-net was a flag with no rendering: Offline looked normal until a driver tried to send.

Anatomy

Four parts. The block is a row with justify-content: space-between. It holds __time on the left, __net after it, then __icons holding two inline SVGs. The first of those two carries __signal, which exists only so the offline rule can dim it. The battery SVG has no class at all.

Because the bar spaces three children rather than two, __net lands between the clock and the icons when it shows, not tucked against the clock. Its --sp-2 left margin is measured from the clock side.

Padding is calc(var(--size-status-bar) - 24px) on top, 24px left and right, nothing at the bottom. The 24 subtracted is a nominal row for the clock. The clock's real line box at --text-body is 18px, so the bar measures 72px while the token says 78. The sheet subtracts the whole 78, so 6px of hero shows between the bar and the sheet when the keyboard is open.

Variants

One, reached by an attribute rather than a class.

Default tonedark ink, for a light screen
9:41 No service
[data-tone="onDark"]on the hero ground
9:41 No service

Honest limit. The tone rules reach the text only. Both icon SVGs carry fill="var(--color-on-dark-strong)" as attributes in the markup, so the signal bars in the default-tone canvas above are white on a near-white stage and cannot be seen. In index.html that never shows, because the bar is written data-tone="onDark" once and never changed. In engenxt-onboarding.html it does: setStatusTone(false) removes the attribute on every light screen while the fills stay where they are. Fixing it means moving the fills to currentColor or to a rule, not adding another attribute here.

States

data-net="online"the default, set on <html>
9:41 No service
data-net="offline"the word appears, the bars drop to 35%
9:41 No service
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Onlinethe absence of the offline flag__net stays display: none, the bars stay at full opacity
Offline[data-net="offline"] on any ancestor__net becomes visible, __signal drops to opacity: .35

Nothing else. The bar has no hover, no focus, no press, no disabled form, because it is not a control. Both rules are descendant selectors, so the flag can sit anywhere above the bar. In the product it sits on <html>. The canvas above puts it on the stage, which is the same relationship one level closer.

Behaviour

No transition, no animation, no pointer handling. Every change is a repaint of a new attribute value.

The tone is script-driven in one of the two pages that use it. engenxt-onboarding.html calls setStatusTone on each screen, which adds data-tone="onDark" or removes it. index.html writes the attribute once in the markup.

Stacking is --z-content, 2, which is the app's own room. That is enough inside the hero, where the bar is a flow sibling. It is not enough under a full-screen surface: engenxt-onboarding.html carries one extra rule raising the bar with the notch to --z-tool-top, because an app screen was covering the clock and the battery. That rule is still inline in that page.

Content Guidance

The clock reads 9:41 in both pages, fixed. It is a specimen value, not a live time, so two screenshots of the same screen match.

The offline word is "No service", the phone's phrase for no signal. Do not write an app sentence here. If a driver has to act on the connection, the screen says so in a surface a screen reader can reach, not in the chrome.

Accessibility

  • It is aria-hidden="true" in both pages. It is a drawing of hardware, so nothing in it is announced.
  • That includes "No service". The word is read by eye only, which is why its problem was a contrast problem.
  • __net was written in --color-on-dark-soft unconditionally: white at 60% on light screens, measured at 2.30:1 on home and 2.47:1 on the scan screen. Dark ink is now the default and the on-dark ramp is the exception, which is the rule the rest of the bar already followed.
  • Its type is on the ramp on purpose. --text-body and --text-caption2 both grow at data-text="large", to 17px and 14px, so the clock and the offline word answer the driver's text-size setting. The drawn keyboard deliberately does the opposite. See m-keyboard.
  • Nothing in it is focusable. It holds no interactive element.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--size-status-bar78px. The top padding is this minus 24. The sheet subtracts the whole token when the keyboard is up
--sp-6left and right padding, 24px
--sp-1hgap between the signal icon and the battery, 6px
--sp-2left margin on __net, 8px
--text-body · --weight-semibold · --tracking-tightthe clock
--text-caption2 · --weight-bold · --tracking-tightthe offline word
--color-text-secondarythe offline word's default ink
--color-on-dark-strongthe clock under data-tone="onDark"
--color-on-dark-softthe offline word under data-tone="onDark"
--z-contentstacking, 2

env(safe-area-inset-top) is not a token. It is read in a max() beside the padding, so a real notched device can push the bar further down than 54px but never less.

Markup Contract

<div class="m-status-bar" data-tone="onDark" aria-hidden="true">
  <span class="m-status-bar__time">9:41</span>
    <span class="m-status-bar__net">No service</span>
  <span class="m-status-bar__icons">
    <svg class="m-status-bar__signal" width="17" height="12" viewBox="0 0 17 12" fill="none">
      <rect x="0" y="7" width="3" height="5" rx="1" fill="var(--color-on-dark-strong)"/>
      <rect x="4.5" y="4.5" width="3" height="7.5" rx="1" fill="var(--color-on-dark-strong)"/>
      <rect x="9" y="2" width="3" height="10" rx="1" fill="var(--color-on-dark-strong)"/>
      <rect x="13.5" y="0" width="3" height="12" rx="1" fill="var(--color-on-dark-strong)"/>
    </svg>
    <svg width="25" height="12" viewBox="0 0 25 12" fill="none">
      <rect x="0.5" y="0.5" width="21" height="11" rx="3.5" stroke="var(--color-on-dark-faint)"/>
      <rect x="1.5" y="1.5" width="18" height="9" rx="2.5" fill="var(--color-on-dark-strong)"/>
      <path d="M22.5 4.2L22.5 7.8C23.4 7.4 23.4 4.6 22.5 4.2Z" fill="var(--color-on-dark-faint)"/>
    </svg>
  </span>
</div>

That is the markup as it stands in index.html, indentation included. The __net span is indented one step deeper than its siblings in both pages. It is a flex item like the other two: the indentation says nothing to the layout.

The block must stay a flow sibling near the top of its screen. It is position: relative with flex-shrink: 0, so a flex column parent cannot squeeze it.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It takes the width of whatever holds it, so it follows the frame through data-width 390, 375, 360 and 320 without a rule. Its height does not move with width: --size-status-bar is one value.

Two things do change it. data-text="large" grows the clock and the offline word, which grows the bar. env(safe-area-inset-top) takes over the top padding when it exceeds 54px, which only happens on a real handset, under the 440px breakpoint where the frame goes full-bleed at 100dvh.

Related Components

Parent: o-auth-hero in index.html, t-device-frame in engenxt-onboarding.html. Sibling chrome: m-notch above it, m-keyboard at the other end of the screen. Reads the same height token: o-signup-sheet.

m-notch

The band that holds the notch atom against the top edge of the phone.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The atom it holdsthe molecule itself is a position, so it cannot stand on a canvas

That is a-notch, 126 by 34, black, rounded on its two lower corners. The molecule adds no paint at all. Everything it contributes is placement, so a specimen of it needs a phone around it, which a canvas is not.

This is measured rather than assumed. Dropped loose into a canvas, the block lands at the top left of the viewport at full window width, because it is position: absolute with no positioned ancestor in the documentation layout. Its stage collapses to 44px of empty padding. It would draw a black bar across the top of this page. Its containing block has to be the frame, or the hero that fills it.

Purpose and Usage

Use it once per screen, at the top of the device frame, to put the handset's notch where a handset has one. It exists so a design can be judged against a real screen rather than a clean rectangle.

Do not put anything else inside it. It is not a header, not a slot, not a place for product chrome. It holds exactly one atom. Do not use it outside the frame: it is hardware, so it has no meaning on a page that is not pretending to be a phone.

Anatomy

Two elements. The block is a full-width band 50px tall, pinned to top: 0, a row flex with justify-content: center. Its one child is a-notch, which hangs from the top edge and takes the horizontal centre. The 50px is a literal rather than a token. It is taller than the atom on purpose: the band is the reach of the hardware, the atom is the shape cut out of it.

It needs a positioned ancestor. In index.html that is o-auth-hero, which is position: absolute; inset: 0. In engenxt-onboarding.html the block is a direct child of t-device-frame, which is position: relative. Either works. Nothing else does.

Variants

None. There is one rule for this block in the whole system.

The hardware does change shape at the Android width, but that change belongs to the atom, not to this block. t-device-frame[data-width="360"] .a-notch turns the 126 by 34 bar into a 14px circle with a 10px top margin, because a 360-wide frame wearing an iPhone notch is a phone that does not exist. The band around it is the same band.

States

None. Grep the block and there is no :hover, no :focus-visible, no :active, no [data-state], no [hidden]. It cannot be hovered or pressed, because pointer-events: none takes it out of hit testing. It is never withdrawn: neither page has code that hides it.

Behaviour

pointer-events: none is the whole of its behaviour. The band covers the top 50px of the screen, which is where a sheet handle or a close button can sit, so without it the hardware would eat a real tap.

It stacks at --z-hardware, 50. That is above the toast at 40, the overlay at 30 and the sheet at 10, because hardware sits above everything the app can draw. It is below --z-tool-top at 60, which is the documentation layer, so the tooling can still be seen over the phone.

One page raises it further. engenxt-onboarding.html carries an inline rule putting the notch and the status bar at --z-tool-top, because a full-screen app surface there was covering the clock and the battery. That rule has not moved into src/css.

Content Guidance

None. It holds no text and takes no label.

Accessibility

  • It is aria-hidden="true" in both pages that use it. A drawing of a bezel is not information.
  • pointer-events: none keeps it out of hit testing, so it cannot steal a tap from the control underneath it.
  • It holds nothing focusable, so it never appears in the tab order.
  • It carries no colour meaning. The black is --color-hardware, which the token file marks as the physical bezel rather than a brand colour.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--z-hardwarestacking, 50. The only token the block reads

Its height of 50px, its top, left and right of zero are literals. Everything visible comes from a-notch: --color-hardware for the fill, --radius-notch for the two lower corners.

Markup Contract

<div class="m-notch" aria-hidden="true"><div class="a-notch"></div></div>

One line, identical in both pages. It sits inside a positioned ancestor, near the top of the frame's markup, above the screen it covers.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It has no media query and no data-width rule. Pinned to left: 0; right: 0, it takes the frame's width at 390, 375, 360 and 320 without being told. It keeps its 50px height at all four.

At the 440px breakpoint the frame goes full-bleed at 100dvh with no radius. The band follows the frame, so the notch stays centred on a real handset screen.

Related Components

Child: a-notch, the only thing it may contain. Parent: t-device-frame, or o-auth-hero where the hero fills the frame. Sibling chrome: m-status-bar beneath it, m-keyboard at the foot of the same frame.

m-keyboard

The Android numeric keypad the signup flow raises, drawn rather than imagined, so the sheet can be judged against the height it really has.

LevelMolecule OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/molecules.css StatusStable

Preview

The drawing390 x 312 in the frame, shown here at its intrinsic 300 wide
.m-keyboard__svg

That is the child, not the block. .m-keyboard itself is display: none until the frame says otherwise. Its height is --keyboard-inset, which is 0px until the runtime writes a real number onto the frame. Both of those make it invisible on a canvas, so the specimen above is the SVG on its own. What is missing from it is the block's ground colour, its 0.5px top border and its clipping. The drawing itself is the whole of what you see in the product.

Purpose and Usage

Use it to make the on-screen keyboard part of the layout instead of something that happens to the layout. The keyboard takes roughly half the screen. A sheet designed without it is designed for a screen that does not exist at the moment a driver is typing, which is the moment the sheet matters most.

It is a numeric pad because both inputs in the flow are inputmode="numeric". That is the keyboard Android actually raises for them. Do not swap in a full alphabetic layout to make a screen look busier.

Do not use it as an input. It has no keys, no handlers, no events. It draws the space the operating system takes. If you need a driver to press something, that is a product control on the sheet above it.

Anatomy

Two elements plus two text classes. The block is an absolutely positioned strip across the bottom of the frame. It owns the ground colour, the hairline along its top edge, the height and the clipping. Its one child .m-keyboard__svg fills it at 100% by 100% and owns the drawing.

Inside the SVG, every shape takes its fill straight from the --kb- palette as an attribute. The two classed elements are .m-keyboard__digit for the numerals and .m-keyboard__sub for the letter labels under them.

Top to bottom the drawing is: a strip of three blank pills between two vertical dividers, closed by a horizontal divider at y 38; four rows of three keys; a backspace key in --kb-key-alt top right; an enter key in --kb-accent spanning two rows at the bottom right; a home indicator bar. The pills are drawn as shapes rather than as words on purpose, so the drawing never invents text the operating system supplies.

Variants

None. One block, one child, no modifier class anywhere in the CSS.

States

Two. Neither can be shown outside the frame. Closed is display: none at zero height. Open is a strip 336px tall at the foot of a 390 by 844 frame. The Keyboard toggle on the demo page opens one.

StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Closedthe default. No attribute on the framedisplay: none. --keyboard-inset stays at its declared 0px
Open[data-keyboard="open"] on t-device-frame, written by the pagedisplay: block. The height comes from whatever --keyboard-inset the page has set on the frame

Nothing else. No hover, no focus, no press, no disabled form, no [hidden], no tone. It is a picture.

The two halves of open are set together but they are not the same thing. The attribute switches the display. The custom property sets the height. The demo's toggle writes 336px. On a real device the number comes from visualViewport, which sets the property first and only adds the attribute once the inset passes 80px. Both paths write the same value into the same place.

Behaviour

The SVG uses preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMax meet", so the drawing is anchored to the bottom of the strip and centred across it. At the 390 width with the demo's 336px inset the viewBox is drawn at 1:1, 312 tall, inside a 336 tall strip. The 24px left over sits above the keys, filled by the block's own background: var(--kb-surface). That is why the ground colour lives on the block instead of only in the drawing.

It stacks at --z-toast, 40, the same room as a-toast. The notch at --z-hardware stays above it, which is correct: one is drawn hardware, the other is drawn software.

No transition of its own. It appears with the display switch. What moves is the sheet above it, which is sized against the same --keyboard-inset so the two never overlap.

Opening it triggers the compression order in the template: tooling goes first, then the step counter, then an instruction the driver has already acted on, then the loose spacing steps. Never the base gap. The base gap is what says which things belong together, which matters most when there is least room to read it.

Content Guidance

The glyphs belong to Android, not to EngenXT. 1 through 9, then * 0 #, with ABC through WXYZ under the digits and a + under the zero. Do not translate them. Do not put product words on the keys. Do not add a "Done" label to the enter key: it is drawn as an arrow because that is what the platform draws.

The suggestion pills stay blank. Words there would be content the product does not own.

Accessibility

  • The block is aria-hidden="true". A real keyboard is never in the accessibility tree of the page it covers.
  • The SVG repeats aria-hidden="true" and carries focusable="false", which keeps older engines from putting it in the tab order.
  • Nothing inside is interactive, so there is no target size to meet and no name to give.
  • __digit at 21px and __sub at 9px sit outside the type ramp deliberately. The ramp exists so app text answers the driver's text-size setting. A drawing of the operating system's keyboard must not: a real keyboard does not grow when app text does. They are the only two literal font sizes in the whole of src/css. Every other size in the product comes off the ramp.
  • The 9px on __sub is below --text-nano. It would fail as product copy. It passes here because it is a picture of a letter hint, not a sentence anyone has to read.
  • Its real accessibility job is the compression order it triggers. Nothing a driver must act on is allowed to end up behind it.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--keyboard-insetthe strip's height. Declared 0px in the token file. The only runtime-owned token in the system: the page writes the real value on the frame
--z-toaststacking, 40
--kb-surfacethe block's background, repeated as the SVG's ground rect
--size-hairlinethe top border, 0.5px. The token file calls this the finest line the scale owns, whose one real border is this keyboard
--kb-dividerthat border, the strip's two vertical rules and the line under the pills
--kb-key · --kb-key-altthe key faces. The alt is the backspace key
--kb-hintthe three blank suggestion pills
--kb-accent · --kb-on-accentthe enter key and the arrow on it
--kb-text__digit fill, the backspace glyph, the home indicator at 55% opacity
--kb-sub__sub fill
--font-sans · --weight-regular · --weight-semibold · --tracking-capsthe two text classes

The --kb- ramp is a separate palette on purpose. This is the operating system's chrome, so it holds its own colours rather than borrowing EngenXT's. Nothing outside this molecule may read them.

The two font sizes are literals: 21px and 9px. They are not tokens. They must not become tokens.

Markup Contract

<div class="m-keyboard" aria-hidden="true">
  <svg class="m-keyboard__svg" viewBox="0 0 390 312" preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMax meet" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false">
    <rect width="390" height="312" fill="var(--kb-surface)"/>
    <line x1="0" y1="38" x2="390" y2="38" stroke="var(--kb-divider)" stroke-width="1"/>
    <rect x="55.0" y="14" width="46" height="10" rx="5" fill="var(--kb-hint)"/>
    <!-- two more pills, two vertical dividers -->
    <rect x="8.0" y="44" width="89.8" height="52" rx="8" fill="var(--kb-key)"/>
    <text x="52.9" y="78.0" class="m-keyboard__digit">1</text>
    <rect x="102.8" y="44" width="89.8" height="52" rx="8" fill="var(--kb-key)"/>
    <text x="147.6" y="72.0" class="m-keyboard__digit">2</text>
    <text x="147.6" y="86.0" class="m-keyboard__sub">ABC</text>
    <!-- 3, then rows 4-6, 7-9 and * 0 #, same rect plus text pattern -->
    <rect x="292.2" y="44" width="89.8" height="52" rx="8" fill="var(--kb-key-alt)"/>
    <g stroke="var(--kb-text)" stroke-width="1.8" fill="none" stroke-linecap="round">…</g>
    <rect x="292.2" y="158" width="89.8" height="109" rx="8" fill="var(--kb-accent)"/>
    <g stroke="var(--kb-on-accent)" stroke-width="2" fill="none" …>…</g>
    <rect x="140.0" y="290" width="110" height="4" rx="2" fill="var(--kb-text)" opacity="0.55"/>
  </svg>
</div>

Abridged at the two comments. Every row is the same rect plus text pattern, at the same four x positions, stepping 57 in y. The full 52 lines are in index.html.

The block must be a direct child of t-device-frame, outside the screen it covers. In index.html it sits after the hero section closes. Placed inside a screen it would be clipped by that screen's own overflow.

Responsive Behaviour

No media query of its own. Pinned to left: 0; right: 0, it takes the frame's width, so the drawing scales with data-width: at 320 it renders at 82% of the size it renders at 390. The keys stay in proportion because the whole thing is one viewBox.

The height does not scale with the width. It is whatever the page writes into --keyboard-inset, which is why the strip can be taller than the drawing needs.

data-text="large" changes nothing here. That is the point of the two literal sizes.

Related Components

Parent: t-device-frame, which owns the attribute that shows it. Sized against the same token: o-signup-sheet. First thing it displaces: a-progress. Sibling chrome: m-notch and m-status-bar at the other end of the frame.

Organisms

Organisms are larger combinations that form a distinct section or workflow inside an interface.

4 exist. All four are product-specific rather than shared. In this product an organism is a whole screen surface rather than a reusable section, which is a real departure from the model and is recorded under Known Limitations.

o-auth-hero

The home screen: a promise, one action and the door into sign-in.

LevelOrganism OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/organisms.css StatusStable

Preview

The brand, the promise and the one actionthe gradient ground is real, the orbs need the frame

EngenXT

Driver rewards

Fill up at Engen.
Earn mobile data.

Your manager added you to EngenXT. Sign in with the mobile number they registered for you.

No password needed. We'll send you a 6-digit code.

Get help on WhatsApp

The block itself is position: absolute; inset: 0, so it can only exist inside t-device-frame. What is drawn above is its content column on the hero's own top colour. The three washes behind it are each absolutely placed against __orbs so they are described below rather than faked here.

Purpose and Usage

Use it for the first screen of the product. It says what EngenXT gives a driver, says where their number came from and offers the single action that opens o-signup-sheet.

Do not use it as a generic section. It fills the frame absolutely, so it is the ground that the sheet and o-status-page stand over rather than a block you can place in a column. Keep it to one action. When entry is blocked the action is rewritten into the recovery, never left on screen looking dead.

Anatomy

A flex column of three parts, plus a decorative layer under them. __orbs is the wash layer at --z-base, holding four __orb circles. __brand is flex-shrink 0 at the top. __body takes the remaining height, scrolls on its own axis and holds __title, __lede, __notice then __actions. __actions holds the button, __how and the help link, centred with an --sp-2 gap, the button stretched to full width.

__title carries margin-top: auto rather than the column using justify-content: flex-end. Flex-end plus overflow pushes overflow past the top edge into a region the scroll container cannot reach. The effect is that every real thing on the screen anchors to the bottom of the frame where the thumb is, while the orbs carry the air above it.

m-notch and m-status-bar sit inside the section in the markup. They are device chrome rather than parts of the hero, so they carry no o-auth-hero__ name.

Variants

None on the block. Four modifiers exist one level down, on __orb. Each fixes one wash's corner, size and radial colour.

ModifierPlacedSizeColour
--atop -80px, right -60px360 × 360--color-primary-30 to --color-primary-08 to transparent
--aInnertop 40%, right -40px180 × 180--color-secondary-18 to transparent
--btop 180px, left -60px260 × 260--color-secondary-18 to --color-secondary-04 to transparent
--cbottom 200px, right -30px200 × 200--color-tertiary-18 to --color-tertiary-04 to transparent

Each one is absolutely positioned against __orbs, so none of them can be drawn on a flat canvas without inventing a box for it. --aInner is nested inside --a in the markup.

States

The hero carries no state attribute of its own. Everything below is done to it, either by the sheet's stage through t-device-frame or by script. The one part with a state of its own is __notice, which is a slot that is either hidden or filled.

__notice, filledthe slot holds an m-alert--onDark and nothing else

You're offline

Connect to Wi-Fi or mobile data to continue.
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Defaultno attribute anywhere42px headline pinned to the bottom, lede, button, help
Notice present__notice loses hiddenan m-alert--onDark appears with --sp-4 under it
A sheet is up.t-device-frame:has(.o-signup-sheet[data-stage])__title drops to --text-title with margin-top: var(--sp-6), __lede goes to opacity 0, __actions goes to opacity 0 with pointer-events: none
The sheet is blocked.t-device-frame:has(.o-signup-sheet[data-stage="blocked"])__title goes to opacity 0 too, so the sheet takes the room
Behind an overlayinert plus aria-hidden="true", set by scriptnothing visual. The section leaves the tab order and the accessibility tree

The copy moves rather than hides when a sheet is up. On the shorter sheets the top edge landed part way down the headline and sliced it. Hiding it left 40 per cent of the screen holding nothing while the one sentence explaining the product was thrown away, so instead the copy goes to the top of the space it has left. The promise stays legible above the glass at exactly the moment a driver is deciding whether this is worth the data it costs.

Behaviour

The three washes are static. They used to drift about 20px on 9, 11 and 13 second loops, which is below the amplitude anyone consciously notices, so the movement bought nothing visible while holding three composited layers alive on the one screen where a driver forms an impression of how fast the app is. The colour is the point.

__title and __lede transition opacity over --dur-base with --ease-out. Margin moves over --dur-sheet with --ease-emphasis. The margin curve is the sheet's own, so the headline moving up and the sheet arriving read as one motion. __actions fades over --dur-base, a rule that lives in templates.css beside the condition that triggers it.

__body scrolls with overflow-y: auto. Nothing else in the organism scrolls.

Content Guidance

The headline is two short lines split with a <br>, not a wrap, so the break lands where the sense breaks. The lede names who registered the number, because a driver who does not know why the app has their number will not trust it. __how is one sentence saying how the door works, so the button below it is an answer rather than a question.

The primary label is rewritten in place for recovery, from "Sign in with mobile number" to "Try again". The help link changes with it. Offline is the one screen where the usual route is unreachable, so the link becomes a phone call, offered only after the second failed check because nobody on a phone line can fix mobile data.

Accessibility

  • The section takes aria-label="EngenXT home", so the screen has a name.
  • __orbs is aria-hidden. It is colour with no content in it.
  • __notice is role="status", so a notice that arrives is announced once without stealing focus.
  • The title is a real <h1>. It is the only h1 in the frame.
  • While the sheet or the status page is up, script sets inert plus aria-hidden on the whole section, then returns focus to the control that opened the overlay.
  • All text sits on --gradient-hero, so --color-text-on-dark and --color-on-dark-soft are the only two inks allowed here.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--gradient-herothe ground, a 175deg run through three hero colours
--z-base · --z-contentthe wash layer, then everything above it
--radius-circleeach orb
--color-primary-30 · --color-primary-08orb --a
--color-secondary-18 · --color-secondary-04orbs --aInner and --b
--color-tertiary-18 · --color-tertiary-04orb --c
--sp-7 · --sp-5__brand padding, 28 top on the 20 gutter
--space-page-inline__body inline padding, the same 20 the sheet uses
--sp-8 plus env(safe-area-inset-bottom)the bottom of __body
--text-hero · --weight-heavy · --leading-tight · --color-text-on-dark__title, with a hand-set -1.6px track
--sp-3the bind under the 42px headline
--text-body · --weight-regular · --leading-body · --color-on-dark-soft__lede
--sp-5the gap under __lede
--text-caption__how, centred under the button
--sp-4the gap under __notice
--sp-2__actions gap
--dur-base · --ease-out · --dur-sheet · --ease-emphasisthe opacity and margin transitions

The -1.6px letter-spacing on __title is off the scale on purpose. At 42px the display step is too loose and the hero step too tight. This is the only 42px line in the product. It is a single headline optic, not a token anyone else may reach for.

Markup Contract

<section class="o-auth-hero" id="authHero" aria-label="EngenXT home">

    </div>
    <div class="o-auth-hero__orb o-auth-hero__orb--b"></div>

  </div>

  <div class="m-status-bar" data-tone="onDark" aria-hidden="true">…</div>

  <div class="o-auth-hero__brand">
    <div class="m-brand-lockup m-brand-lockup--onDark">…</div>
  </div>

  <div class="o-auth-hero__body">

      <button class="a-btn a-btn--onDark" id="homePrimary" type="button">Sign in with mobile number</button>
      <p class="o-auth-hero__how">No password needed. We'll send you a 6-digit code.</p>
      <a class="a-link a-link--onDark" id="homeHelp" href="…" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Get help on WhatsApp</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

The order is fixed. __orbs must come first so it sits under everything without a z-index fight. __notice must sit between the lede and the actions so a notice is read before the button it changes.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It fills whatever the frame is, so the four device widths change it only by changing how much room the column has. Nothing in this block reads a media query. The bottom padding adds env(safe-area-inset-bottom) on top of --sp-8 rather than taking whichever is larger, so a device with a home indicator gets clearance rather than a button sitting on it.

Under large text the whole type ramp is repointed on the root, so __title, __lede and __how grow without any rule here knowing they did. The 42px headline is the line most likely to reflow, which is why it wraps rather than truncating.

Related Components

Parent: t-device-frame, which also owns the rules that respond to the sheet's stage. Siblings over it: o-signup-sheet and o-status-page. Children it places: m-brand-lockup, m-alert in the notice slot, a-btn and a-link.

o-signup-sheet

The glass sheet that rises over the hero and carries every step of signing in.

LevelOrganism OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/organisms.css StatusStable

Preview

The handle, the header and one active stagethe glass, the travel and the height need the device frame

Your number

Use the number your manager registered.

Step 1 of 2

We'll send a 6-digit code on WhatsApp. A small amount of mobile data is needed.

We only use this number for EngenXT.

The .o-signup-sheet element itself is position: absolute; display: none until it has a state, so it cannot stand on a flat canvas. Its glass is --glass-sheet-bg lifted by a backdrop filter, which needs the hero behind it to mean anything. What is drawn above is the column inside it, on a plain ground.

Purpose and Usage

Use it for the whole of signing in. Every step lives in one sheet as a stage rather than as a screen, so the driver never loses the hero behind it or their place in the journey.

Do not use it for a blocking technical fault. That is o-status-page, which covers the whole frame, because an account choice is not the answer to a broken server. Do not add a second sheet. There is one, it is bottom-anchored, it owns the keyboard relationship.

Anatomy

Four nested parts. __grab is a flex-shrink 0 strip at the top holding a-handle. __scroll takes the remaining height with flex: 1; min-height: 0 and is the only thing that scrolls. __body is the padded column inside it. __stage is one screen of the journey, of which exactly one is active.

The sheet does not scroll, its scroller does. That keeps the grab handle put and stops the horizontal travel of the stage animation from creating real scroll range. The scrollbar is hidden on both engines, since a bottom sheet on a phone has no scrollbar.

Inside a stage there are named slots: __cta for the primary action, __resend, __secondary for the way out to WhatsApp, __note for a rule the driver keeps, __noteMore for the disclosure that opens it.

The slots on the code stagecta, resend, secondary, note

Never share this code. Engen will never ask you for it.

__resend is align-self: flex-start. It is what a driver does when the code did not come, so it belongs to the cells above it rather than floating in the centre of the stage. __noteMore is drawn at the sentence's 12px size but pressable at the rule's 48, using a ::before at inset: -17px -10px. That is the same invisible half-target the map pins use.

Variants

None. The sheet has no modifier class. Which screen it is showing rides on data-stage, not on a class, so the layout can respond to a stage without anything building class strings.

States

Two axes on the block. data-state is the sheet's own visibility and travel. data-stage is which screen of the journey it is holding. A third, data-more, is a boolean fact about its scroller. Each stage carries its own data-state as well.

__resendwaiting, ready, then leading on the final attempt
:disabled default [data-lead="true"]
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Goneno data-statedisplay: none. Absence is gone, with no inline display writes anywhere
Openingdata-state="opening"display: flex plus sheetUp over --dur-sheet on --ease-emphasis
Closingdata-state="closing"sheetDown over --dur-quick, the same curve. One exit time, so the sheet and the scrim really do move as one object
Blockeddata-stage="blocked"min-height: 62%, __scroll becomes a column, __body takes flex: 1 and the active outcome panel centres itself in it
More belowdata-more="true"a linear-gradient mask fades the last --sp-7 of __scroll
Stage active__stage[data-state="active"]display: flex, gap: var(--space-stack-related), stageIn over --dur-sheet
Resend waiting__resend:disabledno underline, --color-text-secondary. Weight 500 comes from a-link:disabled
Resend leading__resend[data-lead="true"]--weight-heavy on --color-primary-dark
No backdrop filter@supports notthe glass goes fully opaque to --glass-sheet-fallback

The resend disabled colour is an exception written on purpose. --color-text-tertiary is 3.72:1 on the sheet, which is right for a control being ignored. The disabled resend is not being ignored: it is the sentence telling a driver when they can try again, read one-handed on a forecourt. It is content, so it takes the colour content takes.

data-more is set by script from scrollHeight - clientHeight - scrollTop > 12. Trailing padding is not content, so the fade appears only when something readable is below the fold rather than for the last few pixels of a gutter.

On the final attempt the safest recovery leads. The resend takes data-lead="true" and outranks the channel button beside it, while a-btn answers the same attribute by taking the primary coat where it stands. Neither control moves, so the two do not swap places under the thumb.

Behaviour

The keyboard is part of the layout rather than something that happens to it. The sheet sits at bottom: var(--keyboard-inset, 0px) and measures its height against what is left, calc(94dvh - var(--keyboard-inset, 0px)). Height, bottom and max-height all transition, height over --dur-sheet and the other two over --dur-base, all on --ease-emphasis.

Every stage gap is 8, 16 or 24, one scale with three meanings. 8 is parts of one thing: a label to its field, a field to its helper. 16 is one group to the next. 24 is a decision or a different route. The organism owns all of it through __stage's gap plus a margin on whichever element starts a looser step, so nothing in the markup sets spacing. Gaps used to be composed as 16 plus a 4px nudge, which rendered 18 and 20: close enough to the group step to read as noise rather than as meaning.

Script traps Tab inside the sheet while it is open, sets the hero inert, then returns focus to the control that opened it. Dismissal is armed rather than immediate when a driver is mid-task: a partly typed number or a code in progress gets one warning toast first.

Content Guidance

__note is a rule a driver keeps, not a helper about the current attempt. The code stage carries "Never share this code. Engen will never ask you for it." Two short sentences, no idiom, never naming a scam, because OTP-sharing scams circulate on WhatsApp in South Africa and the rule has to survive being remembered.

The number stage's note carries the POPIA disclosure inline, so the sentence and its door read as one. Section 18 asks that a person is told what is collected and why before collection, in words they can follow. The fact sits in the note, the detail is one tap away in __noteMore, nothing is bundled.

__secondary is only for an action that leaves the app for WhatsApp. If it is used for anything else the glyph lies.

Accessibility

  • The section is role="dialog" aria-modal="true" with aria-labelledby pointing at the sheet title.
  • __grab is not a button. It duplicated the close control and sat ahead of it in the tab order, so it is pointer affordance only. It is aria-hidden.
  • Focus is trapped in the sheet, the hero is inert plus aria-hidden, focus returns to the opener on close.
  • __noteMore carries aria-expanded for the card it opens.
  • __grab is at least --size-target tall, so the pointer affordance is a real 48px band.
  • Errors are announced once through the page's live region, never twice by also carrying role="alert".
