      <span class="a-helper" id="otpHelper" role="status">&nbsp;</span>
      <div id="codeAlert" tabindex="-1" hidden></div>
      <button class="a-btn a-btn--primary o-signup-sheet__cta" id="codeContinue" type="button" hidden>Continue</button>
      <button class="a-link a-link--flush o-signup-sheet__resend" id="resendBtn" type="button" disabled>Request another code in 0:45</button>
      <button class="a-btn a-btn--outline a-btn--sm o-signup-sheet__secondary" id="openWhatsappBtn" type="button">…</button>
      <div class="m-resend-row">…</div>
      <p class="o-signup-sheet__note">Never share this code. Engen will never ask you for it.</p>
    </div>

    <div class="o-signup-sheet__stage o-outcome-panel" id="stageBlocked">…</div>
    <div class="o-signup-sheet__stage o-outcome-panel" id="stageBiometrics">…</div>

  </div>
  </div>
</section>

Two rules bind. An alert host inside a stage is matched by [id$="Alert"], so it must end in Alert to get its 16px separation. The two wrappers, __scroll around __body, are both required: collapsing them puts the padding on the scrolling box then drops the grab handle back inside the scroll range.

Responsive Behaviour

The block reads no media query. Everything that varies by device sits in t-device-frame, which is where it belongs.

ConditionWhat the frame does to the sheet
[data-keyboard="open"]the sheet takes height and max-height of 100% - --keyboard-inset - --size-status-bar, so it sits on the keys and stops below the status bar
[data-keyboard="open"]__scroll becomes a column, __body and the active stage take flex: 1, __cta goes to margin-top: auto
[data-keyboard="open"]__cta and __secondary tighten to --sp-2; body bottom padding becomes max(--sp-4, safe-area)
[data-width="320"]__body inline padding drops to --sp-3

Height rather than max-height under the keyboard is deliberate. A sheet sized to its content leaves a strip of hero showing between it and the keys, then the last row of controls sits on top of the hero headline. Giving the sheet the full remaining height without giving the stage a way to spend it left the number stage holding 97px of empty sheet under its own button, so the stage takes the height too.

When space runs out it is given up in a fixed order: tooling, then the step counter, then an instruction the driver has already acted on, then the loose spacing steps. Never the base gap. The base gap is what says which things belong together, which matters most when there is least room to read it.

Related Components

Parent: t-device-frame. Sibling under it: a-scrim, which shares the sheet's state values so the two surfaces move together. It renders over o-auth-hero. Stages it holds: o-outcome-panel. Children it places: m-sheet-header, m-phone-field, m-otp-group, m-resend-row, a-handle, a-progress, a-btn, a-link.

o-outcome-panel

The centred face a sheet stage wears when the journey has an answer rather than a next step.

LevelOrganism OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/organisms.css StatusStable

Preview

The locked outcome, every slot filledicon, title, body, clock, meta, actions

Sign-in paused until 9:56

Too many wrong codes. Try again in:

14:32

Need to fuel now?

Message my manager

The panel is never a standalone element. Its one layout rule is .o-signup-sheet__stage.o-outcome-panel[data-state="active"], so both classes plus the state have to be on the same element or it does not lay out at all. Drawn here on a plain ground: the real one sits on o-signup-sheet's glass.

Purpose and Usage

Use it when a sheet stage stops being a step. It renders the blocked outcomes and the biometrics offer, which is why it is named for its role rather than for a tone. It used to be called a blocked-panel while also rendering success, which is exactly the naming mistake the system forbids.

Do not use it for a technical fault. That takes the whole frame as o-status-page. Do not use it for an error a driver can correct in place: a wrong code belongs on the field that holds it, with a-helper and a shake, so the correction is where the hands already are.

Anatomy

A centred column of six things, five of them named. An a-icon-wrap--mark leads, then __title, __body, __clock, __meta, __actions. Nothing here is required except the title. The clock and the meta are hidden slots that a definition fills or does not.

The rhythm is one base gap plus a margin on whichever element starts a looser step, so the spacing states the grouping rather than decorating it.

GapRenderedWhy
icon to title24--sp-2 base plus --sp-4 on the title. The icon is the badge for the title, one group away
title to body8base only. A title and the line that completes it are one statement
body to clock8base only. The body ends "Try again in:" and the clock finishes the sentence
clock to meta24base plus --sp-4. A different subject: not the wait, but the way round it
meta to actions8the --sp-6 is cancelled to 0. When the aside is present the button belongs to it
clock to actions, no meta32base plus --sp-6. The panel is one centred group and the action closes it

That last cancellation is written __meta:not([hidden]) + __actions. The :not([hidden]) is load-bearing, because an adjacent sibling selector still matches a sibling that is display: none.

Variants

None. The panel has no modifier. Meaning rides data-tone on the icon it leads with, which is a-icon-wrap's own axis: amber for a pause that ends by itself, red for a dead end, the brand blue for the biometrics offer, because nothing has gone right or wrong yet when a question is being asked.

States

With the aside and without itthe meta slot decides where the button belongs
__meta present

Sign-in paused until 9:56

Too many wrong codes. Try again in:

14:32

Need to fuel now?

Message my manager
__meta hidden

You can sign in again

You can sign in again now.
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Inactiveno data-statedisplay: none, from the stage rule it shares an element with
Activedata-state="active"display: flex, align-items: center with text-align: center, gap: var(--sp-2), padding-top: var(--sp-2), plus stageIn
Aside present__meta without hidden__actions loses its 24px run-in and sits 8 under the aside
Clock absent__clock[hidden]the slot is removed outright, so the body sits 24 above the meta with nothing between
Title focused__title:focus or :focus-visibleoutline and box-shadow set to none, cancelling the system ring
Filling a blocked sheet.o-signup-sheet[data-stage="blocked"] around itflex: 1 plus justify-content: center, so the group centres across the whole sheet

The last one is half of a pair. A driver who cannot get in is given the whole sheet on purpose, at min-height: 62%. Nothing was spending that height: the group stacked from the top then stopped, leaving 135px of panel under the one action the screen has. The height was the right call. The centring is the half that was missing.

The title's ring is suppressed because script focuses it so a screen reader announces the new screen. It is not a tab stop, so no keyboard user ever lands on it by tabbing, then painting a ring around a heading only makes it look like a control.

Behaviour

Entrance is the stage's, not the panel's: stageIn over --dur-sheet on --ease-out, a 10px rise out of transparent. Nothing in the panel animates on its own.

__clock is written once per second by a countdown while a lock runs. It uses font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums, so the digits do not jitter as they change. When the clock runs out the panel is repainted in place: the title, the body and the actions are replaced with the ones that offer the thing the driver wanted, the clock and the meta are hidden.

Opening it releases the keyboard, since a keyboard covering the only way out is worse than no keyboard. Every dismissal route reaches the same one writer, so the grab bar, the scrim and Escape cannot answer the question three different ways.

Content Guidance

Say who did the thing. "Your company paused your access" names the actor, so a driver does not blame the app, the number or the weather. Waiting cannot help there, so no wait is offered.

Say what stopped, precisely. "Sign-in paused" rather than "your number is paused", because a driver told their number is paused will reasonably think their phone line has been cut off.

__meta is a supporting offer rather than the outcome, so it is quieter than both the title and the clock. Offer the one person who can help with the one thing they can actually do. Nobody can shorten a security timer, so the manager is offered for getting the driver fuelling another way in the meantime rather than for lifting the lock.

A reference number rides both the screen and the prepared message, so a manager quotes it to Engen instead of describing symptoms.

Accessibility

  • __title takes tabindex="-1" and is focused by script when the stage becomes an outcome, so the new screen is announced.
  • Its focus ring is suppressed on both :focus and :focus-visible, since it is a heading rather than a control.
  • __clock is aria-hidden. The wait is announced once in words rather than re-read every second for a quarter of an hour.
  • The leading icon is aria-hidden. Its tone is repeated in the title, so colour never carries the meaning alone.
  • The title is an <h3> under the sheet's <h2>, so the heading order holds.
  • Every action is a real a-btn at --size-target or better.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-2the base gap, plus the panel's top padding
--sp-4__title and __meta run-ins, each making 24 with the base
--sp-6__actions run-in, making 32 with the base
--text-heading · --weight-heavy · --tracking-tight__title
--text-caption · --leading-body · --color-text-secondary__body
--text-title · --weight-heavy · --tracking-snug · --leading-tight · --color-text-primary__clock, the largest thing on the panel
--text-caption2 · --weight-medium · --color-text-secondary__meta, quieter than both the title and the number
--space-stack-relatedthe stage's gap, which this panel restates as --sp-2 to the same 8
--dur-sheet · --ease-outthe entrance, borrowed from the stage

The clock takes --text-title at 22px, one step above the 18px title beside it. The number is the thing a driver came back to read, so it is the biggest thing on the panel even though it is not the heading.

Markup Contract

<div class="o-signup-sheet__stage o-outcome-panel" id="stageBlocked">
  <span class="a-icon-wrap a-icon-wrap--lg a-icon-wrap--mark"
        id="blockedIcon" data-tone="error" aria-hidden="true"></span>
  <h3 class="o-outcome-panel__title" id="blockedTitle" tabindex="-1"></h3>
  <p class="o-outcome-panel__body" id="blockedBody"></p>
  <p class="o-outcome-panel__clock" id="blockedClock" aria-hidden="true" hidden></p>
  <p class="o-outcome-panel__meta" id="blockedMeta" hidden></p>
  <div class="o-outcome-panel__actions" id="blockedActions"></div>
</div>

<div class="o-signup-sheet__stage o-outcome-panel" id="stageBiometrics">
  <span class="a-icon-wrap a-icon-wrap--lg a-icon-wrap--mark"
        id="bioIcon" data-tone="info" aria-hidden="true"></span>
  <h3 class="o-outcome-panel__title" id="bioTitle" tabindex="-1">Use Face ID next time</h3>
  <p class="o-outcome-panel__body" id="bioBody">Sign in faster without waiting for a code.</p>
  <div class="o-outcome-panel__actions">
    <button class="a-btn a-btn--primary" type="button" id="bioEnable">Turn on Face ID</button>
    <button class="a-btn a-btn--outline" type="button" id="bioSkip">Not now</button>
    <button class="a-btn a-btn--primary" type="button" id="bioContinue" hidden>Continue</button>
  </div>
</div>

Both classes go on one element. o-signup-sheet__stage alone gives a stage with no centring, o-outcome-panel alone gives a block with no display rule at all. The two markup shapes above differ only in which slots they carry: the blocked panel keeps its clock and its meta as empty hidden nodes, the biometrics offer has neither because nothing there is ever timed.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It reads no media query. Its width comes from o-signup-sheet__body, which tightens its inline padding at 320, so the panel narrows without any rule here knowing it did.

__actions is width: 100% in a column, so a second action stacks rather than sitting beside the first at any width. The clock is the only tabular-numeral line in the flow, so it is the one to check under large text, where a 22px number becomes 24.

Related Components

Parent: o-signup-sheet, which supplies the stage class it shares an element with plus the blocked-sheet centring. Alternative: o-status-page for a technical fault rather than an account outcome. Children it places: a-icon-wrap and a-btn.

o-status-page

A full-frame blocking status with one explanation and one way out.

LevelOrganism OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/organisms.css StatusStable

Preview

__panel, the maintenance statusthe blurred scrim behind it needs the device frame

EngenXT is temporarily unavailable

Please try again after 10:30.

Message my manager

The block itself is position: absolute; inset: 0 with display: none until it has a state, so only the panel can stand on a flat canvas. Behind the panel the real screen is a --color-scrim-deep ground under an 18px backdrop blur, which needs a screen behind it to blur.

Purpose and Usage

Use it whenever the problem is technical: the service is down, or the app on this handset is too old to sign in. It covers the whole frame, states the one fact that changes what a driver does next, offers the one action that helps.

Do not use it for an account outcome. A paused driver or an inactive company is o-outcome-panel inside the sheet, because an account choice is not the answer to a technical fault. Do not put two competing actions on it. Recovery first, escalation second, nothing else.

Anatomy

A centred flex box holding one __panel, which is a full-width white card on --radius-sheet with --sp-7 over --sp-6 of padding, everything in it centred. Inside, in order: __icon, __title, __body, the optional __meta, __actions.

__icon is a plain margin-inline: auto so the mark centres. The panel adds --sp-3 above and below any a-icon-wrap--mark inside it. The mark's halo reaches 34px past its own box, soft to nothing at the edge, so the panel's padding alone left it grazing the top corner and dipping into the title. It carries its own clearance instead, so the light fades in air on both sides.

__meta is a centred row of m-figure separated by --space-section. The panel scopes one rule into the figure: inside this row only, each figure centres its own key over its own value.

__meta, three figuresthe update status, where the numbers are the point

Update required

Signing in needs a newer version than the one on this phone. Use Wi-Fi if you want to save mobile data.

Installed2.4.1 Required2.6.0 Download size42 MB

That centring rule is scoped on purpose. A key-over-value stack is left-aligned so a column of them shares one edge to read down. There is no column here: three figures stand side by side on a panel that centres everything else, each its own island with no neighbour above or below to line up with. The left edge buys nothing there. It costs the obvious thing, since DOWNLOAD SIZE is twice the width of 42 MB, so the number sits off under one end of its own label. The same atom sits in the home notice on the dark hero in a block whose every line is left-aligned, where it is right to stay left.

Variants

None. There is no modifier class on this block. Tone comes from data-tone on the icon, which is a-icon-wrap's axis rather than the page's.

States

One state on the block. Below it, one slot that appears only when it has something in it, plus one focus rule that cancels the system ring.

__meta, present and absentthe only part of the panel that comes and goes
__meta
Installed2.4.1 Required2.6.0
__meta[hidden]
Installed2.4.1
StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Goneno data-statedisplay: none. Absence is gone
Visibledata-state="visible"display: flex, so the scrim, the blur and the centred panel all arrive together
No figures__meta keeps hiddenthe row is removed outright, taking its own margins with it
Title focused__title:focus-visibleoutline and box-shadow are set to none, cancelling the system ring

The focus ring is suppressed on the title alone. Script moves focus there so a screen reader announces the new screen, which is not the same as a driver having navigated to it. A ring drawn round a heading nobody moved to reads as a control. The sibling o-outcome-panel does the same thing for the same reason.

Behaviour

No animation of its own. It appears at --z-overlay, which is 30, above the sheet at 10, so a status raised while a sheet is open covers it rather than fighting it.

When it opens, script sets the hero inert plus aria-hidden, announces the title followed by the body through the live region, then moves focus to the title after 60ms. Closing reverses all three.

The panel is built from a definition rather than from markup. Icon, tone, title, body, figures and actions all come from one object, so a status that exists in the map cannot be missing a way out.

Content Guidance

The body carries the one fact that changes what the driver does next. A closed shop says when it reopens, so maintenance reads "Please try again after 10:30." When there is no recovery time, saying so is the information: "We do not have a recovery time yet." stops a driver waiting on a promise nobody made.

Never claim more than is true. The maintenance copy does not say existing approvals still work, because signing in is how a driver reaches one.

"Update to the latest version" is an instruction with nothing in it. A driver cannot tell whether they already did it last week, cannot say which version they are on when they ring their manager, cannot tell a real prompt from one stuck on screen for a month. So the update status shows both version numbers plus the download size, because a driver on prepaid data decides with the size rather than despite it.

Accessibility

  • The block is role="alertdialog" with aria-labelledby on the title. Script adds aria-modal="true" when it opens, then removes it on close.
  • __title takes tabindex="-1" so script can focus it. It is never a tab stop, so no keyboard user lands on it by tabbing.
  • __icon is aria-hidden. Its tone is repeated by the title and the body, so colour never carries the meaning alone.
  • The hero behind it goes inert plus aria-hidden while it is up.
  • The title and the body are announced once through the live region rather than by a second live role on the panel.
  • Every action is a real a-btn, so it clears --size-target.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--z-overlaystacking, 30, above the sheet
--sp-6the gutter between the panel and the frame edge, plus the panel's inline padding
--color-scrim-deepthe ground behind the panel
--color-surfacethe panel
--radius-sheetthe panel corner, 28px, the same radius the sheet uses
--sp-7the panel's block padding
--sp-3the clearance above and below the icon mark
--text-heading · --weight-heavy · --tracking-tight__title
--sp-4 · --sp-2the title's margins, 16 above and 8 below
--text-caption · --leading-body · --color-text-secondary__body
--sp-5the gap under __body
--space-sectionthe gap between figures in __meta, 24
--sp-2__actions gap

The 18px backdrop blur is a raw value rather than a token. It is the only blur of its kind in the system, unlike the sheet's, which is tokenised as --glass-sheet-bd because two rules need it.

Markup Contract

<div class="o-status-page" id="statusPage" role="alertdialog" aria-labelledby="statusTitle">
  <div class="o-status-page__panel">
    <span class="a-icon-wrap a-icon-wrap--lg a-icon-wrap--mark o-status-page__icon"
          id="statusIcon" data-tone="warning" aria-hidden="true"></span>
    <h2 class="o-status-page__title" id="statusTitle" tabindex="-1"></h2>
    <p class="o-status-page__body" id="statusBody"></p>
    <div class="o-status-page__meta" id="statusMeta" hidden></div>
    <div class="o-status-page__actions" id="statusActions"></div>
  </div>
</div>

The panel ships empty. Title, body, figures and buttons are all written in by script from the status definition, so the markup is a shape rather than a screen. The icon needs all four classes: a-icon-wrap for the box, --lg and --mark for the size and the halo, o-status-page__icon for the centring.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. It reads no media query and no frame attribute. The panel is width: 100% inside a --sp-6 gutter, so it takes whatever the frame gives it at all four device widths.

Under large text every value in the panel grows from the root ramp. The three figures in __meta are the part most likely to run out of room, since they sit in a row with a 24px gap inside a 320px frame.

Related Components

Parent: t-device-frame. It covers o-auth-hero and, when one is open, o-signup-sheet. Alternative: o-outcome-panel for an account outcome rather than a technical fault. Children it places: a-icon-wrap, m-figure, a-btn.

Templates

A template defines layout and content structure. It holds no real content, fetches nothing and knows nothing about application state.

2 exist: the device frame the product is drawn inside, plus the demo page that frame stands on.

t-device-frame

The phone the product is reviewed inside: one box that fixes the screen size, the corner and the shadow.

LevelTemplate OwnershipShared Sourcesrc/css/templates.css StatusStable

Preview

The default frame, 390 × 844empty on purpose: everything you normally see inside it belongs to other components

The frame paints nothing but its own edge. What you can see here is the whole of it: the corner radius, the drop shadow with its 1px hairline, the height a real handset gives you. The black tab at the top is an a-notch inside an m-notch, put there so the next section has something to change.

Purpose and Usage

One per page. It is the screen the product is reviewed inside, so every rule that depends on which handset a driver is holding lives here rather than in the components. A component asks for the space it needs. The frame decides how much space a phone has.

Do not put device rules inside a component. The moment an atom knows it is on a 320 screen, the atom has to be re-tested on four handsets instead of one. The frame reaches down to a descendant by attribute when it must, which keeps that knowledge in a single file you can read end to end.

Do not ship it. It is the review surface, not app chrome. On a real handset the app fills the viewport, which is exactly what the 440px rule under Responsive Behaviour makes it do.

Anatomy

A single element with no parts of its own. Three declarations do the structural work.

DeclarationWhy it is there
position: relativeanchors everything absolute inside it. The notch, the status bar, the scrim, the sheet, the drawn keyboard and the toast are all absolutely positioned. Without this they would anchor to the page instead of to the screen
overflow: hiddenclips those children back to the corner. A sheet that slides up from below starts off the bottom of the screen, not off the bottom of the browser window
flex-shrink: 0stops the page's flex row squeezing it. The frame is the one box on that row whose width is a measurement rather than a preference

Its direct children, in source order, which is also paint order: o-auth-hero, o-status-page, a-scrim, o-signup-sheet, m-keyboard, a-toast, then the u-visually-hidden announcer. Two developer overlays sit after those, outside the product layers on purpose.

Variants

Four sizes, on data-width. They are points, which is what a phone lays out in and what a CSS pixel already is, so these are the handset's real numbers rather than a scaled drawing.

The four sizes, at true scaleeach holds a real notch, so the Android change shows
no attribute · 390 × 844
[data-width="375"] · 375 × 812
[data-width="360"] · 360 × 800
[data-width="320"] · 320 × 720
VariantSizeWhat else changesWhy this size
no attribute390 × 844nothing. This is the base rulethe modern standard iPhone
[data-width="375"]375 × 812size onlythe size a great many designs are still drawn at, also the iPhone SE generation a driver is far more likely to be holding than a new handset
[data-width="360"]360 × 800border-radius drops to 20px. .a-notch becomes a 14px circle with margin-top: 10pxnot an iPhone at all. It is the single most common Android viewport, which for a South African fleet is the one that matters most
[data-width="320"]320 × 720.o-signup-sheet__body takes padding-inline: var(--sp-3). .m-otp-group takes gap: var(--sp-1)not a current phone. It is the floor, kept as a stress test

The 360 case changes hardware, not scale. Same element, other platform: at the Android width the notch becomes a hole-punch and the corners square off a step. A 360-wide frame wearing an iPhone notch was reviewing a phone that does not exist.

The 320 case is the only one that reaches into a component. Six code squares and five gaps cannot each clear the 48px target inside 320, so the row takes the largest size that fits rather than pretending the rule still holds.

States

Two, plus one refinement of the second. None of them can show itself in isolation: every rule under them names a descendant that lives on a screen, so a frame standing on its own has nothing to change. The picture is in o-signup-sheet. The rules are here.

StateHow it is carriedWhat changes
Keyboard up [data-keyboard="open"] shows .m-keyboard. Sizes the sheet to calc(100% - var(--keyboard-inset) - var(--size-status-bar)) on both height and max-height, with a --radius-sheet top corner. Then runs the compression order below
A sheet is open :has(.o-signup-sheet[data-stage]) the hero title takes margin-top: var(--sp-6) and the --text-title ramp. The hero actions go to opacity: 0 with pointer-events: none. The hero lede goes to opacity: 0
The sheet is a dead end :has(.o-signup-sheet[data-stage="blocked"]) the hero title goes to opacity: 0 as well
Tooling suppressed [data-keyboard="open"] .d-demoHint display: none. This one rule lives in developer.css, not here

The sheet states are :has(), so the frame reads the sheet rather than the sheet telling the frame. The button and the help link are what opened the sheet. Leaving them behind it floats a dead control in the middle of the screen. A driver who taps it is tapping the thing they have already done. The lede goes for the same reason: once the sheet explains the step, the lede is the same thing said twice. On blocked the title goes too, because a driver who cannot get in is not being sold anything.

The compression order. The keyboard takes roughly half the screen, so something has to give. It must not be the base gap: the base gap is what says which things belong together. That matters most when there is least room to read it. So the space comes off what the driver has already finished with, in this order.

#Given upRule
1Tooling. It was never product layout.d-demoHint { display: none }
2Orientation. A driver mid-type knows which step they are on, so the step counter is the least useful height on the screen.a-progress { display: none }
3Instruction, but only where it has been acted on. On the number stage the subtitle says which number to use and the driver is now typing it[data-stage="number"] .m-sheet-header__sub { display: none }
4The loose steps__cta and __secondary drop to margin-top: var(--sp-2)
5Whatever is still left over goes to the bottom of the stage, not the end of the column__scroll becomes a flex column, __body and the active stage take flex: 1, __cta takes margin-top: auto, __body takes padding-bottom: max(var(--sp-4), env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px))

Step 3 keeps the code stage's subtitle on purpose. It names the channel and the masked number, which is exactly what a driver checks against their handset mid-code.

Step 5 exists because giving the sheet the full remaining height without giving the stage a way to spend it left the number stage holding 97px of empty sheet under its own button: the one control on the screen, parked at the furthest point from the thumb that is still on the sheet.

Behaviour

Changing data-width transitions width and height over --dur-base with --ease-out, so a size change reads as one phone resizing rather than a second phone arriving. The transition is on the box only. Everything inside relays out at each intermediate width, which is the point: you watch the layout survive every width on the way rather than only the one it lands on.

--keyboard-inset is a custom property the sheet and the keyboard both read. On a device the script sets it from visualViewport and adds data-keyboard="open" once the inset passes 80px. The Keyboard toggle in the demo sets it to 336px flat. Removing the attribute also removes the inline property, so the sheet returns to its own sizing rather than to a stale number.

The sheet is sized against 100% of the frame minus --size-status-bar, so the status bar stays visible with the keyboard up. height is set as well as max-height: a sheet sized to its content left a strip of hero showing between it and the keys. The last row of controls then sat on top of the hero headline.

Content Guidance

It holds no text of its own. What it holds is one screen at a time. Anything that must escape the corner radius cannot: overflow: hidden is absolute, so a tooltip, a menu or a toast has to be built inside the frame rather than layered over it. a-toast is why the toast sits 96px off the bottom instead of floating.

Accessibility

  • It carries no role and no ARIA. It is a layout box, so a screen reader walks straight through it to the screens inside.
  • The landmarks live on its children: <section class="o-auth-hero" aria-label="EngenXT home">, <section class="o-signup-sheet" role="dialog" aria-modal="true">, <div class="o-status-page" role="alertdialog">.
  • The drawn keyboard inside it is aria-hidden. A real keyboard is never in the accessibility tree of the page it covers.
  • overflow: hidden clips, it does not scroll. Anything that has to stay reachable needs a scroller of its own inside the frame, which is what o-signup-sheet__scroll is.
  • The frame does not trap focus. The sheet inside it does. It sets the hero inert while it is open.
  • The announcer is the frame's last product child, so a live message is spoken from inside the screen it describes.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--radius-phonethe corner, 47px, the real radius at 390pt
--shadow-phonethe drop plus a 1px hairline, so the frame reads as hardware on the page
--dur-base · --ease-outthe size transition
--radius-circlethe Android hole-punch at 360
--sp-6the hero title's top margin once a sheet is open
--text-title · --leading-tight · --tracking-snugthe hero title's type once a sheet is open
--sp-2the compressed margin on the sheet's action rows
--sp-4the compressed bottom padding, floored against env(safe-area-inset-bottom)
--sp-3 · --sp-1the 320 overrides: sheet inline padding, code cell gap
--keyboard-inset · --size-status-barthe sheet's height with the keyboard up
--radius-sheetthe sheet's top corner while it is full height

Note what is not tokenised. The four sizes are literals, because 390 and 320 are facts about handsets rather than decisions about this product. The 20px Android corner is a literal for the same reason.

Markup Contract

<div class="t-device-frame" id="deviceFrame">
  <section class="o-auth-hero" id="authHero" aria-label="EngenXT home">…</section>
  <div class="o-status-page" id="statusPage" role="alertdialog" aria-labelledby="statusTitle">…</div>
  <div class="a-scrim" id="sheetScrim"></div>
  <section class="o-signup-sheet" id="sheet" role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-labelledby="sheetTitle">…</section>
  <div class="m-keyboard" aria-hidden="true">…</div>
  <div class="a-toast" id="toast" role="status"></div>
  <span class="u-visually-hidden" id="announcer" role="status" aria-live="polite"></span>
</div>

<div class="t-device-frame" data-width="360">…</div>

<div class="t-device-frame" data-keyboard="open" style="--keyboard-inset: 336px">…</div>

The inline --keyboard-inset is the one thing script writes to the element's style. It is a measurement from the device, not a decision, which is why it arrives as a custom property rather than as a class.

The shared-library gate in tools/check.js compares only the rules whose every class roots into the manifest. That is the frame's own box rule, the four size rules, the 440px rule and the 360 notch rule. Those must be byte-identical between index.html and engenxt-onboarding.html. Everything the frame does to the screens inside it is each file's own business, which is why the keyboard compression order exists in this file only.

Responsive Behaviour

Nothing from the window above 440px. Size is the component's decision, taken from its own attribute, so a wide browser does not make the phone wider. That is the whole argument for the attribute: a reviewer tests a handset, not a window.

Under 440px the frame goes full bleed: width: 100%, height: 100dvh, no radius, no shadow. At that width the browser is a phone, so the drawn phone stops standing in for one. 100dvh rather than 100vh, so it tracks the browser chrome collapsing rather than sitting a toolbar's height too tall.

The tooling overlays that draw inside the frame live in developer-layout.css. It declares @layer templates and loads immediately after templates.css, so those rules keep the exact cascade position they had when the two files were one.

Related Components

Parent: p-signup-demo seats it. In engenxt-onboarding.html the same frame sits inside p-onboard-demo, that file's own page class. Children it holds: o-auth-hero, o-signup-sheet, o-status-page, a-toast, u-visually-hidden. Descendants it reaches by attribute: a-notch, a-progress, m-otp-group, m-sheet-header. The other template in the pair is t-screen, which exists only in engenxt-onboarding.html and is still inline in that page.

p-signup-demo

The page the phone stands on: state navigator on the left, device in the middle, readouts on the right.

LevelPage OwnershipDocumentation harness Sourcesrc/css/templates.css StatusStable

Preview

The page itself cannot be shown inside another page. It is min-height: 100vh and it is the body's only child, so nesting it would fake a viewport rather than show one. What can be shown honestly is its middle column: the stage, holding a frame then the caption.

.p-signup-demo__stagethe frame is at data-width="320" so the preview stays a readable height

Pick a state on the left, or drive the flow with the numbers in the key.

Purpose and Usage

It seats one prototype for review. The middle column is product. The two columns beside it are the harness, which is why they carry d- rather than a level prefix. This page is the only place in the file where the two meet, so the prefix is the border between them.

Left aligned, not centred. The state navigator is the thing you reach for most, so it holds the left edge rather than drifting with the width of the window. The phone then centres in whatever is left. Centring the whole row instead moved the control you use every few seconds every time you resized.

Do not copy it into a product build. Nothing a driver sees is inside it except the frame. If you need a second prototype page, write a second page class beside this one: engenxt-onboarding.html does exactly that with p-onboard-demo, which is the same idea, still inline in that file.

Anatomy

Three columns in one flex row, plus a caption under the middle one.

PartRulesJob
.p-signup-demo min-height: 100vh, flex, align-items: flex-start, justify-content: flex-start, gap: var(--sp-6), padding: var(--sp-8) var(--sp-6) the row. flex-start on both axes is what keeps the nav pinned left and stops a short column stretching to the height of the phone
.p-signup-demo__stage flex: 1 1 auto, min-width: 0, flex column, centred, gap: var(--sp-3) the device column. min-width: 0 lets it shrink below the frame's width instead of forcing the whole row wider
.p-signup-demo__rail flex column, gap: var(--sp-4), align-self: flex-start, position: sticky at var(--sp-8), max-height: calc(100vh - var(--sp-8) * 2) the readouts. Sticky so the eye never leaves the phone to find the answer
.p-signup-demo__caption var(--text-micro), var(--color-text-secondary), centred, max-width: 390px, line-height: var(--leading-body) one line under the phone. 390px is the frame's default width, so the caption never runs wider than the thing it describes

The left column has no class from this component at all. It is .d-stateNav, owned by the developer layer, a real <nav> element. The page positions it by being a flex row, not by styling it.

Readouts belong beside the thing they describe rather than under it. Docking the inspector below the phone made a driver of the reviewer: hovering at the top of the screen, reading at the bottom.

Variants

None. The page has one shape. What changes it is the window, which is under Responsive Behaviour.

States

None. It carries no state attribute of any kind. The demo's own switches live on the root element instead: data-motion, data-text and data-layers are all set on document.documentElement, so they reach the whole document rather than one column of it. The device's own switches, data-width and data-keyboard, sit on t-device-frame.

Behaviour

The rail is sticky at var(--sp-8), so it stays level with the top of the phone while the state list scrolls. Its max-height is set without an overflow rule, so a rail taller than the viewport overflows its own box rather than scrolling inside it. That is a real limit: keep what goes in the rail short enough to fit a laptop screen.

Nothing else on this page moves. It has no transition of its own. The animation you see when the device size changes belongs to the frame.

The caption is rewritten by script as states change, to the state id followed by its label. Its resting text is the instruction below.

Content Guidance

One sentence, at --text-micro, saying what to do next: "Pick a state on the left, or drive the flow with the numbers in the key." It is the only copy the page owns. Once a state is chosen the script replaces it with that state's id and label, so the caption is either an instruction or a location, never both.

Keep it to one line at 390px. It has no truncation, so a long caption pushes the phone up the page.

Accessibility

  • The device column is a real <main>. That makes the phone the page's main landmark, not the tooling around it.
  • The left column is <nav aria-label="Prototype states">. The rail's spec panel is <aside aria-label="Design specification">.
  • The inspector card is aria-hidden="true" in markup and stays that way. It reports whatever the pointer is over, so it has nothing to say to a reader who is not using one.
  • Source order is nav, main, rail. Under 900px the columns stack in that same order, so reading order and visual order never disagree.
  • The caption is a real paragraph after the frame, so it is read after the screen it describes rather than before it.
  • The page sets no focus and traps none. Focus management belongs to the sheet inside the frame.

Tokens

TokenUsed for
--sp-8the page's block padding, the rail's sticky offset, both halves of the rail's max-height
--sp-6the gap between columns, the page's inline padding
--sp-4the rail's own gap, the whole page's padding under 900px
--sp-3the gap between the frame and its caption
--text-microthe caption, 11px
--color-text-secondarythe caption's ink
--leading-bodythe caption's line height

The caption's max-width: 390px is a literal on purpose. It is the frame's default width, so it tracks a hardware fact rather than a spacing decision.

Markup Contract

<div class="p-signup-demo">

  <nav class="d-stateNav" id="stateNav" aria-label="Prototype states">…</nav>

  <main class="p-signup-demo__stage">
    <div class="t-device-frame" id="deviceFrame">…</div>
    <p class="p-signup-demo__caption" id="caption">Pick a state on the left, or drive the flow with the numbers in the key.</p>
  </main>

  <div class="p-signup-demo__rail" id="demoRail">
    <div class="d-inspect" id="inspectCard" aria-hidden="true">…</div>
    <aside class="d-panel" id="specPanel" aria-label="Design specification"></aside>
  </div>

</div>

The page takes no attributes. Three children, in that order, one of them a <main>. The rail is a plain div because it is a column of unrelated panels rather than one region worth naming.

Responsive Behaviour

BreakpointWhat the page does
above 900px three columns. The nav holds the left edge, the phone centres in what is left, the rail sticks
max-width: 900px flex-direction: column, align-items: center, padding: var(--sp-4). The rail goes position: static, max-height: none, width: 100%, align-items: center. It stops being a rail
max-width: 440px padding: 0, gap: 0. The frame's own rule takes it full bleed at the same width, so the phone reaches every edge

The tooling half of both breakpoints is in developer-layout.css, which answers the same two widths for .d-stateNav and .d-inspect. It declares @layer templates and is linked immediately after templates.css, so within that layer it sits exactly where it sat when the rules were interleaved in one file. The test is not that it looks right. It is that the computed-style fingerprint of every element on all 104 states is unchanged.

Related Components

Child: t-device-frame is the only product component on the page. Sibling page: p-onboard-demo, in engenxt-onboarding.html, still inline in that file. Tooling it seats: d-stateNav, d-inspect, d-panel, all in src/css/developer.css, with their breakpoints in src/css/developer-layout.css.

Pages / Screens

A screen is a template with real content in it. Page and screen are one level here, not two. The p- class is the demo harness the phone stands on, not a product surface.

43 states in the sign-up flow and 61 in the app, each with a stable id and a deep link.

How the sign-up surfaces stack

They are not a partition. They stack, so counting the states each one appears in gives a total well above 43. Measured by walking all 43 states in a browser and recording which organisms are visible:

SurfaceTemplatePresent inIs the screen inStatus
o-auth-herot-device-frame432migrated
o-status-paget-device-frame33migrated
o-signup-sheett-device-frame3838migrated

2 + 3 + 38 = 43. The hero sits behind every state. It is the screen in only two of them: #home/default and #home/offline. The status page covers it in three. The sheet covers it in the other thirty-eight.

o-outcome-panel is not a screen. It has no base rule in organisms.css and never sets position. It's a stage inside the sheet's scroll area, laid out by .o-signup-sheet__stage. Across all 43 states it never once appears without o-signup-sheet. It carries 11 of the sheet's 38: the three blocked/, two success/ and six biometrics/ states. It was listed here as a fourth screen on t-device-frame, a containment that does not exist.

The app

Thirteen screens, all in engenxt-onboarding.html. Their styles are still inline in that page, so none of them can be previewed here.

Home

The screen a driver lands on all day: the balance, the vehicle linked this morning and the one control that starts a fill.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4429 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt, styles inline, not migrated

Purpose and User Goal

Home answers two questions in one screen. What have I earned. What do I do next. The balance opens it. The work waits at the thumb.

Every road ends here. Sign-up hands over to it, a returning sign-in lands on it, the scanner returns to it, a live fill sits on it. That is why it carries twelve of the prototype's sixty-one states, more than any other screen.

The driver's goal is a fill. The screen's goal is to state the one precondition before they walk to a pump: a vehicle has to be scanned before fuel can be asked for. It says that in the vehicle card, in the button's own label and in nothing else.

Template

t-screen, at line 796. It gives every screen the same four declarations: position: absolute, inset: 0, display: none until shown, flex-direction: column. Nothing else. Padding, background, the arrival transition and the visibility attribute all belong to o-home.

Visibility is an attribute, not a class. hideAll() at line 5390 sets hidden on all thirteen screens, then showHome() at line 5931 clears it and writes data-state="visible". Arriving from the reward screen writes data-state="arriving" for one frame first, so home fades up.

No preview is rendered for this screen. Its styles are inline, in the single <style> block at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this documentation does not load. A box drawn here would be unstyled markup wearing the screen's class names. That would be a lie about the screen, so there is nothing to look at in this section.

Component Composition

One organism, nine molecules, eight atoms. The tab bar is not among them.

LevelClassWhat it holds
Organismo-homethe screen. Its own parts are __scroll, __greeting, __eyebrow, __name, __balance, __points, __worth, __stats, __stack, __panel, __action, __fuel
Moleculem-app-barthe top row: the lockup, the bell, the avatar
Moleculem-brand-lockupmark plus wordmark, inside the app bar
Moleculem-stat-pillthe per-fill limit, data-tone="success"
Moleculem-linkedthe post-scan band, role="status", shown on that one arrival
Moleculem-vehicle-cardtwo of them: the filled card and its data-state="empty" slot
Moleculem-vehicle-pickerin the markup at line 4507, never rendered. See the note below
Moleculem-list-rowthe Rewards row, data-tile="rewards"
Moleculem-live-barwhat the action becomes while a fill is open
Moleculem-empty-noteno vehicle assigned, with the outline ask
Atoma-avatar#homeAvatar, opens Account
Atoma-badgethe bell dot, up only while a fill is uncollected
Atoma-btn a-btn--primary#homeFuel, the pinned action
Atoma-btn a-btn--outline a-btn--smMessage my manager, inside the empty note
Atoma-icon-btnthe bell
Atoma-icon-wrapthe star on the Rewards row, data-tone="warning"

m-vehicle-picker is dead markup on this screen. paintPicker() at line 5875 hides #homePicker, empties its row, then returns before the code that would fill it. The owner's call is that home never lists the assigned fleet, because the only road to a linked vehicle is scanning the one you are standing at. The count lives on the Account screen instead.

o-tab-bar at line 4622 is a sibling of the screen, not a child. showHome() calls paintTabs('home'), which is the bar's sole writer. Every screen that is not a tab destination passes nothing, so the bar goes down with it.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. App bar. Lockup left. Bell then avatar right, pushed by margin-left: auto.
  2. Greeting. Eyebrow "Good morning," over the h1 name. One sentence broken where it is spoken.
  3. Balance. The figure and its worth as one block: "2 450 pts" over "About 2.45 GB of mobile data earned". A count you cannot price is not information.
  4. Limit pill. "Limit: 60 L per fill", ticked. Tied to the scanned vehicle, so it is absent until one exists.
  5. Linked band. Plate plus "Linked for the rest of today, so fuel when you are ready." Shown only on the arrival that earned it.
  6. The panel. One pane of glass holding the vehicle card, or the dashed empty slot in the same place at the same size. Exactly one of the two is up.
  7. Empty note. "No vehicle assigned." plus the outline ask. Tied to assignment, not to the card.
  8. Rewards row. "Every 1 000 points is 1 GB of mobile data". It says how the number grows, never the number again.
  9. The action. Pinned outside the scroller in o-home__action. Either the primary button or the live bar. Never both.

o-home__stack carries margin-top: auto, so items six to eight ride the bottom of the scrolling column while the bloom shows through the gap above them. The action sits outside the scroller entirely, so a driver at a forecourt never has to scroll to reach it.

The button has one slot with four faces, written by paintFuelAction() at line 6139.

ConditionLabelCarried by
a vehicle is scannedI want to fuelaria-disabled="false"
vehicles assigned, none scannedScan my vehiclearia-disabled="false"
nothing assignedFuel unavailablearia-disabled="true". The fact, in the button, rather than an invitation that fails on press
approval in flightGetting approval…data-state="pending", aria-busy="true", spinner inside. The scroller drops to 0.45 opacity behind it

States

Twelve, from four prefixes. Home is the busiest state target in the file.

State idWhat it shows
homeNew driver, four vehicles assigned, none scanned. Panel up with the empty slot. No limit pill, because the pill is tied to the scanned vehicle. Button reads "Scan my vehicle". The only home state that runs the skeleton fetch, since a new driver is the only one whose list, entitlement and balance have never been fetched.
home-few-vehiclesThe same frame for a returning driver with four assigned. No fetch, so no skeleton sweep. It wears the same rail label as home.
returning-signinSigned in again. Three vehicles assigned, none scanned yet today. The landing for route/returning.
returning-without-vehicleNothing assigned at all. The panel is gone, the empty note is up, the button reads "Fuel unavailable" and is announced disabled.
returning-with-vehicleOne vehicle, scanned yesterday and again today. Card filled, limit pill up, button reads "I want to fuel". No linked band, because this is not the arrival from the scan.
flow1-linkedBack from the camera. Same as above plus the m-linked band naming the plate. The greeting is replaced by the linked announcement.
flow1-open-linkedOpening the app with today's vehicle already linked. openApp() at line 6248 skips the gate and lands here.
fuel-requestingApproval in flight. The wait happens inside the control that started it: spinner in the button, everything above dimmed and inert.
fuel-liveThe live bar in the action slot. "Fuel approved", "CA 123-456 · up to 60 L", call to action "Show fuel code".
fuel-fillingBar at data-phase="filling". "Fuelling", "CA 123-456 · 31.4 L so far", call to action "View live fill". Frozen mid-pour on purpose.
fuel-doneBar at data-phase="done". "Fill complete", 52.4 L, "See points". The bell dot appears. m-push is laid over the screen rather than replacing it.
fuel-stoppedThe same frame reading the partial fill: "Fill stopped early", 23.1 L of the approved 60. Bar, notification, points and receipt all read the same two facts.

fuel-done and fuel-stopped land here with m-push on top. The push is the phone's chrome, not an app screen, so hideAll() deliberately leaves it alone.

Responsive Behaviour

No breakpoint of its own. Home carries no data-width rule, unlike the check screen at line 2877 or the fuel screen at line 3171. It holds one column at every frame size the product targets: 390×844, 375×812, 360×800 and the 320×720 floor.

What absorbs the difference is the split between the scroller and the action. o-home__scroll takes flex: 1 1 auto with min-height: 0 and scrolls. o-home__action takes flex-shrink: 0 outside it. On a short frame the column above compresses by scrolling. The button does not move.

Both edges of the scroller are masked. The bottom fade reads as "more below" instead of a cut. The top fade dissolves content before it reaches the phone's own clock at --size-status-bar, 78px.

Large text is the real width test. [data-text="large"] repoints the whole ramp, so --text-display goes 30px to 34px and --text-caption goes 13px to 15px. Every block on this screen reads tokens, so all of it grows together.

The page breakpoints at 1100px, 900px and 440px govern the demo furniture around the phone. None of them reaches inside the frame.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="homeName". The h1 carries tabindex="-1" and takes focus 60ms after arrival, so a screen reader starts at the driver's name.
  • Its focus ring is suppressed. The move was made by script, not by the driver navigating, so a visible ring would report something that did not happen.
  • The greeting is announced at 500ms through #announcer, an aria-live="polite" region. Behind the focus move, never on the same tick, because a focus change flushes a pending polite message.
  • The announced sentence changes with the state. Arriving from a scan it becomes "Vehicle linked." plus the plate, because news beats a greeting.
  • The balance opens with a u-visually-hidden "Your balance.", so the figure is not read as a bare number.
  • The vehicle card composes one label: "Driving today, CA 123-456, Ford Transit Custom 2.0TDCi. Scan a different vehicle." Three lines read once, with the way out named.
  • The live bar composes its label per phase, so the dot, the title and the call to action are one sentence rather than three fragments.
  • Mid-fill litres are written into the bar's text but not re-announced per tick. Forty updates of one number is shouting.
  • #homeFuel uses aria-disabled, never the disabled attribute, so "Fuel unavailable" stays in the focus order and can be heard.
  • The bell dot is aria-hidden and only present while a fill is uncollected. Every decorative SVG on the screen carries the same attribute.
  • The linked band is role="status", so it reports itself when it appears.
  • Under forced-colors: active the disabled button keeps its opacity and the current tab restates its colour as an underline.
  • Under reduced motion the invite ring is removed rather than frozen. An invitation held at its first frame is a permanent blue band round the button.
  • Known defect. #homeAvatar at line 4445 still carries data-unbuilt="Your account". Its handler at line 8782 opens the Account screen before the fallback that reads the attribute, so nothing announces "not built yet". The attribute is stale and should come out.

Scan the disc or plate

The camera reads whatever the driver points it at, names what it found and says what to do when it cannot.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4682 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt, styles inline, not migrated

Purpose and User Goal

A scan is the only thing that links a vehicle. Picking a plate off a list proves nothing about where the driver is standing. Reading something off the vehicle proves they are at it, which is the whole reason this step exists. confirmByScan() at line 5917 is the single writer of today's vehicle and only a completed read reaches it.

The driver used to say first whether it was the disc or the plate, on a pair of chips. That was a question the app could answer and the driver could not: which reads faster depends on glare, angle and how faded the disc is. The camera now takes whatever is put in front of it. Aiming at the other one is just moving the phone.

The second job is failure. A cracked lens, a dark bay, a plate behind glare or a refused camera permission are all ordinary in a yard. None of them may be the thing that stops somebody fuelling, so every face here keeps a way through.

Template

t-screen, at line 796: absolute, inset zero, hidden until shown, a column. o-scan at line 1948 adds the dark ground (--color-scan-1) with --color-text-on-dark ink, because the controls have to sit on whatever the lens is pointed at.

showScan(face, kind) at line 6845 is the only door in. It clears timers, runs hideAll(), flips the status bar to data-tone="onDark", sets S.scanFace, then hands off to paintScan() at line 6671 which decides every word on the screen. kind exists for the state rail alone. In the flow the camera decides.

No preview is rendered for this screen. Its styles are inline, in the single <style> block at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this documentation does not load. A dark viewfinder cannot be drawn from class names alone, so anything shown here would misrepresent the screen.

Component Composition

One organism, one molecule, four atoms. The lightest composition of any screen in the file bar the live pump, which carries no molecule at all.

LevelClassWhat it holds
Organismo-scanthe screen. Its parts are __view, __bar, __spacer, __stage, __typePanel, __typeLabel, __typeInput, __typeErr, __actions
Moleculem-scan-notethe title and body that name what went wrong. role="status". Parts __title, __body
Atoma-scan-framefour corners plus a-scan-frame__line, the sweep
Atoma-shutterthe driver's own capture. Parts __ring, __label
Atoma-icon-btntwo, both data-tone="onDark": close and torch
Atoma-btn a-btn--primarythree, one at a time: Try again, Open phone settings, Use this vehicle
Atoma-btn a-btn--outlineOnDarktwo: Enter the number plate instead, Ask your manager

o-scan__view is the drawn camera plane: a radial highlight over a two-stop vertical gradient, --color-scan-2 to --color-scan-1. It is aria-hidden and carries no content.

One frame serves both targets. A disc is a circle behind glass at roughly 1:1 and a plate is roughly 4.6:1, so the 3:2 frame is snug on neither. Corners rather than a closed box for that reason: they mark the middle of the picture without claiming to be a crop either target has to fill.

The tab bar is absent. hideAll() calls paintTabs(null) and showScan() never calls it back, so a flow screen wears no navigation it does not own.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. The bar. Close left, torch right, a flexed spacer between them. Both on-dark icon buttons.
  2. The stage. A grid with place-items: center taking all remaining height. Inside it the frame, then the note beneath.
  3. The frame. The only bright thing on the screen. It says where to aim without a sentence.
  4. The note. Title then body, under the frame and never over it. Covering the thing a driver is aiming at is the opposite of helping.
  5. The shutter. Between the stage and the actions. Revealed only after two failed reads.
  6. The type panel. Replaces the shutter when the camera itself is unreachable. Label, field, error line.
  7. The actions. One column, always. Primary first, then the on-dark outlines.

Every word and every button is decided by paintScan(). Three things can override the note, checked in the order they become true: what was read, whether the lamp is on, whether the driver has run out of road.

RuleEffect
struggling, two or more failed facesthe shutter appears, the retry button goes
spent, four unreadable readsthe note stops recommending what has already been tried twice. The manager takes the lead through data-lead="true"
hardFail, permission refusedsettings plus type-instead. No manager, because a permission lives on this phone
lightIt, dark with the lamp offthe primary becomes "Turn on the torch", in the corner the hand is already in
douseIt, glare with the lamp onthe primary becomes "Turn the torch off". The button that caused it undoes it
notyoursthe primary reads "Scan again". No shutter: the camera did its job, so a steadier hand returns the same answer

Only one way to start another read is ever on screen. Before the second failure that is Try again. After it, the shutter, which is the same offer with the driver picking the moment.

States

Seventeen, the densest screen in the file. It is also the only one driven by a single unmixed prefix, flow1-scan-. Two read faces plus fifteen outcomes.

State idWhat it shows
flow1-scan-ready"Line up the licence disc or number plate" over "Hold still when it is inside the frame." The sweep runs. The only face that starts a read: beginRead() at line 6893 decides at 1700ms, then links at 1100ms more.
flow1-scan-reading"Reading the licence disc…" over "Keep the phone steady." Sweep still running, frozen there for review.
flow1-scan-plateThe other half of the same rule: "Reading the number plate…". Both halves are states because the point of the design is that the camera reports rather than the driver choosing.
flow1-scan-glare"Too much glare" over "Tilt the phone slightly, or move so the light is not straight on." Sweep stopped. Primary "Try again".
flow1-scan-dark"Too dark to read" over "Turn the torch on, or move somewhere lighter." Primary becomes "Turn on the torch", data-act="torch".
flow1-scan-blur"Image is blurry" over "Hold still for a moment." Primary "Try again".
flow1-scan-smear"The camera lens may be dirty" over "Wipe it with a clean, soft cloth." A sibling of blur, not a wider blur: the set is organised by remedy.
flow1-scan-dark-litThe torch is on and it is still too dark. Body switches to "Move closer, or bring the vehicle into light." The primary drops back to "Try again", so the words no longer repeat an instruction already followed.
flow1-scan-glare-torchThe state the torch creates. A plate is retroreflective and the lamp sits beside the lens. Body reads "The torch is bouncing back off the glass." Primary "Turn the torch off".
flow1-scan-nocam"Camera is unavailable" over "Close other camera apps, then try again." Retry present, shutter absent: a button cannot produce a camera that is not there.
flow1-scan-denied"Camera access is off". The only true dead end. Primary "Open phone settings" with outline "Enter the number plate instead". The manager button is hidden.
flow1-scan-unreadTwo failures deep. "We could not read it" over "Try the other one. The disc and the plate both work." The shutter is up, the retry is gone.
flow1-scan-spentFour unreadable reads. "We still cannot read it" over "Enter the number plate instead, or message your manager." The manager button takes the primary coat through data-lead.
flow1-scan-offline"Internet connection needed" over "We need Wi-Fi or mobile data to check this vehicle. Try again when you have signal." The root gets data-net="offline", so the status bar reads No service. No shutter, no promise about time.
flow1-scan-notyoursA correct read of a vehicle the driver is not assigned. The body names the registration it read, "We read NP 402-118", so a transposed OCR character can be told from a real refusal in about a second.
flow1-scan-typeThe panel is up, the field is focused at 60ms, the primary reads "Use this vehicle". The frame stays put with its sweep stopped. The shutter is down. Typing a plate that is not assigned fills the error line rather than linking anything.
flow1-scan-manualThe blur face two failures in, so the shutter is showing. Its label reads "Line up the disc or the plate and press when it looks clear", naming both, because the app has just admitted it cannot tell them apart.

Two counters, not one. S.scanTries counts every failed face. S.unreadTries counts only unreadable reads. One sun flare followed by two failed reads must not tell a driver their vehicle is unreadable on the strength of the weather.

Responsive Behaviour

No data-width rule. The screen fits four frame sizes on flex alone: 390×844, 375×812, 360×800 and the 320×720 floor.

The bar and the actions are flex-shrink: 0. The stage is flex: 1 1 auto, so a shorter phone takes its height out of the viewfinder rather than out of a control.

The frame is width: 100% with max-width: 300px at aspect-ratio: 3 / 2. The note caps at the same 300px, so the two stay the same width down to the floor. Below 300px plus padding they shrink together.

The actions are one column at every size, deliberately. Side by side at 320dp gives each button about 140dp and a wrapped label on each.

Under reduced motion the sweep stops and parks at the midline instead of vanishing, so the frame still reads as a scanner. Both prefers-reduced-motion and the in-page [data-motion="reduced"] switch are honoured.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="scanNoteTitle". The screen's name is whatever the camera currently says, which is the only honest label for it.
  • The note title carries tabindex="-1" and takes focus 60ms after arrival. Its focus ring is suppressed because the move is script-made, not navigated.
  • m-scan-note is role="status", so a repaint inside it reports itself. beginRead() announces nothing for exactly that reason. Doubling matters most on the one screen where a driver is listening for a single sentence.
  • showScan() does announce at 500ms, because it un-hides the region rather than changing text inside one already on screen, which a live region does not reliably catch.
  • What is announced is read off the painted screen, not off the face table. Built from the table, the reading state said "Reading…" aloud while the screen named the disc.
  • The torch is aria-pressed on a real button, so its on-state is a state and not a colour. Under forced-colors: active it also takes an outline, because the on-state was drawn as an inversion.
  • Pressing the torch announces "Torch on." or "Torch off." and restarts the read when the lamp was the fix, so the control never leaves a driver with a lit bay and a screen that has not noticed.
  • The camera plane, the frame corners, the sweep line and the shutter ring are all aria-hidden. Only the shutter's own label is readable.
  • The type panel uses a real <label for>. Its error is a paragraph toggled by hidden, cleared on the next keystroke. Enter in the field fires the primary.
  • Every action is a real <button> at --size-action, 52px, above the --size-target floor of 48px.
  • Never more than one primary. The stacking rule exists so a driver on a 320dp screen does not have to read three buttons to learn the first two are the same idea.
  • data-lead="true" promotes the manager button in place rather than swapping the two, so the safest recovery never moves under the thumb.

Step

One shell for four asks: confirm your account, link a vehicle, allow location, enter the kilometre reading.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4647 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt, styles inline, not migrated

Purpose and User Goal

Four questions share one shape: a mark, a heading, a line of body copy, an optional thing to fill in, a column of actions. So they share one organism rather than being four screens whose margins drift apart.

The driver's goal on each is to get past it. Nothing here is a destination. The screen exists to collect the one fact the next screen needs, then leave.

The four are defined as data in STEPS at line 6383, not as markup. A 21-line block absorbs ten states because the shell is empty until a step fills it.

KeyLineAsks for
odometer6397the dashboard reading, which is what a fleet reconciles litres against distance with
account6453the screen lock, only when something has changed or a code is about to be issued
vehicle6476the camera. It lists what is allowed but links nothing
location6536the phone's location, asked after the vehicle rather than before it

Template

t-screen, at line 796. o-step at line 1801 adds the page ground plus a two-stop tonal wash on ::before: no blur, no repaint, because this screen is sized for a 320dp Android Go phone.

showStep(key) at line 6915 is the only door in. It publishes the key as data-step on the section, so the layout can respond. That attribute carries the one real variant: o-step[data-step="account"] centres the column, haloes the mark and grows its icon to 34px. Identity is a ceremony. The other three keep the task voice with a left edge.

No preview is rendered for this screen. Its styles are inline, in the single <style> block at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this documentation does not load. The shell is also empty in the markup: title, body, group and every button label are written by showStep. A static preview would be a blank frame twice over.

Component Composition

One organism, one molecule injected by a step builder, five atoms. Twenty-one lines of markup, one of the smallest blocks in the file.

LevelClassWhat it holds
Organismo-stepthe screen. Its parts are __scroll, __bar, __mark, __shackle, __title, __body, __group, __field, __err, __label, __note, __actions, __tertiary
Moleculem-vehicle-pickerbuilt into #stepGroup by the vehicle step at line 6517, holding a-vehicle-chip rows
Atoma-icon-btn#stepBack, the chevron in the bar
Atoma-btn a-btn--primary#stepGo. Its label comes from the step
Atoma-btn a-btn--outline#stepAlt, hidden unless the step defines an alternative
Atoma-quiet-link#stepBackLow, the second exit where a keyboard covers the first
Atoma-vehicle-chipone per assigned vehicle, inside the picker. Each is a button to the camera

o-step__mark holds one of four inline SVGs: a gauge, a lock, a van or a pin. They are constants, not an atom, because nothing else in the file uses them.

Dead markup. #stepThird at line 4665, class o-step__tertiary, is wired to def.third. No step in STEPS defines third, so the element is never shown. It has a full CSS rule at line 1926.

The tab bar is absent here. hideAll() calls paintTabs(null), so a flow screen wears no navigation it does not own.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. The bar. A back chevron alone, at 48px minimum height. Always shown, because every step is entered from home.
  2. The mark. Centred by the owner's call. It is the screen's emblem on every step, not a bullet point.
  3. The title. The h1, tabindex="-1", written by the step.
  4. The body. One line saying where to look or what happens next.
  5. The group. Whatever this step needs: a field with its error line, a list of chips, or nothing at all.
  6. The actions. Primary, then outline, then the quiet Back, then the unused tertiary.

The vertical rhythm is two auto margins. o-step__mark takes margin-top: auto and o-step__group takes margin-bottom: auto, so a short step centres in the room it is given. The margins collapse to nothing when a step carries a full list, so the tall steps keep their layout.

Labels by step, all supplied from STEPS.

StepPrimaryAlternative
odometerContinue, or "Use this reading" once a low reading is armednone. The quiet Back is shown instead
accountConfirm with screen lockSend me a WhatsApp code instead
vehicleOpen cameraNot at the vehicle yet
locationUse my locationSearch instead

Both location answers carry on to the map. The driver is only here because they pressed the button that leads there, so refusing the phone's location refuses the sorting, never the stations.

States

Ten, from three prefixes. Second only to the scanner.

State idWhat it shows
signup-linkThe vehicle step standing between sign-up and the reward. A new driver with three vehicles assigned. afterScan is set to the reward, so linking is the first act of the app rather than a celebration a driver can stop in.
flow1-account"Confirm your account" over "Use your phone's screen lock, or a code we send you." The ceremony face: centred column, haloed lock, 34px icon.
flow1-vehicle"Link the vehicle you're driving today" with three chips under "Vehicles you may drive". Primary "Open camera".
flow1-vehicle-oneThe same step with one vehicle assigned. One van is still one scan. Being assigned a vehicle is not being in it.
flow1-location"Find approved stations near you". Empty group, so the two auto margins centre the mark, the heading and the body in the column.
flow1-open-freshOpening the app with nothing linked today. openApp() at line 6248 sends the driver to the vehicle step rather than to home.
fuel-odometer"Enter the kilometre reading" over "Find this number on the dashboard. Next: your fuel code." Field seeded with the driver's last filed reading, 84 230. The only step showing the quiet Back, because the only one that raises a keyboard.
fuel-odometer-emptyThe field cleared, then onGo pressed through code. The error line reads "Type the km number first." at data-tone="error", the field takes data-state="error" and focus returns to it.
fuel-odometer-low61 000 typed against a last fill of 61 220 km on CA 123-456. The line reads at data-tone="warning" rather than error. The primary relabels to "Use this reading". Warned, never refused, with the exception recorded on the slip.
fuel-confirm-youThe account step standing in front of a fill rather than starting a session, so the title becomes "Confirm it's you" and the body says the fuel goes on the company's account. The button presses itself at 620ms, because the phone is already unlocked in the driver's hand.

The warning is never raised against a value the app itself prefilled. The field is seeded with the last filed reading, so an untouched field on a second fill would otherwise warn about a number nobody typed.

Back means home for every step except one. stepBackTo is set by the check screen, so a driver at a pump who backs out of "Confirm it's you" returns to the check rather than losing the station, the check and the walk back.

Responsive Behaviour

No data-width rule. The screen is designed at the floor and grows from there: 16dp margins, 16sp body copy, 48dp minimum targets, one column of actions. It holds at 390×844, 375×812, 360×800 and 320×720.

o-step__scroll is flex: 1 1 auto with min-height: 0. o-step__actions is flex-shrink: 0 outside it, so the buttons never scroll away under a raised keyboard. The scroller's top is masked so content dissolves before it reaches the phone's clock.

Actions stay one column at every size, deliberately. Two buttons side by side at 320dp gives each about 140dp and a two-line label on each.

The vehicle step's list changes shape by fleet size rather than by screen width. Six or fewer and the chips are drawn. More than six and it prints the count instead, because twelve arbitrary plates out of a thousand are a tease rather than a reference.

Large text repoints the ramp through [data-text="large"], so --text-lead goes 20px to 22px and the whole screen grows with it. The auto margins absorb the extra height before the scroller has to.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="stepTitle", so the screen is named by whichever question it is currently asking.
  • The title carries tabindex="-1" and takes focus 60ms after arrival. Its focus ring is suppressed, because the move is script-made rather than navigated.
  • The title plus the body are announced together at 500ms through #announcer. Behind the focus move, never on the same tick.
  • The odometer field carries its own aria-label, "Kilometre reading, from the dashboard", plus aria-describedby="stepErr", so the warning is bound to the field rather than floating near it.
  • Its inputmode="numeric" raises the number pad. enterkeyhint="done" names the key. Enter in the field fires the primary.
  • The empty field also takes aria-invalid="true". The low reading does not: a reading below the last one may well be right, since a replaced cluster reads from zero, so that case argues in words rather than marking the field.
  • The low-reading warning is announced in full, with "km" spoken as "kilometres" and the second press named: "Press Use this reading to carry on."
  • An alert glyph rides with every message, because tone is never colour alone. Two tones on one element. data-tone="error" against --color-error-text and data-tone="warning" against --color-warning, both chosen at 4.7:1 on the composited sheet.
  • Two exits, one meaning. The chevron sits in the bar. The quiet Back appears only on the odometer step, where a keyboard covers the top of the screen. It clicks the chevron rather than duplicating its logic.
  • Escape reaches the same control: the key handler at line 8515 clicks #stepBack whenever the step screen is up.
  • Chips announce the whole of what they do: "CA 118-402. Scan the vehicle you are standing at." A bare plate would promise a selection this cannot make.
  • The chip row is role="list", never a radiogroup. There is no aria-checked anywhere in it, because nothing here chooses a vehicle.
  • The lock shackle opens over 350ms as felt confirmation before the next screen says it. Under reduced motion the open frame shows at once.
  • The two a-btn actions clear --size-action, 52px. The quiet Back clears --size-target, 48px. The bar holds 48px on its own.
  • Under forced-colors: active the refused field loses its red border and its fill, so it restates the refusal as a dashed outline. The sentence beneath survives on its own words.

Stations map and sheet

The map says where a station is. The sheet says which one it will be.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4751 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt and reachable. Styles inline, not migrated to linked CSS

Purpose and User Goal

A fuel code is issued against one station, so the station is chosen before the QR exists. The driver's goal here is to pick a forecourt their company pays for.

Only approved stations are drawn. Unapproved ones stay in the STATIONS data because the check screen needs one to tell its story, but no list, no pin and no search result shows them. The filter at line 7045 is the owner's launch law.

The map is the picture. The list is the control. On a phone held in one hand the list is the half a thumb reaches, so the decision is made inside the sheet rather than on a card floating over the map. The plan is drawn as an SVG rather than fetched: a tile server is an external request this prototype cannot make over file://.

Template

t-screen (CSS at line 796). Absolute, inset: 0, display: none until data-state is set to arriving or visible. It fills the t-device-frame and sits above everything but the phone's own chrome.

No preview is rendered here, on purpose. Every rule that styles this screen lives in the single inline <style> at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html. This page does not load that stylesheet, so a preview would be unstyled markup pretending to be a screen. Three of the five molecules are also injected at runtime, so the static markup alone is an empty sheet.

The tab bar is not present. hideAll calls paintTabs(null) and showStations never calls it back, so o-tab-bar is hidden for the whole of this screen. setStatusTone(false) keeps the status bar's default dark ink.

Component Composition

Organism .o-stations (CSS 2101). Its own elements: __map, __plan, __pins, __bar, __filters, __locate, __sheet, __detail, __read, __act, __browse, __vehicle, __list, __empty, __emptyTitle, __emptyActions.

ClassLevelWhere it comes from
m-searchMoleculestatic markup, CSS 1480
