Content and Action Hierarchy

The sheet has two faces that never show together. paintPeek sets data-peek="true" on the organism, unhides __detail and hides __browse (lines 7146 to 7148). hidePeek reverses all three (7193 to 7195). The list is the alternatives. While a station is open the alternatives are not what is being looked at.

Reading order on the browse face:

  1. The map plane, with the back chevron and the three sort chips floating on it rather than sitting in a band above it.
  2. The locate control, bottom right of the map.
  3. The sheet, its handle, then the vehicle line: "For CA 123-456 · Ford Transit Custom".
  4. m-search, placeholder "Search suburb or station".
  5. The station list, one m-station per approved forecourt: name, note, distance.
  6. o-stations__empty when nothing matches, carrying "Search another area" and "Message my manager".

On the detail face:

  1. m-station-head: the pump mark, the name, then the status line. Whether the company pays comes first, because it is the only line here that decides anything.
  2. The street, the pump count, the distance, then the a-shut chevron back to the list.
  3. m-deals, hidden when the station has none, noted as "You pay for these yourself, not your company."
  4. Primary "Choose this station" over outline "Get directions", pinned below the scroller so choosing never scrolls out of reach.

Tapping a pin opens the station. It does not choose it. Committing is a second, deliberate press on the primary, which hands the id to showCheck after a 160ms beat.

States

Four statestwo prefixes, flow1- and fuel-
State idWhat it shows
flow1-mapThe map reached from the linking flow, after the disc is read. Browse face, nothing chosen, home standing underneath.
fuel-stationsThe returning driver choosing where to fuel. Same browse face, active vehicle forced to the first one.
fuel-stations-searchedThe search box holds "zzz". paintStations matches nothing, so the list empties and o-stations__empty shows "No company-approved stations nearby" over its two small outline buttons.
fuel-station-openA station opened on the map. chooseStation('fourways') has run, so the detail face is up, the map has panned to bring the pin clear, the sheet has taken 72 per cent.

Responsive Behaviour

Keyed off data-width on .t-device-frame, never a media query. The phone here is a div inside a desktop page, so a max-height media query reads the browser window. The rule was written, shipped and did nothing (comment at 2367).

  • The sheet caps at 54 per cent, rising to 72 per cent under data-peek="true".
  • On data-width="320" the peeked sheet gives back to 52 per cent, so a strip of map wide enough to move a pin into survives.
  • The pin band starts at a measured 144px at every width, because the filter chips are the same height at every width.
  • The chip row scrolls sideways. The station list scrolls down inside o-stations__browse. The detail scrolls inside o-stations__read while o-stations__act stays pinned.
  • Pins leaving the top of the band dissolve into a mask rather than sliding under the chips.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="stationsTitle". That h1, "Choose a station", is u-visually-hidden and does double duty as the radiogroup's label.
  • o-stations__list is role="radiogroup". Each m-station is role="radio" with aria-checked and a roving tabindex, so one row holds the tab stop.
  • Pins are real buttons with aria-pressed and a name that includes the station and the distance in kilometres.
  • Focus lands on the back button 60ms after arrival. Opening a station moves it to m-station-head__name, which carries tabindex="-1" with its ring suppressed.
  • announce() writes to the polite #announcer: "Choose a station" plus how many are approved, then the opened station's name, status and distance.
  • Escape closes the detail first. Only when the list is already showing does it leave the screen.
  • The locate control answers in words instead of doing nothing: reply() raises the toast and the announcement together.
  • The plan SVG, the a-here dot and every icon are aria-hidden.
  • Reduced motion cancels detailRise and the arriving translate.

Check before the code

Five questions, asked before every fill, answered the same way whether the answer is yes or no.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4855 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt and reachable, kept by owner decision. Styles inline, not migrated

Purpose and User Goal

It sits between the map and the QR. It answers one question: will this fill be paid for. It answers it either way.

The five rows are the rules every fill is measured against. A driver who reads the same five, every fill, learns the system without ever being taught it. That is why the screen stays in the journey even when everything passes, which is a locked owner call.

The verdict speaks about the fill rather than about who settles the account. An earlier version read the station alone, so a driver blocked by the hours or by the account was told "Your company pays here" above a red row. The words caught up with the logic in showCheck at line 7253.

Template

t-screen (CSS at line 796). Absolute, inset: 0, display: none until data-state is set. The organism is a flex column: o-check__scroll takes the height and o-check__action stays outside it at the bottom.

No preview is rendered here. Every rule that styles this screen lives in the single inline <style> at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this page does not load, so a preview would be unstyled markup pretending to be a screen. The five check rows are also built at runtime by paintGate, so the static markup holds an empty #checkList.

hideAll runs on the way in and calls paintTabs(null), so o-tab-bar is hidden here. setStatusTone(false) keeps the status bar's default ink.

Component Composition

Organism .o-check (CSS 2820). Its own elements: __scroll, __bar, __station, __km, __why, __alts, __alt, __altName, __altKm, __facts, __lead, __checks, __action. It carries data-verdict, either clear or block.

ClassLevelWhere it comes from
m-verdictMoleculestatic markup, with __mark. CSS 2955
m-factMoleculetwo static rows, with __name, __value, __sub. CSS 3042
m-checkMoleculeinjected at line 7416, with __mark, __body, __title, __sub. CSS 2640
a-btn--primary · a-btn--outlineAtomstatic, in o-check__action. CSS 530
a-icon-btnAtomthe back chevron. CSS 1210

o-check__alt is an organism element rather than a molecule. It is one button drawn from the alternatives list at line 7294. It exists only on a station refusal.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. The back chevron in o-check__bar, alone on its row.
  2. m-verdict: a mark and a word. "You can fuel here" or "You cannot fuel here". When the station itself is the failure, the headline becomes "This station is no longer approved", because a generic refusal reads wrong over a station that was approved when the driver chose it.
  3. The h1, the station name, at --text-display. It is the answer to the question the map asked, so it is the biggest thing here.
  4. o-check__km, the distance.
  5. o-check__why, hidden on a pass. A refusal that does not say what to do next is a dead end.
  6. o-check__alts, only on a station refusal: the two nearest approved doors, each tapping straight back into this same screen.
  7. o-check__facts, one card of two m-fact rows. Vehicle, with the plate over the model, then Fuel, "Up to 60 L". These are declarations, not tests, so they are not ticked.
  8. The lead "Checked before every fill" over o-check__checks.
  9. o-check__action, outside the scroller: primary "Show fuel code for CA 123-456" over outline "Choose another approved station".

The verdict is carried twice on purpose. The screen wears it because the wash behind the whole check is the screen's. The pill wears it because a pill's colour is the pill's business, not something its parent reaches in to paint.

The five checks come from fuelChecks at line 7346: company list, company account, fuelling hours, your vehicle, litres for this fill. Rows land one at a time, 190ms apart, after a 300ms hold that lets the header finish arriving. The run stops at the first refusal. Every row after it takes data-outcome="skipped", swaps its tick for a dash, then has its title replaced by its short name with the sub "Not checked yet". A row that never ran must not keep a sentence claiming an answer.

The account row never shows a number and never uses the word funds. A driver has no business seeing what their employer holds. They cannot act on it. Telling them turns a company matter into an argument at a forecourt.

The primary is never a dead end. With no vehicle confirmed it becomes "Scan your vehicle" and opens the scanner with a return hook, so the driver lands back on this check rather than on home.

States

Four statesthe pass and its three refusals
State idWhat it shows
fuel-checkThe pass. All five rows tick, data-verdict="clear" lights the green wash, no why line, no alternatives, primary "Show fuel code for CA 123-456".
fuel-check-no-accountS.account = 'blocked'. Row one ticks, row two fails, rows three to five go skipped. Why line: "Your manager can get this going again. Nothing is wrong with your account."
fuel-check-hoursS.hours = 'closed'. Rows one and two tick, row three fails. Why line names the window: "Your company allows Monday to Saturday, 05:00 to 19:00."
fuel-check-blockedshowCheck('randburg'), a station off the list. Row one fails, so nothing after it is asked, the headline becomes "This station is no longer approved" and o-check__alts offers the two nearest approved stations.

Responsive Behaviour

  • One width rule. On data-width="320" the scroller's gap tightens from --sp-2 to --sp-1h, because that frame has 124px less height than 390 and was given exactly the same rhythm.
  • The station name is --text-display rather than the hero size. At 42px the longest name wrapped to two lines on a 320dp phone, costing 55px on the width with the least room to give.
  • o-check__why caps at 34ch. m-fact rows wrap, with the sub line taking the full width.
  • o-check__action is outside the scroller and adds env(safe-area-inset-bottom) to its padding. Both buttons keep their own heights, so only the bar's padding gives.
  • The two verdict washes are 44 per cent tall and fade out by mid-screen, so nothing that reads sits on less contrast than it was measured for.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="checkStation", so its name is the station. Focus lands on that h1 60ms after arrival. It carries tabindex="-1" with its focus ring suppressed.
  • announce() fires 500ms later, behind the focus move rather than racing it. It reads the station, the verdict, the litres on the plate when clear, then the why line when not.
  • o-check__checks is role="list". Each m-check is role="listitem".
  • Colour is never alone. The verdict is a word and a shape. Each row carries a tick, a cross or a dash beside its sentence.
  • Skipped rows lose their claim in text, not only in grey. A screen reader hears the short name plus "Not checked yet" rather than a sentence about a question the app never asked.
  • The primary is never disabled. It keeps aria-disabled="false" and changes its label, so it stays in the focus order and still answers.
  • o-check__scroll and o-check__action both take z-index: var(--z-content). The refusal wash is an ::after, which is the last child, so without it the red painted over the back button and the verdict.
  • Reduced motion drops the arriving translate and sets the row stagger to zero, so all five answers land at once.
  • Back and "Choose another station" both run backToMap, which stands home up again underneath, so the next Back has somewhere to land.

Fuel code (QR)

The square an attendant scans, with everything else on the screen kept quieter than it.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4914 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt and reachable, show-QR-only per the locked owner call. Styles inline, not migrated

Purpose and User Goal

One job: hold a machine-readable square up to a till. The driver's goal is to be served without explaining anything. Show-QR-only is a locked owner decision, so there is no scan-the-pump path here.

The screen holds itself awake while it is the thing on screen. holdScreenAwake at line 8167 takes a Wake Lock, refusal is caught and ignored. The lock is released by hideFuel and by hideAll, which is the one place nothing can get past.

Closing is not cancelling. The X hides the code, the fill stays open and home says so. Cancelling is a separate, quieter control that asks first, because voiding a live authorisation cannot be undone.

Template

t-screen (CSS at line 796). Absolute, inset: 0, display: none until data-state is set. The organism is a flex column: o-fuel__scroll takes the height and centres its content, while o-fuel__action sits outside it.

No preview is rendered here. The rules that style this screen live in the single inline <style> at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this page does not load. A preview would be unstyled markup pretending to be a screen. The square itself is drawn at runtime by qrArt(fuelRef()) at line 8215, so a static copy would be an empty box under a title.

hideAll hides o-tab-bar on the way in. setStatusTone(false) keeps the default status-bar ink. A second attribute, data-expired, is set on the organism when the clock reaches zero.

Component Composition

Organism .o-fuel (CSS 3063). Its own elements: __scroll, __bar, __title, __tick, __lead, __spacer, __action.

ClassLevelWhere it comes from
m-placeMoleculestatic, with __pin. The station the code is scoped to. CSS 3183
m-fuel-figuresMoleculestatic, three __cell pairs of __label and __value. CSS 3203
m-fallbackMoleculestatic, with __label and __value. The typed digits. CSS 3114
m-confirmMolecule#fuelConfirm, a dialog laid over the screen for the cancel. CSS 3806
a-qrAtomfilled at line 8215. CSS 3148
a-btn--primary · a-btn--outlineAtomthe confirm's Keep it; Hide code in the action bar. CSS 530
a-icon-btnAtomthe close X. CSS 1210
a-quiet-linkAtomCancel this request; Cancel it inside the dialog. CSS 3790

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. The close X, top right. The way out sits where a driver's thumb is not, because closing this by accident at a pump means asking for the QR again in front of somebody waiting for it.
  2. The h1, with o-fuel__tick riding inside it rather than as a 64px mark above it. A big mark plus the word "Approved" says one thing twice.
  3. o-fuel__lead: "Show this code to the attendant."
  4. a-qr. Pure black on pure white with its own quiet zone, never tinted with the brand. It keeps its white card in dark mode. The thing being read is not the thing being themed.
  5. m-fallback, the number for a till that cannot read the square. Mono, grouped, spaced for dictation across a counter. Quiet, because reaching for it means the fast path has already failed.
  6. m-place, the station, directly under the code, because that is what the authorisation is scoped to.
  7. o-fuel__spacer, flexible, so the code sits high where a held-out phone is read and the detail sits low where it is looked up.
  8. m-fuel-figures: Vehicle, Up to, Expires in. Three figures taken in at once, because somebody at a pump is checking the screen rather than reading it.
  9. o-fuel__action, outside the scroll: outline "Hide code" over the quiet "Cancel this request".
  10. #fuelConfirm over all of it when cancel is pressed, quoting the plate so nobody voids the code they are holding while looking at another.

The plate is the only vehicle detail on this screen. Make and model are how a driver picks a van out of a yard. This screen is read standing beside one, where the plate is the only part written on the vehicle itself.

"Hide code" goes forward rather than back, by the owner's call: what follows showing the code is the fill, so it opens the watched fill. The X remains the way home for a driver pocketing the phone.

States

Two statesthe live code and the dead one
State idWhat it shows
fuel-approvedThe live code. Tick in the title, full-contrast square, fallback digits, then the expiry counting down from 29:41, which is the half hour a forecourt approval is good for. This is the one rail state that runs rather than freezes: land on it and the attendant scans, the litres count, the notification drops and the pay-off plays.
fuel-code-expiredS.fuelExpiryLeft is zero, so paintFuelExpired sets data-expired="true". The tick is removed, the square drops to 0.25 opacity and grayscale, the fallback digits are hidden entirely, the title becomes "Code expired", the lead becomes "This fuel code has expired. Request a new one to fuel." and the outline button becomes "Request a new code".

The expired face is a repaint of the same organism, not a second screen. Pressing the renewed button runs showFuel again down the same issue path a fresh approval takes.

Responsive Behaviour

  • The square is sized by the height it is given, not by a cap per phone width: flex: 0 1 auto, height: 260px, aspect-ratio: 1. It asks for 260, shrinks when the screen cannot spare it, then stays square on the way down.
  • One width rule remains: data-width="320" tightens the scroller's gap to --sp-3. The number under the square costs height. The narrowest phone has the least of it.
  • o-fuel__spacer is flex: 1 1 var(--sp-6) with a --sp-4 floor, so a tall screen does not strand the detail in the middle of nothing and a short one runs the two blocks together.
  • The three figure cells size to their content and are pushed apart rather than given a third each. A plate is twice the width of a time, so equal columns would break the plate over two lines to leave white space beside the clock.
  • Each cell states its own alignment, left, centre then right, because the screen is centred and an inherited cell would put its label in the middle of a figure that starts at the edge.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="fuelTitle". Focus lands on that h1 at 60ms, with tabindex="-1" and its ring suppressed.
  • The announcement fires at 500ms, behind the focus move so it is not flushed: it names the plate, the station and what to do with the screen. It does not spell out digits, because there are none on the square to spell.
  • a-qr is role="img" with an aria-label written per fill, naming the vehicle and the station.
  • The printed fallback number is aria-hidden. A visually hidden twin, #fuelRefSpoken, carries it as a sentence so a reader hears it once, in words.
  • The figures read as label then value in the markup. The cell reverses them visually. A screen reader says "Vehicle, CA 123-456" while an eye lands on the plate.
  • #fuelConfirm is role="dialog" aria-modal="true", labelled by its own title, which takes focus. Escape presses "Keep it", never "Cancel it": Escape must not be able to void a code.
  • a-quiet-link holds the 48dp floor and the secondary ink. It was 31px tall and measured 1.00:1 against this screen before, which is white on white on the one control that stops a fill.
  • The wake lock is best effort and is retaken on visibilitychange only while the code is still the screen.
  • Reduced motion drops the arriving translate.

Filling (live pump)

The one screen in the app that is alive while nothing is asked of the driver.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4379 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt and reachable. No molecule layer. Styles inline, not migrated

Purpose and User Goal

Tapping the Filling bar on home opens this. A driver stands at a pump for a few minutes with nothing to tap, so this is the one screen allowed to be looked at rather than used. The whole of the file's animation budget is spent here. Every other screen stays flat on purpose.

It answers three things at once: how much fuel has gone in, whether it is safe to walk away, what to do when the station or the signal fails. When the pump finishes while the driver is watching, the screen says so and offers the points, with no notification detour for somebody already here.

The light costs nothing to run. --fill-level is a number the JS was already writing on every tick to move the water line. The wash reads the same value, so there is no timer, no loop and nothing second to keep in sync. It moves only when the fuel does.

Template

t-screen (CSS at line 796). Absolute, inset: 0, display: none until data-state="visible". The organism is a centred flex column with the stage taking the middle by margin-block: auto.

No preview is rendered here. Every rule that styles this screen lives in the single inline <style> at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this page does not load, so a preview would be unstyled markup pretending to be a screen. The picture is driven by --fill-level, written by paintFilling at line 8019, so a static copy would show an empty vessel under a stalled number.

hideAll hides o-tab-bar on the way in and removes data-face from the organism at line 5403, so a state picked from the rail cannot inherit the previous state's frame. setStatusTone(false) keeps the default status-bar ink.

Component Composition

Organism .o-filling (CSS 3528). Its own elements: __bar, __hideWrap, __hideNote, __stage, __spin, __vessel, __liquid, __wave, __wave--back, __mark, __litres, __unit, __verdict, __facts, __note, __action.

No molecules. This is the only screen in the file with an empty molecule layer: everything here is either organism-level or an atom.

ClassLevelWhere it comes from
a-icon-btnAtomthe close X in o-filling__bar. CSS 1210
a-spinnerAtominside o-filling__spin, at stage size. CSS 657
a-btn--outlineAtomShow fuel code again, Message Engen support. CSS 530
a-btn--primaryAtomSee your points. CSS 530
a-quiet-linkAtomClose live view, at the bottom. CSS 3790

The refusal mark and the drop vessel are both drawn inline as SVG inside organism elements rather than promoted to atoms. The mark is the check screen's verdict seal at --size-stage, in the warning hue rather than the error red, because nothing has gone wrong with the driver's code or their litres.

Content and Action Hierarchy

The organism carries data-face, set by paintFilling to one of waiting, pump, running, done, refused or offline. A fault is a coat over the phase, not a phase of its own: the fill underneath stays wherever it was and the coat comes off when the fault does.

  1. The close X, top right, with o-filling__hideNote under it: "Fuelling will continue if you close this screen." That note speaks on the running face only.
  2. o-filling__stage, centred: the spinner while waiting, the drop vessel once litres flow, the warning mark on a refusal. Only one of the three is ever visible.
  3. o-filling__litres at --text-figure, with o-filling__unit beneath it. Once litres flow the counting number is the loader.
  4. o-filling__verdict, on the finished and refused faces only.
  5. o-filling__facts: plate, station, cap. On the waiting face it also carries "code valid until 10:11".
  6. o-filling__note, one line of what is happening and what is safe.
  7. o-filling__action: outline "Show fuel code again", quiet "Close live view", outline "Message Engen support", primary "See your points". Each is hidden or shown per face.

The X was a worded pill, changed at the owner's call. What keeps it safe now is not its own label. The note directly under it says fuelling continues. The same exit spells itself out in words at the bottom where a thumb reaches mid-fill. The accessible name is written out rather than left to a glyph.

On a refusal the two outline buttons are not equal. The code is the way this fill still happens and support is the fallback, so "Show fuel code again" takes data-lead="true". That is the atom's own state, so nothing here restyles a button.

States

Four statestwo prefixes, fuel- and fill-
State iddata-faceWhat it shows
fuel-watchingwaitingScanned nothing yet. The spinner runs, the vessel and the count are hidden, the unit reads "Waiting for the attendant", the note reads "The fuel code has not been scanned yet." and "Show fuel code again" is available. A zero would be the screen inventing a fill that has not started.
fuel-filling-openrunning31.4 litres of a 60 L cap. The vessel fills, both waves drift, the room lights up with the tank at --fill-level. Unit "litres so far", note "The pump is running.", the hide note visible under the X.
fill-refusedrefusedThe station could not start the fill. The vessel, the count and the unit all go, the warning mark draws itself, the verdict reads "The station could not start this fill." in the warning tone, the note reads "Your code and litres are safe." Show-code leads and "Message Engen support" appears.
fill-signal-lostofflineA fill at 31.4 litres with live updates paused. The number stays but takes data-stale="true", the unit becomes "litres at 9:41 · waiting for signal", the note reads "Live updates are paused. Fuelling will continue." A number that stopped moving is not the truth any more, so it says so.

Two further faces exist without a rail state of their own. pump is a code that was scanned before the station started the pump, and done is the finished fill. Both are reached while the journey is running, most directly from fuel-approved, which plays the whole back half rather than freezing.

Responsive Behaviour

  • No width rules of its own. Nothing on this screen is keyed to data-width.
  • The stage takes the middle by margin-block: auto, so a taller frame gives its extra height to the space around the vessel rather than to any block.
  • The vessel SVG is a fixed 108px box. The litre figure is --text-figure, which is 64px and steps to 68px at the larger type scale.
  • o-filling__note caps at 30ch, so the reassurance wraps to a readable measure on every frame.
  • o-filling__action stretches to the full width. The bottom padding adds env(safe-area-inset-bottom).
  • The two washes are declared on every face and lit on the live ones. At completion one fades down over the same beat the other fades up, so the room changes colour rather than going out and coming back on.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="fillingFacts", so its name is the plate, the station and the cap.
  • o-filling__litres is deliberately not a live region. A reader hearing five updates a second of one number is being shouted at. The arrival, the finish and the verdict all go through announce() instead.
  • Focus lands on the close button 60ms after arrival. Its accessible name is "Close live view", written out rather than left to a glyph.
  • The same exit is repeated as visible text at the bottom of the screen, where a thumb reaches mid-fill.
  • The spinner, the vessel and the refusal mark are all aria-hidden. Everything they encode is written in the unit line, the verdict or the note.
  • A stopped number says so in words as well as in style: data-stale is the paint, the unit line is the sentence.
  • Escape presses the close button, which stands home back up rather than leaving a blank frame.
  • Reduced motion stops the waves, the mark's stroke draw and the landing animations. Both the attribute form and the media query are covered.

Rewards

Where points become mobile data: the balance, what it is worth, the bundles it buys.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4345 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt and reachable, styles still inline

Purpose and User Goal

A driver arrives here with a number they have watched grow on home. This is the only room where that number becomes something. It answers three questions in the order a driver asks them: how many points do I have, what is that worth, what can I get now.

It is not a tab. It is opened from the balance tile on home, the data-tile="rewards" branch at line 8798, so it is a room a driver chooses to enter rather than one of the four they live in. showRewards (line 7734) never calls paintTabs, so the paintTabs(null) inside hideAll leaves the tab bar down for the whole visit. The single way out is the back chevron, which returns to home.

The spend cannot be undone, so nothing spends on a first press. Every road to a spend runs through askBundle (line 7824) and the confirm dialog first, including the retry after a failure.

Template

t-screen, defined at line 796. The template gives four declarations and no more: position: absolute, inset: 0, z-index: var(--z-overlay), display: none with flex-direction: column. Padding, background, scroll behaviour and the visible switch all belong to o-rewards, which is why .o-rewards[data-state="visible"] { display: flex; } sits at line 3415 with the organism rather than with the template.

No preview is rendered in this page. Every rule this screen uses lives in the single inline <style> block that starts at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this documentation does not load. Markup pasted in here would render unstyled, so a preview would show something the product does not contain. Read the screen in the prototype instead.

One dead branch is worth knowing about. In paintRewards the bare icon wrap is written under status === 'short', but short is a separate boolean while status only ever holds processing, sent, delivered or failed. The test can never be true, so a-icon-wrap--bare never renders on this screen.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. Back chevron, alone in the bar. Nothing competes with it.
  2. Rewards, the h1, which takes focus 60ms after the screen shows.
  3. The balance card. Label "Your points" in caps at --text-micro, the figure at --text-display, the worth line under it in --color-on-dark-mid. The figure is the destination of the whole journey, so it wears the hero light.
  4. "Use your points", the only instruction on the screen.
  5. The bundle rows, in ascending price: 500 MB at 500 pts, 1 GB at 1 000 pts, 2 GB at 2 000 pts. Each row is a real button. The right-hand cell is the action when it can be pressed, the status when it cannot.
  6. The earn note, last and quietest: how points are earned, plus the number the data goes to.
  7. The confirm dialog, over everything, only when a spend has been asked for.

Nothing on this screen counts up. The arrival animation lands the card first, then the rows, each delayed calc(90ms + var(--bundle-i) * 60ms). The comment at line 3504 gives the reason: the medal counts because points were just earned. Nothing was just earned here, so a balance that counted would be fiction.

States

State idGroupWhat it shows
rewardsreturningThe plain list. Returning driver, balance 2 450 pts, worth "up to 2.45 GB". All three bundles are affordable, so all three rows read can with a Get action. Defined at line 8941.
rewards-spentreturningThe ladder itself. sendBundle(0) and sendBundle(1) run through the one writer, then each is fast-forwarded a different distance: 500 MB is delivered, 1 GB is sent. Balance drops to 950 pts, so 2 GB falls to short and asks for 1 050 more. The toast is stripped, because news of a send is stale in a frozen frame. Defined at line 8946.
rewards-delivery-failedreturningsendBundle(0) then failRedemption(0). The 500 MB row wears the warning coat, its action reads "Try again", its note says the points are back in the balance. The points really are back: failRedemption subtracts from S.spentPts before it sets the status, so the balance still reads 2 450 pts. Defined at line 8961.

Three screen states, six row faces. The row faces are a separate axis, written by paintRewards onto m-bundle[data-state]. They are not entries in STATES.

Row faceRight-hand cellCoat
canm-bundle__go, "Get 1 GB"surface, hairline border, small card shadow
short"Needs 1 050 more"sunken fill, no shadow, plus a 120px m-bundle__track drawn to the balance as a percentage of the price
processing"Processing…"unchanged, secondary ink on the status
sent"Sent"unchanged, secondary ink on the status
delivered"Delivered ✓"success border, success background, the icon turns success
failedm-bundle__go, "Try again"warning border and background, with a note under the price

Sending has two real beats. armRedemption moves a row from processing to sent after 1 400ms, then to delivered after a further 2 000ms, replying with the delivery line. Both beats live in the tracked timers, so a frozen rail frame stays frozen. showRewards re-arms anything a screen change paused mid-journey.

Responsive Behaviour

The organism scrolls itself: overflow-y: auto with overscroll-behavior: contain, top padding calc(var(--size-status-bar) + var(--sp-3)), bottom padding calc(var(--sp-5) + env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px)). A mask image fades content out under the status bar rather than letting it slide behind the clock.

No [data-width] rule targets this screen, so 390, 375, 360 and 320 all get the same rules. Two fixed measurements survive at 320. One is the m-bundle__track at 120px, which sits inside a row that already wraps its text. The other is the m-confirm__panel at max-width: 320px, which the dialog's --sp-5 padding keeps off the edges. Every bundle row holds min-height: var(--size-control), 56px. It grows when its text needs two lines.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="rewardsTitle". The h1 carries tabindex="-1" and is focused 60ms after the screen shows, so the new room is announced. Its focus ring is suppressed at line 3276, because it is a heading rather than a control.
  • announce() writes the balance and its worth to the polite live region: "Rewards. 2 450 points. About 2.45 GB of mobile data."
  • The list is role="list" with role="listitem" on each row. The rows are real buttons, so Enter and Space both work without a tabindex.
  • Rows that cannot be pressed carry aria-disabled="true": short, processing, sent, delivered. They stay in the focus order, so a screen reader can reach one to hear why it is inactive. The failed row stays pressable, because trying again is allowed.
  • Every state a row carries is stated in words as well as colour. Under forced colours the only colour-only signal left is the pressable row, so line 3848 restates it with border-width: 2px.
  • The spend dialog is role="dialog" aria-modal="true", labelled by its own title. The title takes focus after 60ms. announce() reads the title with the body. Cancel returns focus to the row that opened the dialog.
  • The back button's accessible name is aria-label="Back to home". Its chevron is aria-hidden.
  • Reduced motion drops both arrival animations at lines 3514 to 3517. Nothing else on the screen moves.
  • One defect, measured. The Escape chain tests S.step === 'rewards' at line 8501 before it tests the dialog at line 8502. The dialog can only be open on this screen, so Escape with the dialog up leaves for home instead of cancelling the spend, which makes line 8502 unreachable. The fuel code screen nests its dialog check inside its own step branch instead, at lines 8504 to 8508, so Escape there presses Keep it.

Sign-up reward

The first payment. Fifty points counted onto a medal, then the meter that says what they are worth.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4128 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt and reachable, styles still inline

Purpose and User Goal

Sign-up is over. This screen pays for it. The driver's goal here is small: see that something arrived, understand what it is, carry on. The screen's goal is larger. It has to teach the whole economy in one frame, because this is the first time the driver sees points at all.

So it does two jobs that the fill's reward screen does not. It counts the score, then it converts it: fifty points is fifty megabytes of a three gigabyte ceiling, with 950 points to the first gigabyte. A bar with no target is decoration. The target is the part that brings a driver back.

It is also the file's fallback screen. Line 9844 sends any unknown hash here, so a reload on nothing lands on the reward rather than on an empty frame.

Template

t-screen, defined at line 796: position: absolute, inset: 0, z-index: var(--z-overlay), display: none with flex-direction: column. Everything else is the organism's. o-onboard at line 2594 owns the padding and the scroll, .o-onboard[data-state="visible"] { display: flex; } at 2599 owns the switch, o-onboard--reward at 2620 lays the hero gradient over it.

No preview is rendered in this page. Every rule this screen uses lives in the single inline <style> block that starts at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this documentation does not load. Markup pasted in here would draw a dark screen with no dark surface under it, so a preview would be a picture of something the product does not contain.

The organism is shared with earnedScreen, on purpose: same medal, same wave, same burst, same earn row. What separates the two is this screen's m-reward-goal. Nothing else here is unique to it.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. "Sign-up complete", the eyebrow, in caps at --text-micro. It names the moment before anything is claimed about it.
  2. The medal. The largest numeral in the file at --text-hero, closed up by 2px because no display setting goes far enough. Bolt above the figure, unit below, so the column reads symbol, amount, unit.
  3. You're signed up, the h1, at --text-title.
  4. The earn row: one act, one row. "Signing up" and "+50 pts". The pill never shrinks, so the label wraps before the value does.
  5. The goal card. "Data reward" and "50 MB of 3 GB" over the meter, the scale under it, then "950 points to your first 1 GB" behind a hairline.
  6. The flexible spacer, then Continue, alone. One action, at the thumb.

The order is deliberate: score, then what the score was for, then what the score buys. Every figure on the card is derived by paintGoal at line 5427 from rewardTotal(), S.ptsPerGb (1 000) and S.gbCap (3). Even the scale ticks are built rather than typed. The comment at 5430 records why: typed once into the markup they ran to 5 GB while every word on the card said 3, so the fill landed at the wrong tick.

States

State idGroupWhat it shows
signup-rewardafter-signupThe one state. reset('new-driver', true, ASSIGNED_ON_ARRIVAL), three vehicles waiting, then showReward(). Balance is the sign-up total alone, 50 points. Defined at line 8927, also the fallback the file boots to at line 9844.

One state, because there is only one thing that can have happened by the time a driver gets here. It plays rather than freezes: the counting is what the state exists to review, so showReward does not call clearTimers after itself the way the frozen states do.

BeatAtWhat moves
Reset0msplayReward (5496) zeroes a-medal__value, strips data-state="landed" off the earn row, snaps the meter to 0% with data-snap, paints the goal at zero points
Focus60msthe h1 takes focus, after the announcement is queued

Fifty points on a three thousand point track is three pixels, which reads as broken rather than as early. setMeterFloor holds the fill at a --sp-5 minimum whenever there is anything to show. Nothing is overstated by it, because the exact figure sits directly above the track.

Under reduced motion settleReward (5454) paints the finished frame with nothing moving, the medal takes data-motion="still" for a static glow, the confetti canvas is display: none. The one exception is Continue: the cross-fade to home survives, shortened to 160ms, because a cross-fade contains no motion while a hard cut is worse for the person the setting protects.

Responsive Behaviour

The organism scrolls itself. Padding is calc(var(--size-status-bar) + var(--sp-6)) at the top, --sp-5 at the sides, calc(var(--sp-6) + env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px)) at the bottom. The spacer is flex: 1 1, dropped to a --sp-5 basis on the reward variant, so Continue sits at the bottom of a 844 frame and is simply the next thing along in a 720 one.

No [data-width] rule targets this screen. Three things carry the small frames instead. m-reward-goal__head is flex-wrap: wrap, so the title and the value take two lines at 320 with large text. playConfetti sizes the canvas to min(medal × 3, frame width - 8), so the 320 frame still gets a whole burst rather than half of one off the edge. The meter's gradient is spanned by --meter-span, measured off the track by a ResizeObserver at line 5487, so switching device width re-sizes the spectrum instead of leaving a stale colour at the wrong point.

The medal itself is a fixed 156px square with flex-shrink: 0. It is the one measurement on the screen that does not respond.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="rewardTitle". The h1 carries tabindex="-1" and is focused at 60ms, so the new screen is announced. Its focus ring is suppressed at line 2787, because a ring on a heading makes it look pressable.
  • announce() writes "You are signed up. You earned 50 points." to the polite region at line 5550. The burst is delayed to 240ms so neither it nor the counting interrupts that.
  • The medal is role="img" with aria-label="50 points earned for signing up". The stepping digits sit inside a labelled image, so a screen reader hears the total once rather than every intermediate number.
  • The meter is a real role="progressbar": aria-valuemin="0", aria-valuemax set to 3 000 by paintGoal, aria-valuenow the points, aria-label="Data earned so far". Its aria-valuetext carries the sentence a percentage cannot: "50 of 3 000 points, 50 MB of 3 GB".
  • m-reward-goal__scale is aria-hidden. The valuetext already states the range, so the ticks would be a second reading of the same fact.
  • The orbs, the wave, the bolt and the confetti are all decorative and all hidden. Every SVG inside the earn row and the goal head is aria-hidden, because the words beside them already say it.
  • setStatusTone(true) puts the clock, the signal and the battery into the on-dark ramp for this hero. The screen states its own tone rather than inheriting whatever ran before it.
  • Continue is a real <button type="button"> at --size-control, 56px.
  • Escape does nothing here. The chain at lines 8497 to 8515 has no branch for S.step === 'signup-reward', which is correct: nothing sits behind this screen to go back to.

Points earned

The fill's pay-off, said in the sign-up reward's exact language: same medal, same wave, same burst.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4187 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt and reachable, styles still inline

Purpose and User Goal

The pump has stopped. The driver wants one thing: what did that fill pay me. The screen answers with a figure they watch being counted, then three flat facts under it. Nothing is asked of the driver here. Both actions lead onward.

It is deliberately the sign-up reward screen with different facts. The comment at line 4182 gives the reason: gamification that changes shape between screens stops reading as one system, so the second reward wears the first one's clothes. A driver who saw fifty points on day one recognises 524 on day nine without learning anything new.

It is also where the fill is banked. showEarned calls bankFill(), which is guarded by S.fillBanked so the balance moves once. The earned screen and the receipt can both run for the same fill, one opened by the notification and one by the live bar, so the guard is what stops a driver being paid twice.

Template

t-screen, defined at line 796: position: absolute, inset: 0, z-index: var(--z-overlay), display: none with flex-direction: column. Padding, scroll, background and the visible switch belong to o-onboard at 2594 with its --reward modifier at 2620. This screen shares those rules with rewardScreen byte for byte.

No preview is rendered in this page. Every rule this screen uses lives in the single inline <style> block that starts at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this documentation does not load. Markup pasted in here would render unstyled, without the hero the whole screen is drawn on, so a preview would misrepresent it.

What it does not have is the difference. There is no m-reward-goal here, so no a-meter. The conversion is stated in a plain row instead of drawn on a track, because the driver already learned the scale on the sign-up screen. In exchange it gains a second action, the quiet a-quiet-link.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. "Fill complete", the eyebrow.
  2. The medal, counting to 524. Same 156px graphic, same two rings, same amber bolt.
  3. 524 points earned, the h1, written by showEarned from fillPts(). The comment at 4205 records the copy decision: plain words for a second-language reader, subject first, one fact. "Pays you back" read as a promise or a threat depending on the reader's first language.
  4. Row one, the earning: "Fuel: 52.4 L" with a "+524 pts" pill. Litres and points both come from the fill, at ten points a litre.
  5. Row two, the worth: "Worth about 524 MB of mobile data". The same line home taught the driver to read, on the screen where the number just grew.
  6. Row three, the claim: "Your company pays for the fuel. The points belong to you." Two short sentences, no pill, because the fact needs no dressing.
  7. The spacer, then See receipt as the primary with Done quiet beneath it.

Both actions are exits. See receipt opens the slip. Done runs finishFill(), which files the slip, bumps the history badge and returns to home. The receipt is filed either way, so Done is the driver who does not need to read it now rather than the driver who is discarding it.

States

State idGroupWhat it shows
fuel-earnedfuellingThe one state. reset('returning-driver', true, 1), confirmByScan(readDisc()), station set to Bryanston, S.fuel = 'done', then showEarned(). A full 52.4 L fill paying 524 points. Defined at line 9239.

One state, because the screen has one shape. What changes between visits is data, not face: the litres, the points, the worth line and the medal's label are all rewritten from the fill. A stopped fill is not a second state either. It shortens the litres, which changes every derived figure. The only other thing it changes is the first word of the announcement, "Fill stopped early" instead of "Fill complete".

Like signup-reward, this state plays rather than freezes. The comment in its own definition says so: the medal counting and the burst are what the state exists to review, so it does not call clearTimers after itself.

One row, so one beat. The sign-up screen staggers its rows 260ms apart because it has a list to build. Here the medal jumps to its total in a single step rather than ticking through the digits.

Five doors lead here, which is why the screen never assumes what came before it: the notification tap at 8302, the home live bar at 8294, the home bell at 8417, the filling screen's "See your points" at 8397, the fuel tab when S.fuel === 'done' at 8794. Every one of them lands on the same frame, because showEarned rewrites the whole screen from the fill rather than from the route.

Responsive Behaviour

The organism scrolls itself, with calc(var(--size-status-bar) + var(--sp-6)) at the top, --sp-5 at the sides and calc(var(--sp-6) + env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px)) at the bottom. The spacer is flex: 1 1 on a --sp-5 basis, so the two buttons sit at the bottom of a 844 frame and simply follow the rows in a 720 one.

No [data-width] rule targets this screen. It carries more text than the sign-up reward does, so the rows do the work: m-earn-row__label is flex: 1 1 auto with min-width: 0 and the pill is flex-shrink: 0, so a long label wraps to a second line while the amount keeps its place. Row three has no pill at all, so its sentence takes the full width. Nothing truncates.

The medal stays a fixed 156px square. playConfetti caps the burst at the frame width less 8px, so 320 gets a whole burst rather than half of one.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="earnedTitle". The h1 carries tabindex="-1" and is focused at 60ms. Its focus ring is suppressed at line 2787, because it is a heading rather than a control.
  • announce() writes the outcome to the polite region before anything moves: "Fill complete. You earned 524 points." A partial fill opens with "Fill stopped early" instead.
  • The medal is role="img", relabelled by showEarned to "524 points earned for this fill". The counting digits sit inside the labelled image, so they are not read one by one.
  • Every icon on the screen is aria-hidden. The words beside them already carry the meaning.
  • The orbs, the wave, the bolt and the confetti canvas are decorative and hidden.
  • setStatusTone(true) puts the clock into the on-dark ramp for this hero, so the screen does not inherit a light bar from whatever ran before it.
  • Both actions are real buttons. See receipt is --size-control, 56px. Done is an a-quiet-link, which is text rather than a surface, correctly, because it is a change of mind rather than the way forward.
  • Escape presses See receipt (line 8497) instead of going back. The comment at 8494 gives the reason: there is nothing behind this screen to return to, because the fill has happened.
  • showEarned calls hidePush() on arrival, so the notification that may have opened the screen does not stay over it.

Runtime writers: showEarned at 7645, playEarned at 7668, playConfetti at 5266, bankFill at 7872, finishFill at 7892. The figures come from fillLitresFinal() at 6093 and fillPts() at 6094, both derived from FILL_LITRES (52.4, line 6082) at ten points a litre. Receipt

The slip for a finished fill: one fact per row, in the order a driver checks them against the pump.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4225 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt, inline CSS

Purpose and User Goal

A fill just ended. The driver wants three things: what the pump took, what it paid them in points, whether the record matches the number on the pump display. This screen is that record.

It also serves the person who is not holding the phone. Send to my manager opens the same prepared-message door every other escalation in the app uses, with the litres, the station, the plate, the points and the reference already written (line 8311).

The screen stays until Done. A receipt that walks away on a timer is not a receipt. Done does not destroy it either: finishFill at line 7892 files the slip into S.receipts, raises the History badge by one and returns the driver to Home.

Template

t-screen (line 796). It supplies five declarations only: absolute to the phone frame, inset: 0, z-index: var(--z-overlay), display: none and a column direction. Everything that makes this screen itself stays on o-receipt: the page ground, the padding, the success bloom and the [data-state="visible"] { display: flex } switch that reveals it.

No preview is rendered here, on purpose. Every rule this screen depends on lives in a single <style> block at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html. This documentation page does not load that file, so a pasted copy of the markup would render as unstyled text under a correct class list. That would be a lie about the screen rather than a picture of it. Read it in the prototype, at the fuel-receipt state.

Component Composition

LevelClassPart it plays
Templatet-screenthe shell, shared with twelve other screens
Organismo-receiptpage ground, the bloom, the arrival choreography
Elemento-receipt__whenthe timestamp under the title
Elemento-receipt__cardthe document itself, one card holding seven rows
Elemento-receipt__spacerflex: 1 1 auto, pushing the actions to the thumb
Elemento-receipt__actionthe two buttons plus the filed note
Elemento-receipt__filed"Saved in History."
Moleculem-slip-rowone fact: __label, __value, optional __sub
Atoma-screen-titlethe h1, shared with History, Account and Rewards
Atoma-btn a-btn--primaryDone
Atoma-btn a-btn--outlineSend to my manager

m-slip-row is a molecule rather than an element of this organism because History renders the same row inside o-history__card. It was o-receipt__row once, which put an element of one block inside another block's card. The receipt still owns the arrival of these rows. It does not own the rows.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. a-screen-title "Receipt", the h1.
  2. o-receipt__when, "Today · 9:41".
  3. The card, seven m-slip-row facts in fixed order: Station, Vehicle (plate over model), Fuel (litres over "of 60 L approved"), Points earned, New points balance, Kilometre reading, Reference.
  4. Primary Done. Outline Send to my manager. The quiet o-receipt__filed line under both.

The order is deliberate: where, what, how much, then what it paid. Money last, because the driver checks the physical facts against the pump first.

One row is allowed a colour. Points earned carries data-tone="success", a wash bleeding to the card edges through a negative inline margin that gives back exactly the --sp-4 its card holds. It is the row this app added to the paper slip, so it is the only row the screen is pleased about.

Three values are written with firstChild.textContent rather than textContent (lines 7694, 7697 and 7703). Their m-slip-row__sub note is a child of the same value node, so rewriting the whole node would take the note with it.

States

One state in the rail reaches this screen.

State idGroupWhat it shows
fuel-receipt fuelling Line 9248, labelled "The receipt". Seeds a returning driver with one vehicle, confirms it by scan, sets the station to Bryanston and S.fuel to done, then calls showReceipt() and clears the timers. The slip reads 52.4 L at Engen Bryanston, +524 pts, a 2 974 pts balance.

The screen has no variant faces of its own. One row does change shape at runtime. #receiptOdoNote stays hidden unless S.fillOdoLast is set, in which case the kilometre row gains a "last fill was …" sub-line. That note is the only trace the odometer warning leaves, which is the reason the warning is allowed not to block.

showReceipt() (line 7685) calls bankFill() (line 7872), which is guarded to be idempotent. The earned screen and the receipt can both run for the same fill, so the second one must not pay the driver twice.

showReceipt does not call paintTabs. hideAll already ran paintTabs(null), so the tab bar is down here. A fill is a flow, not a section.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. The screen fills t-device-frame, which is 390×844 by default with data-width variants at 375, 360 and 320 (CSS at lines 785 to 802). The card is fluid inside --sp-5 side padding, so it narrows with the frame. Nothing truncates. A long station name or model wraps and the card grows.

The demo page's own breakpoints at 900px and 440px move the rail and the frame. They do not reach inside the screen.

Vertical fit is handled by two rules working together. o-receipt__spacer takes the slack when the slip is short. overflow-y: auto with overscroll-behavior: contain takes over when it is not. Bottom padding adds env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px) so the buttons clear the home indicator.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="receiptTitle", so it is named by its own h1.
  • #receiptTitle carries tabindex="-1" and is focused 60ms after the screen opens, with preventScroll. Focus lands at the top of the new screen rather than staying on the button that left the old one.
  • The title's focus ring is suppressed (.a-screen-title:focus-visible, line 3276). It is a programmatic focus target rather than a control, so a ring there would read as an interactive element.
  • announce() writes to the aria-live="polite" region at line 4990: "Receipt. 52.4 litres at Engen Bryanston. 524 points earned." The three figures that matter are spoken without the reader walking the card.
  • Every figure uses font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums, so the values stack into a column a thumb can run down.
  • The success row is not colour alone. Its label reads "Points earned" and its value carries a + sign.
  • Escape or the device back gesture presses Done (line 8498). The screen has no dismissal that loses the slip, because Done files it.
  • Both buttons are real <button type="button"> elements at --size-control height, above the 48px --size-target floor.
  • Motion is a global concern here. prefers-reduced-motion and the demo's [data-motion="reduced"] attribute both collapse every animation to 0.001ms (lines 821 to 835), which includes the slip entrance and the points spring.

Every token above resolves in the :root block at the top of the same file. The type ramp is re-pointed by [data-text="large"] at line 447, so --text-caption becomes 15px and --text-callout 18px without a single rule in this screen changing.

The arrival is one sequence declared in one place. Title at 0s, timestamp at 0.06s, both on checkRise. The card slips in at 0.12s on slipIn. The seven rows fill in behind it from 0.20s to 0.50s, one step apart, with an eighth row simply landing with the seventh. The actions rise at 0.50s. The points figure lands last, on a spring at 0.55s. The slip reads as being written rather than as arriving whole.

Fuel history

The drawer the slips live in. Every filed receipt, newest first, in the shape the driver already learned to read.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4270 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt, inline CSS, body injected

Purpose and User Goal

Someone wants proof of a fill that already happened. Usually that someone is not the driver. It is the manager, a week later, asking what CA 214-887 took on Tuesday.

This screen answers without the driver reconstructing anything. Done on the receipt files the slip here rather than destroying it, so the drawer fills by using the app. A receipt that cannot be found again is not a receipt.

It is a tab destination, so it carries no task of its own. There is nothing to submit here. The one thing pressing a card would do is show a fact already on the card, so the cards are not pressable.

Template

t-screen (line 796): absolute to the frame, inset: 0, the overlay layer, hidden, a column. o-history adds the page ground, the padding, the brand bloom and the [data-state="visible"] { display: flex } switch.

Its bottom padding is calc(var(--sp-8) + var(--sp-8) + var(--sp-1)), which is 68px of clearance for the tab bar standing under it. That number is the one thing separating this organism's box from o-receipt's, which uses the safe-area inset instead because no bar stands there.

No preview is rendered here, on purpose. Two reasons compound. The styles live in a single <style> block at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this page does not load. And the body of this screen does not exist in the markup at all: it is written by showHistory at line 7954. A static copy would be an empty column under a correct class list. Read it in the prototype, at the fuel-history state.

The static markup is ten lines. Everything the driver reads is built by el.historyList.innerHTML = S.receipts.map(...) at line 7954, from filed facts rather than live state, using the same m-slip-row markup the receipt uses. That is why the row is a molecule: it outlives the block that first drew it.

m-screen-bar is a molecule for the same reason. o-history could not honestly chrome Account and Rewards, which need the same shelf.

Two pieces of app chrome sit outside this screen but belong to its reading. o-tab-bar at line 4605 is a sibling of the screens rather than a child, marked aria-current="page" on the history tab by paintTabs('history') at line 7949. The a-badge a-badge--onBar at line 4634 sits on that tab.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. m-screen-bar holding one a-icon-btn, labelled "Back to home".
  2. a-screen-title "Fuel history", the h1.
  3. o-history__list: for each receipt, an o-history__when date over an o-history__card.
  4. m-empty-note--history, shown only when the list is empty.
  5. The tab bar, standing under all of it.

Each filed card holds six m-slip-row facts: Station, Vehicle (plate over model), Fuel, Points earned, Kilometre reading, Reference. The receipt holds seven. The row History drops is New points balance, which is a live figure rather than a fact about the fill. A balance printed a week ago would be wrong by now.

The kilometre row keeps its argued-with note. If the filed receipt carried odoLast, the card renders "last fill was …" beneath the reading, exactly as the receipt did.

The filed card is the receipt's card, smaller. Same surface, same border, same --radius-card, but --shadow-card-sm instead of --shadow-card and --sp-1 block padding instead of --sp-2. The document was the only object on the receipt. Here it is one of many, so it takes less lift.

There is one action on the screen and it goes backward. Opening the drawer is itself the second action: showHistory sets S.newSlips = 0 and repaints the badge, so unread becomes read by being looked at.

States

One state in the rail reaches this screen.

State idGroupWhat it shows
fuel-history fuelling Line 9254, labelled "History, the filed receipt". Seeds a returning driver, confirms the vehicle by scan, banks FILL_PTS, then puts today's fill at the head of S.receipts and concatenates the three PAST_RECEIPTS from line 6102. Four cards render: Today · 9:41 at Bryanston, Tuesday · 15:42 at Sandton City, Monday · 07:18 at Bryanston, Friday · 16:55 at Woodmead. The state exists to show the drawer doing its job, so it files the fresh slip the same way finishFill files it.

The screen has two faces the rail does not separately name, both decided by one line at 7952: el.historyEmpty.hidden = S.receipts.length > 0. A returning driver is seeded with the three past receipts (line 8863), so the list is populated. A new driver starts with S.receipts = [] and gets the empty note instead. There is no third face: the list cannot fail to load, because it is not fetched.

The badge is a third variable, carried by the tab rather than the screen. paintHistoryBadge at line 7880 hides it at zero, prints the count otherwise, then rewrites the tab's aria-label to "History, N new". Seeded history is old news by definition, so reset sets S.newSlips = 0. The count is real or it is absent.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. The screen fills t-device-frame at 390×844, 375×812, 360×800 or 320×720 (lines 785 to 802). Cards are fluid inside --sp-5 side padding. Nothing truncates: a long station name wraps and the card grows.

The list is the scroll surface. overflow-y: auto with overscroll-behavior: contain keeps a flick inside the phone rather than dragging the page behind it. A top mask (line 3740) dissolves scrolled slips before they reach the clock, so the status bar never has type running under it.

The entrance is length-aware. Cards arrive in pairs at 0.10s, 0.18s, 0.26s, with everything past the sixth landing together at 0.34s. A driver scrolling to their ninth receipt is reading rather than watching an entrance.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="historyTitle".
  • #historyTitle carries tabindex="-1" and takes focus 60ms after the screen opens, with preventScroll. Its focus ring is suppressed, since it is a landing target rather than a control.
  • announce() reports the count, correctly singular: "History. 1 receipt." or "History. 4 receipts." or "History. No fills yet." The reader learns whether the drawer has anything in it before walking it.
  • The back button has aria-label="Back to home", because its only content is an aria-hidden chevron. It is --size-target square, 48px.
  • Escape or the device back gesture presses the same button (line 8499). There is one way out and it works from both.
  • The badge is aria-hidden on purpose. Its count would be read twice otherwise, because paintHistoryBadge puts the same number into the tab's own aria-label.
  • Injected rows carry no interactive elements. The list is a document, so it needs no roles beyond the ones the h1 gives it.
  • Values use font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums, so litres, points and readings line up as columns.
  • Under forced-colors: active the active tab loses its colour but keeps its weight, with an underline restating which tab it is (line 3840).
  • prefers-reduced-motion and [data-motion="reduced"] both collapse the staggered entrance to 0.001ms.

The success wash uses --sp-4 as a negative inline margin, which is correct only because both cards that hold the row pad by --sp-4 on the inline axis. A future card padding differently would have to say so in the row's rule.

Data comes from two places in the same file. PAST_RECEIPTS at line 6102 seeds the returning driver. finishFill at line 7892 unshifts a fresh slip onto the front of the same array. Both write the identical shape, which is what lets one renderer draw them all.

Account

The driver's own facts and the one setting that is theirs to change.

LevelScreen Sourceengenxt-onboarding.html:4285 Templatet-screen StatusBuilt, inline CSS

Purpose and User Goal

This screen exists because the app promises it twice. Sign-up says "You can turn it on later in Account". The tab bar shows an Account tab. Both pointed at a locked door. A promise a curious colleague can disprove in two taps is worse than no promise.

The driver comes here for one of three reasons: to turn Face ID sign-in on or off, to check which number the company registered against them, or to sign out. Nothing else on the screen is editable, because nothing else belongs to the driver. Vehicles are assigned by a manager. The mobile number was registered by the company.

That is the screen's real subject. It draws a line between what the driver owns (one switch) and what their employer owns (everything else), then says so in plain words rather than by greying a field out.

Template

t-screen (line 796): absolute to the frame, inset: 0, the overlay layer, hidden, a column. o-account adds the page ground, the padding and the [data-state="visible"] { display: flex } switch.

Its box matches History's exactly, including the 68px of bottom clearance for the tab bar (--sp-8 twice plus --sp-1). It is the third member of the filed-screen family that shares the top scroll mask at line 3740. Unlike the other two it has no ::before bloom. Only o-receipt and o-history carry one, in success and brand hues respectively. A settings list is neither good news nor a drawer of slips, so it stands on plain page grey.

No preview is rendered here, on purpose. The rules for o-account, a-switch and m-screen-bar live in a single <style> block at line 8 of engenxt-onboarding.html, which this documentation page does not load. A pasted copy would show unstyled rows and a switch drawn as a bare button. Read it in the prototype, at the account state.

o-tab-bar at line 4605 is a sibling of the screens rather than a child of this one. paintTabs('account') at line 7926 raises it and marks the account tab aria-current="page".

a-switch is drawn from two tokens that waited years for a switch to exist. --color-switch-track is a grey chosen to clear 3:1 against white. --shadow-knob lifts the knob off it. This is the only place either is used.

Content and Action Hierarchy

  1. m-screen-bar holding one a-icon-btn, labelled "Back to home".
  2. a-screen-title "Account", the h1.
  3. Face ID sign-in, with the a-switch on the right. The __rowSub is written by paintBioSetting (line 7935) rather than fixed in the markup.
  4. Mobile number, value +27 82 ··· 567, sub "Your company registered this number. Ask your manager to change it."
  5. Vehicles assigned by your company, value written from S.vehicles.length at line 7928, sub "Linked one at a time, by scanning."
  6. o-account__out with the outline Sign out, pushed to the bottom.
  7. The tab bar, under all of it.

The order is deliberate. The only actionable row comes first. The two facts follow. The destructive action sits alone at the bottom, in outline rather than primary, separated by margin-top: auto so it cannot be hit while reaching for a row.

Every row states its own authority in the sub-line. That is the pattern to keep: a read-only value is explained by who owns it rather than by looking disabled.

Sign out is real. It sets window.location.href to index.html#home/default (line 8406), landing on the sign-in flow's front door, which is what signing out of the finished app would do.

States

One state in the rail reaches this screen.

State idGroupWhat it shows
account returning Line 8970, labelled "Account". Resets to a returning driver with three vehicles assigned, calls showAccount() then clears the timers. Face ID reads On, the number reads +27 82 ··· 567, the vehicle count reads 3.

The switch has two faces the rail does not separately name, both driven by S.bioOn through paintBioSetting:

ValueSwitchSub-line
S.bioOn === truearia-checked="true", track goes --color-primary, knob translates 20px"On. Face ID is checked securely by your phone."
S.bioOn === falsearia-checked="false", track stays --color-switch-track"Off. We send a code to sign you in."

The class never changes. aria-checked is the state, which is why the same attribute the reader announces is the one CSS styles.

Toggling flips S.bioOn, repaints the row, then fires a toast: "Face or fingerprint is on." or "Face or fingerprint is off. We will send a code." (line 8398). The change is seen as well as heard.

The demo driver arrives with the switch on. The sign-up flow lives in index.html, a separate file with no build step between them, so its enrolment answer cannot be read from here. On is the value the sign-up happy path produces. The -nobio route aliases (line 9293) set S.bioOn = false after the state runs, which is the one channel the two files share.

The vehicle count is not a state either. It is S.vehicles.length || 0, so an unassigned driver reads 0 rather than an empty cell.

Responsive Behaviour

None of its own. The screen fills t-device-frame at 390×844, 375×812, 360×800 or 320×720 (lines 785 to 802). The demo page's 900px and 440px breakpoints move the rail and the frame, never the screen inside it.

Each row is a flex pair. o-account__rowBody is flex: 1 1 auto with min-width: 0, so at 320px the title and sub-line wrap while the right-hand side holds. o-account__value is white-space: nowrap by deliberate choice: a masked number split over two lines is two fragments to a driver checking it against their SIM. a-switch is flex-shrink: 0, so it never squashes.

Rows have min-height: var(--size-target), 48px. A row whose text wraps grows past that. A short row cannot fall below it.

overflow-y: auto with overscroll-behavior: contain handles a frame too short for the list, with the top mask dissolving scrolled rows before they reach the clock.

Accessibility

  • The section is aria-labelledby="accountTitle".
  • #accountTitle carries tabindex="-1" and takes focus 60ms after the screen opens, with preventScroll. Its focus ring is suppressed, since it is a landing target rather than a control.
  • announce() writes "Account." to the polite live region. Short on purpose: the rows are the content, so the announcement names the room only.
  • The switch is a real <button type="button" role="switch"> with aria-checked and an explicit aria-label="Face ID sign-in". Enter and Space both fire it.
  • Its drawn size is 52×32, below the 48px floor. a-switch::before with inset: -8px -4px extends the hit area to 60×48 without changing the drawing. Drawn at the platform's switch size, pressable at the rule's.
  • The sub-line under the switch is not aria-describedby-linked. A reader hears the label plus the checked state, then reaches the sub-line as the next node. It restates the state in words for a sighted driver rather than duplicating it for a listening one.
  • The back button has aria-label="Back to home", since its only content is an aria-hidden chevron. It is 48px square.
  • Escape or the device back gesture presses the same button (line 8500).
  • The masked number is written with middle dots rather than asterisks. It is a display string, so it is read as one value rather than as punctuation to be spelled out.
  • Under forced-colors: active the active tab keeps its weight with an underline restating which one it is (line 3840).
  • Values use font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums.
