Values resolve in the :root block at the top of the same file: --size-control-sm 32px (line 370), --size-target 48px (line 328), --color-switch-track #8A91A3 (line 167), --shadow-knob (line 289). [data-text="large"] at line 447 re-points the ramp, so --text-callout becomes 18px here with no rule in this screen changing.

Two routes reach the screen. The account tab dispatches at line 8786. The home avatar #homeAvatar (line 4445) dispatches at line 8782. Both call the same showAccount() at line 7922, so both arrive with the tab bar raised and the account tab marked.

One stale attribute to check. #homeAvatar at line 4445 still carries data-unbuilt="Your account" from the period when this screen did not exist. Its handler now opens the real screen. The attribute is a leftover rather than a behaviour.

Accessibility and Responsive Quality

  • Keyboard. Every control is a real <button> or input. Disabled primary actions use aria-disabled, so they hold their place in the focus order and can still say why they are unavailable.
  • Visible focus. One ring for the whole product. It is two-tone, so it reads on the near-black hero and on the light sheet.
  • Labels. Persistent and visible. No placeholder-as-label anywhere.
  • Errors. Placed against the control they belong to. Colour never carries an error on its own: a field in error gains a glyph in its helper and shakes once.
  • Forced colours. @media (forced-colors: active) throws away every background and border colour. That is every cue the OTP group and the field have, so both re-express their state structurally: a solid outline for valid, a dashed one for error. The build gate fails any page missing that block, so nobody has to remember it.
  • Landmarks. Each screen is a <section> labelled by its own heading; the device chrome is aria-hidden.
  • Reduced motion. Honoured through the media query and through an explicit switch, so you can demo it without touching an OS setting.
  • Contrast. Sampled from rendered pixels on all 43 states, not reasoned about from tokens. Gradients and glass make the arithmetic unreliable.
  • Targets. A 48px floor, measured with pseudo-element extensions included. The drawn size and the pressable size are allowed to differ, so only the pressable one counts.

Known limitations

  • Screen-reader behaviour is reasoned about and coded for, but nothing has been run against a real screen reader.
  • The status-bar contrast check samples one point per state. It would miss a failure elsewhere in the bar.

Responsive behaviour

The product is a phone app. The design target is a phone, so there is no desktop layout to shrink from. What varies is the device width (390, 375, 360 and 320) and whether the keyboard is up.

The compression order

When the keyboard takes half the screen, space is given up in a stated order, starting with what the driver has already finished with: the tooling first, then the step counter, then instruction that has already been acted on, then the loose gaps. The base gap between related things goes last. It is what says which things belong together. That matters most when there is least room.

Content reflows by priority. Nothing is hidden merely to make it fit. No page scrolls sideways.

Content resilience and states

Only states that exist in the screens are listed. Nothing here is invented.

StateHow it is expressedWhere to see it
Defaultnone#number/empty
Focus:focus-visible. A 3px ring plus a white halo, two-tone so one ring works on the dark hero and on the light sheetTab through any screen
Hover--color-hover-wash or --color-hover-lift, by what the control stands onPointer devices only
Active--press-firm on controls, --press-subtle on rowsAny button
Disabledaria-disabled plus a flattened coat; stays focusable#number/empty
Loadinga-spinner inside the control that caused the wait#number/sending
Errordata-state="error" on the field, a-helper[data-tone="error"] beneath it#number/err-server
Successdata-tone="success"#code/success
EmptyA stated sentence, never a blank region#number/err-unregistered
Offlinedata-net="offline" on the root#home/offline
Long contentSheets scroll; the status bar clearance and edge masks holdLarge text mode
Reduced motionBoth the media query and a data-motion switch; animations collapse to a still frame, never to nothingRail toggle

Contribution and Governance

How to add something without breaking what is here. And what stops you if you do.

Choosing an Atomic Level

The level decides the file, the prefix and what the thing is allowed to know. Work down the list and stop at the first that fits.

AskIf yesGoes in
Is it a value, not a thing?Tokentokens.css
Does it apply to a bare element, with no class?Primitivefoundations.css
Would splitting it leave nothing that means anything?Atomatoms.css
Is it a small group of atoms doing one job?Moleculemolecules.css
Is it a distinct section, or a whole screen?Organismorganisms.css
Is it arrangement, holding no content of its own?Templatetemplates.css
Is it one job with no opinion, used anywhere?Utilityutilities.css
Is it demo tooling that must never reach a driver?Developerdeveloper.css

The filing law

A rule is filed by the block that owns it (the leftmost in its selector), not by what it targets. .o-auth-hero__actions .a-btn is the hero's rule about a button it contains, so it belongs with the hero. Filing it under atoms would put a screen's override in front of the screen.

There is one live exception. It is not a good one: three .o-signup-sheet__resend rules sit in atoms.css, pinned there when the stylesheet was split so the rendering would not change. See Known limitations.

Adding a Token

  1. It goes in the :root block of src/css/tokens.css.
  2. Its name must open with a sanctioned family. The gate keeps the list and fails the build for anything outside it: --color- --glass- --gradient- --kb- --font- --text- --weight- --leading- --tracking- --radius- --shadow- --sp- --size- --press- --z- --ease- --dur- --d-.
  3. Name it for what it is for, not for what it is.
  4. Add it to engenxt-onboarding.html as well. That page still carries an inline copy of the scale. The gate compares the two property by property, so a token in one file and not the other fails the build.
  5. If it is a type size, decide whether it also needs a value under [data-text="large"]. Fifteen tokens do.

How a token is named

A token opens with the family it belongs to: --color-, --text-, --sp-, --radius-, --dur- and the rest. The family is enforced. The gate keeps a list and a name outside it fails the build, so nobody can quietly start a --brand-blue-2 alongside the scale.

After the family, the name says what the value is for, not what it is. --color-text-secondary, not --color-grey-60. That is the rule that lets a value change without a rename. It is followed almost everywhere; see how many tiers there are for the count. --d- marks the developer panel, which never ships.

Token names are kebab-case, with one exception: --color-hover-onDark. That is not a slip. onDark is a single concept throughout this codebase (data-tone="onDark", .a-btn--outlineOnDark) and it is spelled the same way in all three places. The cost is that you cannot assume a token is kebab-case and autocomplete your way to it. It is the only one.

Adding a Component

  1. Pick the level from the table above; the prefix follows from it.
  2. Put it in that level's file, filed under its own leftmost block.
  3. Take --text-* for size rather than raw pixels, so it answers large text.
  4. Runtime state is a data-* attribute, never a class.
  5. If any state is signalled by colour alone, add a @media (forced-colors: active) branch restating it structurally. The gate requires that block to exist.
  6. Never remove the focus ring without drawing an equivalent on the component's own edge.
  7. Document it here. The gate fails if a block in src/css is never mentioned on this page.

Naming

Every class states its level in its first two characters. The build gate enforces that, not convention.

PrefixLevelExample
a-Atoma-btn
m-Moleculem-sheet-header
o-Organismo-signup-sheet
t-Templatet-device-frame
p-Pagep-signup-demo
u-Utilityu-visually-hidden
d-Developer tooling, never shipsd-panel

Blocks are kebab-case; multi-word element and modifier tails are camelCase (__balanceCard, --outlineOnDark). Elements never nest (block__a__b is refused).

State is never a class

Runtime state rides a data-* attribute: data-state, data-tone, data-verdict. There is no is-open anywhere. The gate fails the build if one appears. A class says what a thing is; an attribute says what it is doing.

Required States and Accessibility

A component is not finished when it looks right. These are the states it has to answer for, plus the ones it may honestly skip.

StateRequired whenHow it is carried here
Focus visiblealways, for anything focusablethe system ring from foundations. Never removed without drawing an equivalent on the component's own edge
Disabledthe control can be refusedaria-disabled for a primary action, so it stays in the focus order and can explain itself. The real attribute where the thing is content rather than a control
Errorthe control can be wrongdata-state="error" or data-tone="error", plus a non-colour cue. Colour alone fails
Loadingthe action takes timecomposition: a spinner beside the label. Not a variant
Hoveronly where a pointer is plausiblemost filled controls have none, because a thumb does not hover

Two accessibility rules are absolute. Every interactive thing clears --size-target, counting a pseudo-element hit area. Any state signalled by colour must restate itself structurally under @media (forced-colors: active). The build gate fails a page without that block.

Documentation Requirements

A public component is incomplete until it is documented. Not as a courtesy: the build gate fails if a block in src/css has no item of its own on this page. It fails again if that item is missing any of its subsections.

An item is complete when it carries all of these:

  • a rendered example drawn by the production CSS, never recreated in docs.css;
  • metadata: level, ownership, source file, status;
  • every implemented variant, rendered;
  • every state the CSS actually implements, plus none it does not;
  • the tokens it consumes;
  • its accessibility contract;
  • copyable markup that matches what the product ships.

Do not pad. If a component has no variants, write "None." A subsection filled with filler is worse than a subsection that says there is nothing to say, because the next reader cannot tell which one they are looking at.

Verification Gates

Four checks, cheapest first. Set CHROME=/path/to/chrome if Chrome is not at the macOS default.

CommandTakesChecksIn CI
node tools/check.jsUnder a second Scripts parse, ids exist, state rails agree, tokens identical across both files, class shapes, layer nesting and that this page agrees with the code Yes
node tools/journey.js≈2 minutes Walks the flows with real events and the real clock Yes
node tools/sweep.jsA few minutes Renders all 104 states and measures contrast off real pixels, tap targets against --size-target, nothing stranded, nothing thrown Yes
node tools/sweep.js --largeA few minutes The same 104 states with large text on No. Run it yourself before merging anything that touches type or spacing

.github/workflows/check.yml runs the first three on every push to main and on every pull request. They run in two jobs, so a parse failure reads as itself rather than as a dead browser. Those three block a merge. The large-text sweep is discipline, not enforcement.

The repo deploys straight from main to GitHub Pages, so main being green is the only gate that means anything. There is no install step and no package.json. The checks have no dependencies. Adding one so the workflow looked conventional would hand this prototype the toolchain it has done without for its whole life.

Deprecation

Nothing is deprecated today, so this is the rule rather than a list.

  1. Mark the item's status Deprecated in its metadata and say what replaces it.
  2. Leave the CSS in place. Removing it and updating the callers in one change makes a rendering regression indistinguishable from a refactor.
  3. Move the callers, one at a time, checking the computed-style fingerprint each time.
  4. Remove the rules only when the gate reports the class is styled but never used. That gate already exists: it fails on a dead name in either direction.

A token is retired the same way, with one extra step: it must come out of both files at once, because the gate compares the two scales property by property.

Ownership and Review

Anything under Known limitations and unresolved decisions is waiting on the design owner, not on a developer with a spare afternoon. Those entries are there because the code has two defensible answers and choosing one moves pixels. Raise them; do not settle them inside a refactor.

No-Build Constraints

There is no package manager, no bundler and no build step. That is a requirement rather than an accident. Both pages open by double-clicking them. The deployed site is those same files served statically. A reviewer with no toolchain can open the product.

What that rules out:

  • no npm dependency, in the product or in this documentation;
  • no preprocessor. nesting, mixins and variables come from CSS itself;
  • no module scripts, because they do not load over file://;
  • no absolute paths, because GitHub Pages serves this from a subdirectory;
  • no external font, image or script host. Every glyph is inline markup.

This constraint ends when the React rewrite begins. It exists to keep a prototype reviewable without a toolchain. A React project has a build by definition, so the rule to carry over is not "no build" but what it protected: no dependency the team cannot explain, plus nothing that stops a designer opening the thing and looking at it. See Building This in React.

Known Limitations and Proposed Improvements

Approved and done

  • The resend button is organism-owned. It is now filed that way. Four rules style .o-signup-sheet__resend. Three of them sat in atoms.css, pinned there when the stylesheet was split. They now sit in organisms.css with the fourth. Visual correctness outranks a filing taxonomy, so the move was made and then measured. Every element on all 104 states is computed-identical before and after. The move stands. No cascade exception was needed. The countdown reads 500 while it waits and 600 once Resend is live. That is what the stylesheet says now, not what source order happened to produce.
  • Linked CSS, no build. Eight layer files loaded by plain <link>. Verified to render identically over file:// and http.
  • Cascade layers. Order declared once. Every rule is filed by its owning block, not its target, so a screen's override stays with the screen.
  • Gates keep their strictness. The three CLI gates learned to find linked CSS. Not one assertion was relaxed.

Implementation assumptions

  • A rule is filed by the leftmost block in its selector. .o-auth-hero__actions .a-btn is the hero's rule about a button it contains, so it lives with the hero.
  • An at-rule is filed by what it contains, so a media override lands beside the rules it overrides rather than in front of them.

Inconsistencies that need a design ruling

  • The semantic spacing layer. Approved and built. Seven aliases, each mapped one-to-one onto the reference step it already rendered, so no pixel moved. Adoption is the open part. Most --sp-* uses were left where they are, because picking which of two near-identical values is intended takes a design review. See Foundations.
  • Same-level nesting. Three components hold another of their own level: a-icon-btn holds a-badge, m-app-bar holds m-brand-lockup, m-alert holds m-privacy. Strictly, each pair is one level up. The build deliberately doesn't fail on these. A gate that fails on judgement calls teaches people to reach for the exception list.

Known limits of this page

  • Opened straight off disk, a browser won't let this page read the authored form of a token. It sees only the resolved value. Serve the folder over http for the full table.
  • Organisms are described here rather than rendered. They are full screens and need the device frame to make sense.
  • Only index.html is migrated. Until the app follows, its components stay documented in its own in-page spec panel.

Source: src/css/*.css · Screens: index.html · Gates: tools/check.js, tools/sweep.js, tools/journey.js and the in-page Self-check on the rail's Design notes tab.
